- **D-4660** — `the-restore-marker-refusal-sits-beside-the-opener` (Task 8; held-out review 518 F7; coordinator mail 5512): Task 8 Step 7 requires that "startup refuses active marker" and names no file for that check. The File Map's ownership note for Task 7's files (`schema.ts`, `store.ts`) and Task 7 Step 6 admit an edit to `server/src/coord/db.ts` only when an injected migration fault first proves its loop inadequate. Neither the File Map nor Task 8 names `server/src/index.ts`. The refusal was built in those two files all the same:
  - `coordinationRestoreRefusal(ccrcDir, markerFile)` is exported from `server/src/coord/db.ts`, after `openCoordDb`, and the file gains one `lstatSync` import. `server/src/index.ts` calls it once, before `new CoordStore(openCoordDb(...))`, prints the answer with `console.error` and exits 1.
  - It returns the refusal text when the restore marker exists, and `null` only on ENOENT. Any other answer (a directory, a dangling link, a parent that is not a directory, a permission error) is a refusal, because a marker that cannot be measured is not a marker that is missing.
  - **Why it lives there.** The check must answer before the coordination database is opened, so that the process stops instead of opening a `coord.db` that disagrees with half-replaced sidecars. `db.ts` is the module that decides whether `coord.db` may be opened, and it already holds the server's other refusal to start (`CoordDbUnmigratable`). `index.ts` is the wiring ring (L5, which decides nothing) and starts the server when it is imported, so a decision placed there could not be called by a row. The marker's file name is handed in by the caller, because `config.ts` imports `db.ts` and the delegation modules must not enter every importer's graph.
  - **What did not change.** `openCoordDb`, its migration loop and the `CoordDbUnmigratable` path are as they were; the diff to `db.ts` is the import and the new function. No migration fault asked for the edit, so Task 7 Step 6's admission is not what licensed it. The review's minority reading, that the note is scoped to the migration loop, is recorded here and not relied on. No code moved in the fix round for review 518.
  - **Rows**, all in `delegation-watch.test.ts`, in "boot refusal (R24): an active restore marker, or one that cannot be measured, stops the server before coord.db is opened":
    - "a marker present is a refusal that names the recovery command and says it is the server";
    - "no marker (ENOENT, even when the directory itself does not exist yet) is no refusal";
    - "fail closed: any other answer than \"absent\" is a refusal, because an unmeasurable marker is not a missing one";
    - "a marker that is a directory or a dangling link is still a marker";
    - "index.ts checks it BEFORE the line that opens coord.db, says it with console.error, and exits nonzero";
    - "the real server process exits 1 with the refusal on stderr and never opens coord.db".

  Commit `739735f38`.
- **D-4661** — `the-worktree-listing-is-spliced-onto-the-jq-line` (Task 5; held-out review 518 F9; coordinator mail 5512): spec §5.3 ("the line is built in the hook's existing single `jq` call") and Task 5 Step 7 ("Implement the hook append in its existing `jq` call") put the whole spool line in that call. Two parts of a Bash worktree line are not built there, and the hook comment "THE LISTING" in `ccd/session-hook.sh` says so:
  - The `listing` is built by a shell function, `_hook_spool_listing`, from the `.git` entry found walking up from the line's cwd. `_hook_spool_append` splices it onto the jq-built line as text.
  - When the listing is dropped, `"truncated":true` is added to the line the same way, by cutting the closing brace and appending the key.

  **Why.** jq cannot list a directory, and the cwd that names the directory is itself learned from the payload by that one jq call. So the listing cannot be built inside the call, and it cannot be built before it, because the cwd is not yet parsed. A second jq call after the cwd is known would add a fork to every Bash worktree event.

  **The hot-path contract still holds.** Each part, checked in the hook at this commit:
  - **No extra fork.** `_hook_spool_listing` runs no external command: it uses `[[ ]]`, `read -N`, parameter expansion, `shopt` and the glob `"$w"/*`. The one jq call stays the payload parse. (The hourly prune's `rm`, only when a stale bucket exists, is older and unrelated.)
  - **One write.** The finished line goes out in one `printf '%s\n' "$dsp_line" >> "$f"` to an O_APPEND descriptor, after the splice.
  - **Under 4 KiB.** `_hook_spool_append` writes nothing unless `${#dsp_line} + 1 < DSP_LINE_BELOW` (4096), under `LC_ALL=C`, so the count is bytes. The spliced candidate is tested against the same bound before it is taken. On a trigger line the jq reserves the 17 bytes of `,"truncated":true` (`$rs`), so the dropped-listing form still fits.
  - **The listing is shed first.** It is added only when the jq line shed nothing (the line does not end in `"truncated":true}`), so a line that already shed fields never carries one. When it does not fit, or a name or the count is out of bound, it is dropped whole and the line is marked truncated. No other optional field is shed for it.

  **Pins.** The review found the splice's two edge conditions unpinned (F1: the 17-byte reservation; F2: the rule that a line that already shed carries no listing). They are pinned in `delegation-spool.test.ts` by R96 (a near-cap worktree Bash line whose listing is dropped is still written) and R97 (a line that already shed fields carries no listing), both added in the fix round for review 518. The contract's other parts are pinned by existing rows in the same file:
  - "listing is dropped FIRST when it cannot fit: the other optional fields stay, truncated is set, the line is under the cap";
  - "when the listing is dropped the line is exactly the line it would have been with no listing, plus truncated:true";
  - "the shed order is listing, claudeVersion, cwd, agentType, transcriptPath: ...";
  - "the strict boundary: a line that is 4095 bytes with its LF is kept whole; 4096 is shed";
  - "a name outside [A-Za-z0-9._@+-] (or over the name bound) drops the WHOLE listing and marks the line; a name at the bound stays";
  - "more names than the count bound drops the whole listing; exactly the bound keeps it";
  - "lines near the cap do not interleave: twelve hooks at once, each writing ~3.9 KiB";
  - under "the append forks nothing the event did not already fork", "PreToolUse Bash that names a worktree: the same externals run whether the spool directory exists or not, so a line costs no fork".

  Commit `e0cbd11cb`.
- **D-4662** — `spool-bounds-and-grammars-are-chosen-in-shared-api` (Task 5; held-out review 518 F10; coordinator mail 5512): the vocabulary (Fixed Vocabulary and Interfaces, under the raw spool line) says "String and array bounds are fixed in Task 5's validation table". Task 5 has no such table. Its Step 3 names "identifier/list bounds" and fixes only the 4096-byte line cap. The worker chose the rest, from measurement, and declared them once:
  - `DELEGATION_SPOOL_BOUNDS` in `shared/api.ts`: `lineBytesBelow` 4096 (the plan's), `idBytes` 64, `ownerIdBytes` 128 (the hook's `CCRC_ID_MAX`), `labelBytes` 128, `pathBytes` 512, `listingNames` 256 and `listingNameBytes` 64. The constant's doc comment gives each value's measured basis. Every count is UTF-8 bytes.
  - The id grammar `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, for agent, tool-use, task, run and Claude session ids. It reuses the hook's own agent-id grammar (`idch`, `idmax`). Ingest spells it as `ID`.
  - The listing-name grammar `[A-Za-z0-9._@+-]{1,64}`, tested in the hook against `DSP_NAME_BYTES` and a `=~` match, and in ingest as `LISTING_NAME`.
  - A hook cannot import the constant, so `session-hook.sh` spells the numbers again (`def idmax:`, `def maxline:`, `def maxlabel:`, `def maxpath:`, `CCRC_ID_MAX`, `DSP_LINE_BELOW`, `DSP_LIST_NAMES`, `DSP_NAME_BYTES`). The server's ingest imports the constant.

  **A hook drop that leaves no trace.** An id outside the grammar is never cleaned into one. An event whose agent id is outside it writes no line, and neither does a SubagentStart or SubagentStop with one; a session id or a tool-use id outside it does the same. The hook exits 0 with nothing printed and nothing recorded: no marker, no counter, no stderr. The server cannot tell such an event from one that never happened, and only the census backstops it. This entry used to end "A lost event can delay or prevent a link and never create one (spec §5.3, D-4659)". At rung 4 that is false for a silent drop (review 556 W3). Take a dropped Bash Pre or Post of ANOTHER session whose window overlaps the creator's and whose listing would have shown the tree. Its loss removes the overlap that keeps the rung-4 candidate ambiguous, so the creator's call becomes "the one Bash call", and it links. D-4659's lost windows cover only a loss the lane can see: a shed listing, an unmeasured hour, a torn tail. A drop that leaves no trace is not one of them.

  **Two more silent exits, before the spool line is written.** Neither is a grammar drop, and each drops the WHOLE event: no join record, no turn mark, no hookstate and no spool line. `runAsync` reads exit 0 as success either way.
  - **The 2 s tmux lookup** (`"$hooktmo" 2 tmux display-message -p -t "$TMUX_PANE" '#S'`). A tmux server that does not answer within 2 s leaves the pane's name empty. A process with a pane has no fallback id, so a live pane's event exits 0 at `[[ -n "$id" ]] || exit 0`. This exit depends on time.
  - **The payload parse's read** (`{ read -r event; … read -r dsp_line; } < <(jq …) || exit 0`). The `||` tests only the last `read`, so it exits 0 whenever jq dies, cannot fork, or prints fewer than eight lines. This exit depends on resources.

  README's "What rung 4 cannot see" list does not name these drops, nor the grammar drops above. Wave 3 makes no hook change. The drops ride the list in coordinator mail 5483, which carries the hook-level drops to the operator with wave 4's plan. The contradiction between spec §5.1 and §5.3 that this exposes is answered in coordinator mail 5472: the operator ruled "keep, decide later" on 2026-10-16, for rung-4 links journaled during the soak.

  **Rows.** In `delegation-spool.test.ts`:
  - "the hook's literal bounds equal DELEGATION_SPOOL_BOUNDS" (the jq defs, and the shell constants, each spelled exactly once);
  - "every bound is a positive integer, and the cap is the plan's 4096";
  - "with every bound at its maximum, the REQUIRED fields of the largest variants still fit one line (so a line that cannot fit is a bug, not a case)";
  - "an agent_id outside [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}: no line, from any event (a cleaned lookalike must not ride, and a line without the id would read as the main thread)";
  - "a PreToolUse with no tool_use_id, or an id outside [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}, records nothing";
  - "the Claude session id is the payload's, not the environment's; a missing or malformed one records nothing";
  - "an owner id at the bound writes a line; one byte over writes nothing";
  - "a source at the label bound is kept; one byte more is not";
  - "an optional path over the byte bound is dropped, never cut, and marks the line; multibyte is counted in BYTES";
  - the two listing rows named in D-4661 for the name grammar and the name and count bounds.

  In `delegation-ingest.test.ts`: "identifiers are [A-Za-z0-9_-] and at most the bound", "labels, paths and the agent type are bounded in UTF-8 BYTES, not characters", "a listing is a bounded list of admin-record NAMES: ...", "the list-count bound alone refuses a listing of 257 SHORT names" and "the module reads no bound from a literal: every number comes from DELEGATION_SPOOL_BOUNDS or READ_FROM_MAX_BYTES".

  Commits `e0cbd11cb` and `8453b53e1` (the bounds comment, and the owner bound read from `CCRC_ID_MAX`).
- **D-4663** — `the-fallback-deploys-prune-takes-no-keep-set` (Task 8 Step 6; held-out review 518 W2; coordinator mails 5512 and 5515): Step 6 says "Keep newest N timestamp dirs, current operation dir, newest valid pre-operation coordination snapshot, and newest pre-operation tree backup independently", and D-4641 added a fifth set. Those keep sets bind only `_bak_prune` and `_bak_gc` in `ccd/ccrc`. A third pruner applies none of them:
  - `prune_backups` in `deploy/deploy.sh` lists `~/ccrc-backups/[0-9]{8}-[0-9]{6}`, sorts by name and runs `rm -rf` on all but the newest `CCRC_BACKUP_KEEP` (default 10), through `head -n -$keep`. That is age only, with no classifier and no update lock. `ccrc update`'s `_upd_backup` names its directories in the same `%Y%m%d-%H%M%S` shape, so the glob reaches them too. The fallback deploy calls it once on each of its two arms, after that arm's own verification.
  - Since this wave, the server arm of the same deploy writes the O3 coordination snapshot into one of those directories (`coordination-snapshot.mjs capture`, before the rsync). So the eviction D-4641 reserves to the operator, an empty snapshot displacing the newest data-bearing one past `CCRC_BACKUP_KEEP`, can happen through this pruner, which counts directories and cannot tell them apart.
  - At `CCRC_BACKUP_KEEP=0`, `head -n -0` lists every directory, so the pruner removes the directory the same run just captured. At the default, the directory just captured always survives.

  Wave 3 makes no `deploy.sh` edit. The file is inside the centralised-update programme's W16 fenced region (PR #444, which replaces the line that reads `CCRC_BACKUP_KEEP` with a check that accepts 0 through 9999).

  **The operator's ruling of 2026-10-16 (mail 5515):**
  1. The fallback deploy's age-only `prune_backups` is accepted for the soak.
  2. Centralised-update W16 (#444) meets the operator's condition as D-5144. At `9c355b049`, #444's `CCRC_BACKUP_KEEP` check accepts 0 through 9999 (`deploy/deploy.sh` line 242). W16 keeps that test byte-equal to `_keep_ok` (D-5142) and adds a second, separate test: `prune_backups` refuses any all-zeros `CCRC_BACKUP_KEEP` (`0`, `00`, `0000`), prunes nothing, warns and returns 1. Its directory selection and its delete line stay byte-unchanged. `_bak_prune` and `_bak_gc` keep accepting 0 through 9999. D-5144 lands in W16's landing round, after #448, so it is pending at this writing (its head is not yet known).
  3. `prune_backups` gets the full keep sets in a later wave, which the coordinator carries on delegation-broker wave 4's list.

  **Rows.** None pins the keep sets on this pruner, because it has none. "both branches prune timestamped backups to a bounded set, without failing a completed deploy" in `agent/test/deploy-verify.test.ts` pins its present shape (timestamped directories only, `head -n -`, `xargs -r rm -rf`, one call per arm).

  Commits: none in this wave changed `prune_backups`; the coordination snapshot that now shares its directories is `a0c385acc`.
- **Unused:** D-4664 was issued to this wave (block D-4633..D-4664) and is unused.

Found in the wave-3 follow-up (run 600, block D-5433..D-5438, taken in order; each defined in the commit after its work):
- **D-5433** — `a-deliberate-stop-is-not-a-death` (item 1; review 578 F4; the operator's ruling of 2026-10-18, "Fix before soak"): the lane's circuit breaker counted every parent it saw alive and then `dead` as a fresh death, so #512's `ccd stop --surface agent`, an operator's `ccd stop`, `ws-archive` and #594's `ccd hibernate` read as deaths, and two of them inside ten minutes read `suspended` (D-4659's F4 bullet). `DelegationLane.noteSessions` now reads the row's stop evidence, `stoppedBy` (epoch ms) or, when there is no stamp, `archivedAt` (epoch seconds, × 1000). A dead row carrying it is not a fresh death, and a death already recorded for it is erased, when the stop's instant is no later than the recorded death plus `DELEGATION_CLOCK_SKEW_MS`, or, with no recorded death, this pass's instant plus that skew. The skew is there because the stamp is the fleet box's clock, and the bound on a recorded death covers the torn tick: `assembleFleet` reads the registry before it probes tmux, so a stop can read as a crash for one pass and show its stamp on the next. A stop stamped later than that leaves the death counted (an operator cleaning up after a crash), and every other dead row counts as before, `restarting` and unmeasurable rows included; a swap stays on wave 4's list. A hibernated row is excluded at any instant by main's own D-5242 conjunct (#595), and D-5433 also clears the death a torn tick records for one: `cmd_hibernate` writes `.hibernated`, then the stop stamp through `_ws_unsupervise`, then kills the pane, so a pass can see the pane gone with neither field set, and on the next pass the stamp is inside the skew of that recorded death. In the absorb of main, `noteSessions` tests the status once per row and reads an absent stop field as null (a lane caller may hand rows without them), which keeps the hibernation census's `dead-compare` count in `watch.ts` at 5. Bounds: lane memory only; `LaneSession`'s Pick gains `stoppedBy` and `archivedAt`; no policy code, constant, signature, file, wire type, health field, column, journal or snapshot change. **Residual:** a crash followed by a stop or an archive within one pass, or within the skew of its recorded death, is not counted; the 60 s cadence and the clocks' sync bound it. The rule bounds the stop's instant from above only, so a stop stamp or an archive marker that outlived its pane (`shared/api.ts`'s note on markers that outlive their pane) also hides that pane's later crash; a lower bound would be a new ruling, and is not taken here. An archive stamps `.stopped` through `_ws_unsupervise` before it kills the pane and writes `.archived` after, so it normally carries `stoppedBy`; the `archivedAt` fallback serves a row with no stop stamp, an archive older than the stamp or one whose stamp a revival cleared. **Rows**, in `delegation-watch.test.ts`'s breaker describe, beside the three existing rows, which are unedited: R1 "two parents stopped on purpose inside ten minutes are not a mass death: the breaker and the health read clear, and the lease is made"; R2 "two unstamped archives (archivedAt set, stoppedBy null) are not a mass death either"; R3 "one parent stopped and one that crashed is one fresh death: clear"; R4 "the torn tick: a stop that read as a crash on one pass and carries its stamp on the next (a stamp before the recorded death) erases that death"; R5 "the torn tick with the fleet clock ahead: a stamp 30 s after the recorded death is inside the skew, so it still erases that death". R1 to R5 are red at main (`expected 'suspended' to be 'clear'`, at `517ed6cba`) and green at the tip. R6 "a stop stamped later than the recorded death plus the skew (an operator cleaning up after a crash) leaves the death counted", R7 "an unstamped archive later than the recorded death plus the skew leaves the death counted (archivedAt is read in seconds)" and R8 "stamps more than the skew past this pass's own instant, on parents first seen dying, are not a stop: both count" pin today's answer, green at both. R9 "stamps 30 s past this pass's own instant (the fleet clock ahead), on parents first seen dying, are inside the skew: neither counts" was added because the table's runs found a mutant R1 to R8 let live (below). `sessionOf` in `delegation-watch.test.ts` and its copy in `delegation-wave3.integration.test.ts` take an optional override, defaulting `stoppedBy` and `archivedAt` to null. **Mutation table, run:** ignore `stoppedBy` reds R1, R3, R4, R5 and R9; ignore `archivedAt` reds R2; never erase a recorded death reds R4 and R5; erase whatever the stop's instant reds R6 and R7; drop the skew from the recorded bound reds R5; read `archivedAt` without the × 1000 reds R7; drop the first-pass bound reds R8. Dropping the skew from the first-pass bound alone survived R1 to R8, because R8's stamps are twice the skew ahead; R9 reds it. Commits `517ed6cba` (the rows, red at main), `dc5464859` (the fix), the absorb of main, `e3b8ef5cd`, and `44fd7e371` (review 610 F1: the outlived-stamp residual in the code comment).
- **D-5434** — `the-first-joins-never-refuse-each-other` (item 2; CI run 39103454968 attempt 1, job `server 7/12`, `expected [ 'w0-1' ] to deeply equal []`; the operator's ruling of 2026-10-18): a bounded diagnosis of the lost join in "a workflow fan-out: eight SubagentStarts at once, three rounds, all recorded". **The paths** by which one SubagentStart can end without its join line durable: (1) the bounded tmux lookup (`"$hooktmo" 2 tmux display-message …`): an empty answer leaves `$id` empty, and the event exits 0 at `[[ -n "$id" ]] || exit 0`; (2) the payload parse's read (`{ read -r event; … } < <(jq …) || exit 0`); (3) `_hook_join_record`'s entry guard, `[[ ! -L "$jf" && ( -f "$jf" || ! -e "$jf" ) ]] || return 1`, which the caller's `|| true` hides. (1) and (2) are D-4662's two silent exits, which drop the whole event. The lock, the rename and the cap rotation run only past `JOIN_ROTATE_AT` (128) lines, which this case's 24 ids never reach. **The reproduction** (in a scratch test, not committed): (1) and (2) are deterministic with a BASH_ENV shadow (`timeout() { return 124; }`, `jq() { return 1; }`): no join line. (3) is a lost update: a first writer sees `-f` false (no file yet), a sibling's `>>` creates the file, and the same writer's `! -e` then sees it, so the guard reads "exists and is not a regular file" and refuses, while every lookup succeeded. Plain rounds of eight simultaneous first Starts on a fresh row, 200 of them, lost nothing either way, because the window lies between two `stat` calls of one `[[ ]]` and the hooks arrive milliseconds apart. With a barrier (a BASH_ENV `set -T` and a DEBUG trap that holds each writer just before the guard until all eight have arrived, changing no hook code and no command's answer): at main, 4 lines lost in 400 rounds, each matched by the guard's `return 1`; with the operands reordered, 0 in 400, and no refusal. The case itself, run 30 times at main (`-t 'a workflow fan-out'`, each under the gate), was green 30 times. **Load, a substitution of the ruling's "under load":** every run was slowed in its own scope (`systemd-run … -p CPUQuota=25%`), never by loading the shared box. **The decision:** a lost update, so a wave-3 defect, fixed. The first fix reordered the operands, `( ! -e "$jf" || -f "$jf" )`; review measured that it opens the mirror race, a sibling rotator's `mv -f` landing between the two reads, so the guard now refuses only a POSITIVE non-regular type: `-L`, `-d`, `-p`, `-S`, `-b` or `-c`. No move between absent and regular makes any of these true, so neither a creation nor a rotation can refuse an append, and a symlink (a dangling one too), directory, FIFO, socket or device is still refused. Measured against a path a background loop creates and renames away, about 4 s per guard: main's order refused 315 of 126,920 checks, the reordered form 416 of 136,130, the positive-type guard 0 of 73,290. **Row:** no shadow can split one `[[ ]]`, and a many-round race row is the flaky row the task text forbids, so the red-first row is a source pin, "the entry guard refuses only on positive non-regular type tests, never on absence or regularity, so neither a creation nor a rotation race refuses an append (D-5434)", red at main and green at the tip. After review 610 (F2, F3) the pin asserts all six type tests, and that no code line between the function's opening and its `>>` append carries `-f "$jf"` or `! -e "$jf"`. Mutants, whole file each: main's line and the reordered line each red it; deleting `-S || -b || -c` (H6) reds it, which survived the first pin; a second two-read refusal after the guard (`[[ -e "$jf" && ! -f "$jf" ]] && return 1`) reds it; dropping `-L`, and dropping `-p`, each red it and "a directory, a symlink or a FIFO at the path joins nobody, is never written through, and never blocks the hook" (which, without `-p`, hangs on the FIFO until its timeout); removing the guard reds the same two; dropping `-d` reds the pin only, because `>>` onto a directory fails and returns 1 anyway. D-4662's two exits are unchanged, and stay on wave 4's list. **Not fixed, the same shape:** `_hook_spool_append` (the delegation spool's append) and `_hook_history_scope` open with the same `( -f || ! -e )` guard, so the spool's first-creation race can lose a spool line in the same way; that is outside this ruling, and goes to wave 4's list. The "concurrent rotators" red (mail 5849) is likely the same cause: a rotation's `mv -f` leaves `joined` absent, so the appenders after it are first writers to a fresh file, and under main's order one whose `-f` saw the path renamed away and whose `! -e` then saw a sibling's new file refused. The positive-type guard closes that too; it was not reproduced separately. D-5435's cause does not apply on Linux. Commits `e4143582e` (the reordered guard and the pin), `4b0917c2b` (the positive-type guard, after review) and `44fd7e371` (review 610 F2 and F3: the pin's six types and its scan to the append).
- **D-5435** — `the-join-rotation-runs-on-macos` (item 3; the operator's ruling of 2026-10-18, "Add to the follow-up"; main's daily 39093762060 and another branch's full run 39096832066): `full-suite` needs `test-macos`, so 11 macOS reds in wave 3's own files (10 steady, one flaky) blocked every stable promotion. **The rotation family, 7 cases** ("the record rotates by RENAME …", 4; "no window orphans a join record …", 2; "rotation cannot be turned against a swapped path …", 1): Homebrew's `flock` is installed on the runner, and the cases red even with `flock` shadowed to succeed. A darwin-only probe (full run 39124847732, removed again) measured the cause: `stat` of `/dev/fd/N` on macOS answers the file's inode but the fdesc mount's device, so `[[ "$jf" -ef "/dev/fd/$fd" ]]` was always false there, and the rotation never ran where Homebrew supplies `flock`. The hook already knew this of `/dev/fd` on macOS: `_hook_lock_same`'s comment records the fdesc device, measured there. A code defect, fixed: the re-check compares the locked descriptor with a fresh descriptor of the path (`[[ "/dev/fd/$fd2" -ef "/dev/fd/$fd" ]]`), so both sides read through the same `/dev/fd` on either platform and the inode decides; no fork, no platform branch. On Linux "a rotator whose path no longer names its inode skips: the -ef re-check" stays green and reds when the comparison is made always true. **The doctor's spool cases, 3:** on darwin the check asks `_svc_is_active` per unit (`launchctl print`), never systemd's batched `is-active`. "asks the service manager ONCE for all its pending units, in one is-active call" and "a short answer from the manager (fewer words than units) is never read as "not running"" pin that batched call, so they are Linux-only by design and skip on darwin, each with its reason. "a session the manager could not be asked about is a WARN, never a pass and never a missing directory" holds under launchctl, so it is fixed, not skipped: on darwin it plants the launchctl fixture empty (launchctl then exits 1, which is no answer) and reads launchd's remedy; its Linux arm is unchanged. **Verification:** full run 39136192335 at `380ee56fa` verified both on darwin: its `test-macos` ran `session-hook-turnmark.test.ts` (109 cases, none skipped) and `ccrc-doctor.test.ts` green, and the two doctor skips skip there. The descriptor compare is pinned by `test-macos` alone: restoring main's path-against-descriptor `-ef` is green on Linux, and `test-macos` never runs on a pull request. Commit `c992c704e`. **The spool's interleave, the eleventh, flaky** (the coordinator's mail 6462; one red in four darwin runs): `delegation-spool.test.ts` › "lines near the cap do not interleave: twelve hooks at once, each writing ~3.9 KiB" red on darwin in job 117716174716 (another branch's full run 39140769427, which touches no spool code) with `expected 4948 to be less than 4096`, and passed in 39136192335, 39093762060 and 39096832066. The cause, shown from the source: the line is written by a builtin `printf`, which goes through bash's stdout (`shell_initialize`'s `sh_setlinebuf`, a line-buffered `setvbuf` of `BUFSIZ`), and macOS's `BUFSIZ` is 1024; its line-buffered `__sfvwrite` writes a long line one buffer at a time, so a 3,924-byte line is four write(2)s, and a racing hook's whole line can land between two of them. The log matches it to the byte: the merged line is 4,947 bytes, which is 1,024 plus 3,923, the length of `toolu_big6`'s line (the twelve lengths measured on Linux). Linux never shows it: `strace -e write` of bash 5.2.21 on glibc measured a 3,924-byte line as one write, 8,192 bytes as 4096 + 4096, and 9,001 as 4096 + 4096 + 809, because glibc sizes the buffer from the file's `st_blksize`. The hook's comment, that one `printf` of a line under 4 KiB is one write(2), was true on glibc only. The join record's and the history spool's lines stay under 1 KiB, so they are one write on macOS too. Fixed by D-5436.
- **D-5436** — `the-spool-cap-is-the-platforms-buffer` (D-5435's eleventh case; the operator's ruling of 2026-10-18, about 17:45Z, "macOS cap, record losses", relayed in the coordinator's mail 6499): a departure from the plan's one 4096 cap. Spec §5.3's "no locks, no waiting, no extra forks on the probe path" refused a lock and an external writer, so the cap is the platform's. One value drives every check: the statement before the spool gate, on the line above the parse, sets `DSP_LINE_BELOW` to 4096 only where `${OSTYPE:-}` is `linux-gnu*` and `${BASH_VERSINFO[5]}` is `*-linux-gnu*`, and to `DSP_LINE_SMALL` (1008) everywhere else; the jq's `maxline` reads it as `dspcap` (so its 17-byte reservation follows), and the listing splice and the final gate read it by name. 1008, not 1024, because a bash 4.4 to 5.1 built with its own malloc has a 1008-byte buffer; musl splits as macOS does. `OSTYPE` comes from the environment, which is how a row forces the small cap on Linux; `BASH_VERSINFO` is compiled in and read-only. `DSP_LINE_SMALL` is pinned against the new `DELEGATION_SPOOL_BOUNDS.lineBytesBelowSmall` in the existing spelled-once style, and the reader's bound stays `lineBytesBelow`, 4096. **The losses on the small cap:** a listing has room for about 15 to 20 names of the usual length, against about 130 to 136 on 4096 (by the cwd's length), so a busy repository's worktree window is lost and rung 4 is unreachable there; a deep-path Workflow launch (the worst launch's required fields are under 3.2 KiB, over 1008) is not written, the D-4662 class; and "a line that cannot fit is a bug" holds on the 4096 cap only. **Residuals:** a glibc bash built with bash's own malloc passes both tests and splits lines over 2016 or 1008 bytes (distro bashes are built without it); a Linux session that exports an `OSTYPE` other than `linux-gnu` gets the small cap; and glibc sizes the buffer as the smaller of the file's `st_blksize` and 8192, so a filesystem reporting an `st_blksize` under 4096 (some FUSE or network mounts) would split lines on the 4096 cap too; the usual home filesystems report 4096. **The lossless route, for wave 4:** `history -a` appends with one O_APPEND write(2). Measured with strace on Linux (bash 5.2.21): one write of 3,936, 8,204 and 9,013 bytes for history files holding a 3,924, 8,192 and 9,001-byte line (each 12 bytes more: the `#<epoch>` line an empty `HISTTIMEFORMAT` still writes). It needs `HISTFILE`, `HISTCONTROL` and `HISTIGNORE` isolated from the user's history, and `HISTTIMEFORMAT` unset, because set (even empty) it writes a `#<epoch>` line first. It is unmeasured on darwin. **Rows**, in `delegation-spool.test.ts`, red against `380ee56fa`'s hook: (a) "under the small cap a ~2 KiB line is written, shed in the declared order (claudeVersion, then cwd) and marked truncated" and (b) "under the small cap a Workflow launch whose required fields pass 1008 writes no line, and the hook exits 0", both under `OSTYPE=darwin23` on any platform; (c), the control, keeps that line whole and runs only where the native cap is 4096. The pins on the hook's literal bounds now read `def maxline: dspcap;`, the one splice that defines `dspcap` from `DSP_LINE_BELOW`, both caps, the two gates that read the variable, and the platform statement's exact text. A darwin-only row (`OSTYPE=linux-gnu` still picks 1008 on a bash not built for glibc Linux) ran and passed on darwin: full run 39156998467's `test-macos` 1/4 (job 117770051296) ran `delegation-spool.test.ts` green with 279 cases and 16 skipped, which are exactly the 4096-only cases, while Linux skips only that row. The near-cap rows (the strict cap, its boundary, the listing-first drop and R96/R97) run once per cap: the small arm everywhere, the 4096 arm only where this bash picks it, with the skip's reason in its title; the tests learn the cap by running the hook's own statement in the same bash, never from `process.platform`. The count bound's row and the ~3.9 KiB interleave arm are 4096-only, and a new arm races twelve lines just under 1008 on every platform. Against `380ee56fa`'s hook, 15 cases red: (a), (b), three pins, four small-arm strict rows, four R96 rows and two R97 rows; (c) stays green. **Mutants**, whole file each: no small arm reds 13 (the platform pin, (a), (b) and the small arms); a small cap of 4096 reds 14 (adding the shell-constants pin); a jq cap of 4096 beside the shell's reds 11 ((b) survives, because the shell's gate still refuses the line); gating on `OSTYPE` alone reds the platform pin only on Linux. `test-macos` in the full run after the last push is a no-regression check for a one-in-four flake, not its proof; rows (a) and (b) are. Spec §5.3 and the README's "path of one fact" name the small cap, in place. Commits `f7b71745e` (the cap and its rows) and `410265acb` (the round's own review: the native arms drop an inherited OSTYPE, the small cap's envelope, the residuals).

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 2 (run 627, block D-5604..D-5607, taken in order; each defined in the commit after its work):
- **D-5604** — `rung-4-reads-each-window-once-per-pass` (mails 6586, 6590 and 6593; the operator's ruling of 2026-10-19, "Fix first, then soak"; asked urgently at 04:30Z, with the restart case fixed too): the server's event loop was blocked for 15 to 22 s every 60 to 72 s, and the delegation lane's reconcile pass was the block. **The finding**, measured read-only on the server box on 2026-10-19 (the boxes run v0.0.294, and main ran this path unchanged): between blocks `/health` answers in 20 to 30 ms; during a block the main thread runs at about 100% CPU and the checkpoint's mtime ticks in the block's first second; registry reads queued behind a block come back unreadable (46 warnings in 24 h); the journal is 75.6 MB, the checkpoint 8.6 MB and coord.db 198 MB, and none of those sizes drives the block. **The cause**, with lines as of main `ad65fb394`: reconcile runs once per 60 s pass and has no await between `readMetas`' return (`watch.ts:10778`) and `applyEffects` (`:10882`). The windows are built once per pass from the 20,000-event slice through `delegationListingWindowsOf` (`:10802-10807`), plus the lane's own lost windows (the not-ready window and the retention floor); a window's `before` and `after` are the hook events' `listing` arrays (`:9543-9544`), and one `windows` array is shared by every census record's input (`inputFor`, `:10809`). Every census record of every project is correlated (`pairs`, `:10824`), and for each one rung 4 (`delegation-correlation.ts:1075`) filtered every window twice (`:1077-1078`); called `addedExactly` (`:996-1004`) on every held window of the tree's repository, which built two Sets and two arrays from the whole listing; called `addedIncludes` (`:1006-1008`, two `.includes` over the listings) on every held window that may be on the repository, in `heldCreators` (`:1085`) and in the overlap loop (`:1102`); in that loop (`:1089-1108`), once a tree has a candidate, parsed both instants of every held window that may be on the repository through `spanOf` (`:1017-1018`, `parseDelegationInstant`, about 1.1 µs each) once per candidate per ladder run; and, with an evidence horizon set (live, because the slice is full and evicting), the horizon test (`:1139-1141`) parsed every held window's `startedAt` again, and the lost-window loops (`:1048-1051`, `:1115-1118`) parsed again too. **The ladder runs per tree, k**: the lane re-runs the ladder (`watch.ts:10829-10849`): `delegationMarkForced` (`:715`) reruns it twice for an `unmeasured('evidence-horizon')` verdict under a marked index; when that answers null and a held window has a null repository, `delegationUnknownWindowForced` (`:737-750`) reruns it twice for a verdict that is not a link; `delegationLostEvidenceForced` (`:758`) reruns it once for every unmeasured verdict on a tree that no unreleased lease holds. So k is 1 for a link, 3 for an unresolved verdict, 4 for an unmeasured one, and up to 6 with marks. **After a restart** `sessionProject` is filled only from live records and sessions (`watch.ts:10239-10240`), so the slice's windows from sessions this process never saw read a null repository, and a null-repository window may be on every repository (`mayBeOn`, `:1012-1014`). At main such a window never reached `addedExactly`, because `:1079` checks the repository first; the restart's cost was `addedIncludes` at `:1085` and the parses on every window, for every rung-4 tree of every project, and it scales with the fleet-wide tree count (mail 6593; not confirmed live). **The shape**, shape 1 of the task text, inside `delegation-correlation.ts` only, with no `watch.ts` edit (run 561 holds it) and no exported signature, type or wire change: (a) `listingDeltaOf` reads and fills `listingDeltas`, a WeakMap keyed by the window, which holds the distinct names its after-listing adds to its before-listing and whether any name is removed (null for a null `after`), computed at most once per window; `addedIncludes` answers from it, as a set test, so duplicates change nothing. (e) `windowSpanOf` reads and fills `windowSpans`, a second WeakMap keyed by the same window (the task text says "the same memo"; it is a sibling map with the same key and the same lifetime, the one departure of form), holds each instant as epoch ms, or as the `DelegationModelError` it threw, which `startOf` and `spanOf` rethrow at the call site, at the moment the code reads it today, so the throw order of the `.some` short-circuits and the arms at `:1105-1106`, `:1120-1121`, `:1142-1145` and `:1050` are unchanged; an input's own instants (`evidenceHorizon`, `firstSighting`) are not memoised. (b) `soleAddedIndexOf` reads and fills `soleAddedIndexes`, a WeakMap keyed by the window LIST rung 4 is handed, and maps a held window's sole added name (it adds exactly one name and removes none) to its windows in list order, so the candidates come out in the order `held` gave them and the first other-session overlap and the first unreadable instant decide as before; `addedExactly` is deleted, its rule being the index's. The index is built at most once per list rung 4 is handed, so a forced rerun, whose functions hand fresh filtered lists, rebuilds it for that list. **The no-mutation citation**, read, not assumed: a window is an object literal built in `delegationListingWindowsOf` (`watch.ts:9514-9548`, its `before` and `after` aliasing the events' own `listing`), `delegationLostHourWindow` (`:9558`), `delegationNotReadyWindowOf` (`:9577`), `delegationLostWindowsOf` (`:9602`, whose retention floor is its own literal) or `pendingWindows` (`:10344`, through `delegationLostHourWindow`); `windows` in reconcile is a local const (`:10802-10807`) handed to every `inputFor` and never written; the forced functions hand fresh filtered lists; and `git grep` finds no write to any window field (`before`, `after`, `repository`, `listedRepository`, `lost`, `startedAt`, `endedAt`) and no in-place mutator on a listing, in `server/src` or in the delegation tests. So neither WeakMap carries an identity re-check. **The coordinator's two readings of the ruling**, which are the coordinator's and are recorded here as such: "computed once per pass" covers each window's parsed instants too (the task text's item e), because a memo of the deltas alone leaves seconds per pass (the diagnosis measured a delta-only memo at about 10 s for the horizon shape and 4.7 s for the late overlap); and "looked up" is a lookup keyed by the tree's admin record (item b). **Rows**, in the new `server/test/delegation-correlation-cost.test.ts`, named D-5604 R1 and R2 because R1 to R9 already name D-5433's rows. R1, "D-5604 R1: rung 4 reads each listing window, and parses each of its instants, a constant number of times per pass however many trees there are", in four shapes, each with one held, closed window with a null repository and the lane's two lost windows (the not-ready window and the retention floor, built by the lane's own functions): (i) at least 20 trees with no window adding their record (unresolved), (ii) the same windows with every tree under an evidence horizon, (iii) the restart, every window's repository null, (iv) four trees with a candidate window and the rest none. The calls are the lane's, in its order and under its conditions (`correlateDelegation` for every tree, then with a measured dropped index `delegationMarkForced` and, when it answers null, `delegationUnknownWindowForced`, then `delegationLostEvidenceForced` for each unmeasured verdict), each around a fresh input object over one shared windows array. The windows' `before` and `after` are Proxies that count every whole-listing read (the iterator and every array method), and `parseDelegationInstant` is wrapped, by a pass-through `vi.mock`, to count the parses of each window instant. The bound is at most 1 read per array and at most 1 parse per window carrying an instant (the lane's two lost windows share the epoch start, so that string's allowance is 2), and the same count at 20 and at 40 trees; no clock anywhere. A sanity row per shape pins the verdict, the forced kind and the lost-evidence answer of all 20 trees, so a scenario that stops reaching rung 4 reds there. R2, "D-5604 R2: the answers of the four public functions are the same before and after the cost fix": seed `0x5eed627` (the test's own mulberry32), 562 passes (2 hand-built and 560 random) and 2,537 inputs, each random pass one windows array shared by 3 to 6 census records (the two hand-built passes hold one tree each) with different admin records (15% on the other project), answered through the lane's calls; the generator makes adds, removes, duplicates and no-ops from a small name pool, null repositories, other-repository windows adding the same name, `listedRepository` absent, null, equal and other, lost and open-ended windows, same-session and other-session overlaps on a 10 s grid, a `toolUseId` reused across sessions, non-canonical instants, `bash-worktree` activities with accepted evidence, rung-3 hits and parent-cwd vetoes, measured and unmeasured dropped indexes with every mark kind, and horizons. It asserts the digest `delegationSha256Hex(canonicalDelegationJson(answers))` of `{verdict, mark, unknown, lost}` over every input in generation order, `f3048fef6dd780fb6ca4293da685e698e4d47c22ef50cd33153f31934c88c051`, computed at main and equal at the delta-only commit and at the tip, and a histogram with every item above zero: linked at rung 4 44, `overlapping-listing-window` 99, `ambiguous-listing-windows` 35 (16 of them from a closed same-session window that overlaps the candidate and adds the record with another name), `listing-window-instant-unreadable` 122, a lost creator beside a rung-3 link 171 (7 whose only lost-creator evidence is held, closed null-repository windows that each add the record with another name, and 7 twins that link at rung 3; 8 and 8 while the selector read `.some`, before run 629's correction, review 628 F2), marks lossy 67, project 61, prefix 43, path 34, id 28, the unknown-window kind 40, lost evidence forced 190, a candidate refused for a removed name 86 and for a second added name 88, rung-3 links 601, conflicting 17, `unmeasured('evidence-horizon')` 590. **Red and green:** R1 is red at main in all four shapes (per array, 60 reads at 20 trees and 120 at 40 in (i), 90 and 180 under the horizon, 62 and 122 with candidates, except `unknown`, whose `before` reads 0 and 0 in every shape and whose `after` reads 40 and 80 in (i), 80 and 160 in (ii) and 32 and 72 in (iv); in (iii) only the `after` arrays read 60 and 120, `unknown`'s included, and the `before` arrays read 2 and 2 for creator0 and creator1, 4 and 4 for creator2 and creator3, and 0 and 0 for the rest, measured again at `ad65fb394` for review 631 F6; instants 40 and 80 for the epoch start, with up to 27 parses of a candidate's instant); at the delta-only commit `87e239db6` the reads are 1 and 1 in every shape and R1 is red in (i), (iii) and (iv) on instant parses (shape (ii) parses nothing, because under a horizon `creatorMayBeLost` answers before it reads any lost window); at the tip R1 is green in all four. R2 is green at main, at `87e239db6` and at the tip, with the same digest and histogram. **Departures of the rows and the shape from the task text**, which are this fix's own and not separate numbers: (1) shape (ii) carries a rung-3 hit per tree, because the task text's "the same trees under a non-null horizon answer `unmeasured('evidence-horizon')`" cannot hold without one (`combine` drops a lost creator alone), and odd trees are path-marked so `delegationMarkForced` reruns twice; (2) R1 gains shape (iv), which the text did not ask for, because (ii) parses no window instant and the overlap-loop and horizon parses need a candidate; (3) R2 evaluates both forced kinds for every tree, a superset of the lane's `??` (which would skip the unknown-window kind after a mark); it adds nothing today, because no input has both a mark and the unknown kind; (4) the span memo, `windowSpans`, is a sibling WeakMap with the same key as `listingDeltas`, not one map; (5) the rows were rewritten under revision 2 of the task text after the delta commit, so the rows commit follows the delta commit, and the rows' red at main was measured by running them against main's module, not by commit order (`beed5317b` held their first form, with delta-only counts). **Mutation table, run** against the tip, each mutant applied to `delegation-correlation.ts` and restored: M1 the delta per call again reds R1 in all four shapes ((ii) is the isolating one at the delta-only commit); M2 removals ignored reds R2; M3 the delta built from `after` alone reds R2 (the `conflicting` item unreached) and R1 (iv)'s scenario row; M4 the memo a Map keyed by `toolUseId` reds R2 (`twinLinksAtRung3` unreached) and all four R1 cost rows (the Map leaks across passes, so the second pass reads nothing); M5 `delegationUnknownWindowForced` rerunning on copies of the windows reds R1 in all four shapes; M6 `addedIncludes` answering "the sole added name" reds R2; M7 a memo caching the first record's answer per window reds R2; M8 a lookup holding only the tree's repository's windows, so a null-repository window's `addedIncludes` answers false, reds R2 (`lostCreatorOnlyFromNullWindow` unreached); M9 the index's lists reversed reds R2; M10 the span memo turning a throw into NaN, and separately into 0, reds R2 (`unreadable` unreached) in both; M11 the span parsed per call again reds R1 in (i), (iii) and (iv) ((ii) stays green, parsing nothing). 12 of 12 mutants killed, none survived. For M2, M6, M7 and M9 the red assertion was the histogram's, which sits before the digest line, so the digest was measured alone in a scratch copy with the digest assertion moved first: it reds each of the four by itself. **The bench** (reported, not a test; a throwaway script outside the tree, nothing committed): 9,000 held windows of 126 names (fresh arrays; 40 sessions round robin; each lasting 50 ms, none overlapping), the lane's two lost windows written literally (the not-ready window and the retention floor), one held, closed window with a null repository, a 20,000-event accepted-evidence slice (18,000 Pre/Post Bash and 2,000 SubagentStart, a cwd that matches nothing), 76 trees named from the names, a measured dropped index with no keys and no marks; shapes (A) no window adds a tree's record, unresolved, (B) one window adds exactly each tree's record and every tree links at rung 4, (C) B under an `evidenceHorizon` before every window, so every verdict is `unmeasured('evidence-horizon')`, (D) B with one other-session window last in held order, so every candidate is refused as `overlapping-listing-window` only after the overlap loop has scanned every held window, (R) the restart, every repository null, at 76 trees and at 300 trees across 6 projects; the lane's calls in its order, a fresh input per call over one windows array; three runs each under a 2 GB scope, only while `free -m` showed at least 4000 available; the sha256 of every verdict, forced kind and lost flag is equal at main and at the tip in all six shapes. **k is counted**, by a counter injected into scratch copies of the module at the ladder's entry, for all six shapes at the tip (A 3, B 1, C 4, D 3, R76 3 and R300 3 for every tree, equal to the derived values) and at main for shape A (3 for every tree); the other main shapes' k is derived from the lane's documented rerun counts, and equal. **Timings, first set, on the quieter box (3 runs, seconds, range):** tip A 0.35-0.41, B 0.34-0.38, C 0.86-1.02, D 0.54-0.66, R76 0.38-0.44, R300 1.06-1.25; main A 25.4-25.5, B 9.7-10.0, C 41.1-42.5, D 28.4-29.4, R76 0.89-0.98, R300 3.07-3.48. **Second set, with the k counter in the module, at load 12 to 13 (other sessions running):** tip A 0.33-0.53, B 0.34-0.39, C 1.13-1.30, D 0.49-0.55, R76 0.44-0.46, R300 1.24-1.37; main A, re-run with the counter, 25.0-27.6. The counter is not separable from noise (the spread inside one variant is larger than any gap between variants, but the counted copy was higher in most paired runs, so a few percent to about 15% is not excluded for R300), and the higher figures of the second set are mostly the busier box. Four shapes (A, B, D, R76) stay under 1 s in both sets; C is 0.86-1.02 s on the quieter box (one run in three just over 1 s) and 1.13-1.30 s under load; R300 is 1.06-1.37 s; no reported run of either set is over 2 s, and the median of every reported set is under 2 s. The interleaved comparison runs taken beside them, at load 12 to 13, are not all under it: C, uninstrumented, 0.88, 0.93 and 1.45 s against 0.97, 1.02 and 1.26 s with the counter; R300, uninstrumented, 1.11, 1.17 and 1.21 s against 1.35, 1.38 and 1.47 s with the counter; and a second R300 set of four runs each, uninstrumented 1.13, 1.20, 1.55 and 1.59 s (median 1.37 s) against 2.17 s at the worst with the counter (1.33, 1.35, 1.62 and 2.17 s; median 1.49 s). So of the tip's runs reported here, one, the counter copy of R300 at 2.17 s under that load, was over 2 s, and two uninstrumented runs reached 1.55 and 1.59 s; the set's medians were not. **The split of the remainder** (diagnostic, not a reported run): in R300 the pairs phase is about 0.55 s and the forced phase about 0.6 s; with the slice removed R300 drops to 0.90-0.96 s and C to 0.63-0.74 s, so rung 3's unhoisted scan of `acceptedEvidence` is about 0.2 s of R300 and 0.3 s of C; a CPU profile of one R300 run (1.37 s with setup) put self time at `listingDeltaOf` 245 ms (its WeakMap lookups across about 8 million `addedIncludes` calls and the 9,000 first computations), the held, lost and `heldCreators` filter callbacks 224 ms, `rung3` 201 ms, `addedIncludes` 157 ms, `rung4` 142 ms, `soleAddedIndexOf` 111 ms (the index rebuilt for each of 600 reruns' fresh lists, 9,000 WeakMap gets each) and `delegationUnknownWindowForced` 48 ms. **The ruling**, quoted: "A second wave-3 follow-up run, opened now with fresh D-numbers. Each listing's added and removed names are computed once per pass and candidates are looked up; results unchanged, proven by an equivalence test and a test that fails if the slow per-listing work comes back. Rollout and soak start on the release that carries it. The freeze continues until it lands." The operator declined the option that would also yield in the pass, so this run adds no yield. **Residual, all on wave 4's list:** (1) what still runs per tree per pass, O(windows) each: the held and lost filters, `heldCreators`' `addedIncludes` on null-repository windows, the index rebuilt for each forced rerun's fresh list, the WeakMap lookups, rung 3's evidence scan, and `acceptedEvidence.filter` on the linked path; building the held and lost split and the index once per pass in the caller (shape 2, which needs one optional field on `DelegationCorrelationInput` and a `watch.ts` edit) removes most of them; (2) what a restart still does to VERDICTS: a null-repository window vetoes a rung-4 link until the slice turns over, which this run does not change (it fixes the restart's cost only); (3) the flush and repair projections beside the block, about 0.5 to 3 s, split by fsync awaits; (4) no yield in the pass (declined). **Bounds:** one file of source, `delegation-correlation.ts`, with its L1 header clause updated; no exported signature, type, wire, `watch.ts`, README or spec change; the module stays free of the filesystem, the clock, randomness and the store, and its only state is three module-level WeakMaps: `listingDeltas` and `windowSpans`, keyed by the window, and `soleAddedIndexes`, keyed by the window list. Commits `87e239db6` (the delta memo), `0e7f86bc4` (D-5604 R1 and R2), `228e72c78` (the span memo and the lookup), and this entry's commit.
