Found in the wave-3 follow-up 3 (run 629, block D-5642..D-5645, taken in order; each defined in the commit after its work):
- **D-5642** — `the-ladder-builds-the-pass-wide-work-once-per-pass` (review 628 F1 and F9; the operator's ruling of 2026-10-19, "Bound it before soak"): after #681 the reconcile pass no longer blocked for 15 to 22 s, but it still grew by about 4 ms per census tree, because work done once per tree, and again per ladder rerun, traversed arrays that every tree shares. **The census**, every traversal of a pass-wide array (the listing windows, the checkpoint's activities, the accepted evidence, the point metas) that runs once per tree or once per rerun, with lines in `delegation-correlation.ts` as of `59984b350`. *Every tree:* `delegationMarkForced`'s held filter (`:721`); `delegationUnknownWindowForced`'s `.some` (`:742`), held filter (`:743`) and named-repository filter (`:745`); `delegationLostEvidenceForced`'s held filter (`:761`); `claimsOf`'s three loops over the activities, the metas and the evidence (`:816`, `:817`, `:819`), run for every `agent-*` or `wf_*` tree and for every rung-3 hit (`:971`); `rung2`'s activities filter (`:914`), metas filter (`:916`) and evidence loop (`:924`); `rung3`'s evidence loop (`:949`), its copy-on-append of a parent's reported journal ids (`:954`, quadratic in the parent's events at a path) and its `activities.find` per matching event (`:960`); `rung4`'s held filter (`:1137`), lost filter through `mayBeOn` (`:1138`, `:1054-1056`), the sole-added index (`:1139`), rebuilt for each forced rerun's fresh list, and `heldCreators` (`:1145`); and `creatorMayBeLost`'s loops over the lost windows and the held creators (`:1108-1117`), which the lane always reaches, because the retention floor and the not-ready window are always in the list. *Bounded or link-only:* `combine`'s `parentOf` (`:1228`, once per linked rung); `delegationLeaseSourceOfLinked` (`:1273`, called by the policy per linked tree); the policy's `holdersOf` (`delegation-policy.ts:348`, the open leases, per linked or ccd-workspace record); `readAgentMeta`'s `.some` (`watch.ts:10968`, inside the meta-read budget); and `delegationGoneTrees`' `records.some` per remembered tree (`:442`), reached through `pruneDropped` (`watch.ts:10788`) on a prune pass only. *Candidate-only:* the overlap loop over `held` (`:1149-1168`), the candidate's lost-window loop (`:1175-1182`), `activities.find` (`:1184`), `acceptedEvidence.filter` (`:1186`), the horizon `.some` (`:1200`) and the filter of the tree's own candidates (`:1139`). *Reconcile's stretch* (`watch.ts:10778-10882`) itself traverses no pass-wide array per tree: it builds the windows, the evidence and the metas once, computes `windows.some` (`:10829`) and `leasedNow` once, and `inputFor` builds an object per call. **The shape**, shape 1, inside `delegation-correlation.ts`, with no exported signature, type or wire change: WeakMap memos keyed by the pass-wide arrays and by the lists derived from them, each built the first time a tree asks (`memoOf`). (a) `partitionOf`, keyed by a window list: its held windows in list order (the list itself when none is lost, and the held list is given its own entry, sharing everything but the lost windows, so a forced rerun handed `held` meets the same memos); its held windows with a named repository (the list `delegationUnknownWindowForced` hands over); whether a held window's repository is null (`:742`'s `.some`); the sole-added index of D-5604 (b); the held null-repository windows by EVERY name their delta adds; its lost windows and its open-ended held windows; and, per project, built when first asked (`projectWindowsOf`), the lost windows that may be on it IN LIST ORDER (the candidate's loop reads them in order), their span index, and the start index of the open-ended held windows that may be on it. `heldCreators` becomes those two lookups, one per arm of R85, and `creatorMayBeLost`'s two loops become order-free lookups: the lost spans sorted by start with a running maximum of ends (any overlap of [lo, hi]), and the creators' sorted starts (any start in [lo, hi]), each with a flag for a refused instant, because every exit of those loops answers true. (b) `activityIndexOf`, keyed by the activities: by (sourceKind, upstreamId) and by activityId, each key's rows in input order, so a duplicated key still answers its FIRST row, as `find` and `filter(...)[0]` did. (c) `metasOf`, keyed by the metas: by (sourceKind, upstreamId), in input order. (d) `evidenceIndexOf`, keyed by the evidence: by `cwd` (a non-empty string) for rung 3; by the agent id each event CLAIMS (`claimedAgentOf`, which replaces `claimsAgent`: a SubagentStart by its `agentId`, an Agent or Task launch response by its launch's `agentId`; `claimsAgent(e, x)` equals `claimedAgentOf(e) === x` for every string x); and by agent id, the SubagentStart and SubagentStop events naming it, for rung 2's spool; only string ids are keys, because the ids asked about are strings and `===` never matched anything else. (e) `claimsOf`, memoised per (activities, metas, evidence) and then per sourceKind and upstream id; the cached claims are shared and no caller writes them. (f) `rung3`'s reported journal ids are pushed, not copied (the list reaches an answer only through `ids()`). (g) `combine`'s `parentOf` reads the activityId index: the census lists it as link-only, but D-5642 R1's shape (iv), whose plain trees link at rung 3, counts its `find` once per linked tree, so it is indexed too (a departure of form, recorded here). Every index holds COPIES of the fields it answers with, taken once per pass, so a tree reads plain data and never the shared row again. Rung 4's candidate-only work is unchanged. `listingDeltaOf` builds its two Sets with `for…of` loops, not `new Set(array)`: on node 24 `new Set` over a frozen array loses a fast path (measured twice over 18,000 listings of 126 names: plain array 97 and 101 ms, frozen array 131 and 109 ms, frozen with `for…of` 98 and 89 ms, frozen with an indexed loop 85 and 90 ms), and an indexed loop would read no listing through its iterator, which D-5604 R1's floor counts. **The order that reaches an answer**, kept: `held` in the overlap loop and the project's lost windows in the candidate's lost-window loop (the first unreadable instant and the first overlap give different reasons); rung 2's `activities[0]`; the first match of every `find`. Rung 3's `hits` order reaches no answer (`only` is read only when there is one hit, and every journal list passes through `ids()`). **The no-mutation citations**, read, not assumed: D-5643 freezes the windows, their listings and the window list, the evidence array and its `{journalId, event}` rows (not each row's `event`), and the point metas and their array, where the lane builds them (every array afresh each pass; the meta rows frozen in place); `state.checkpoint.activities` outlives a pass, and is replaced, never written: `state()` returns the held state (`delegation-checkpoint.ts:1503`), `this.current` is only ever reassigned (`:1536-2041`), and a new activities array is built only when a checkpoint is built (`:935`); and `git grep` finds no write to any field an index reads (`activityId`, `parentId`, `parentIncarnation`, `sourceKind`, `upstreamId`, `identityJournalId`, `worktreePath`, `evidenceJournalId`, an event's `cwd`, `agentId`, `eventKind`, `toolName`, `launch`, `sessionId`) in `server/src` or `server/test` after construction (the ingest's own `optional.cwd`/`optional.agentId` build the event). `DelegationCorrelationInput` now says its four arrays are the pass's and never changed once handed over. **The coordinator's reading of the ruling**, which is the coordinator's and is recorded here as such: "the shared work" covers every pass-wide array a tree's ladder traverses, the events, the activity rows and the point metas as well as the listings, because a per-tree scan of any of them still makes a pass scale with checkouts. **Rows**, red first, in the rows commit `9796d8381`, which also holds every digest and count taken at main, in the new `server/test/delegation-correlation-bound.test.ts`. R1, "D-5642 R1: a pass reads each pass-wide array, and each of its elements, a constant number of times however many trees it correlates, over shapes with no rung-4 candidate": ONE world of 40 census records, built afresh (fresh Proxies) for each of two runs of the lane's calls in the lane's order, a fresh input per call, over the first 20 records and then over all 40; each of the four arrays is a Proxy counting gets of the iterator, of every array method and of every numeric index, and each element (each window, each evidence row and its event, each activity row, each point meta) is a Proxy counting gets of its own fields, so a per-tree traversal of a memoised derived list shows through its elements when it reads one of their fields; in five shapes, none with a rung-4 candidate: (i) unresolved, (ii) under a horizon, every tree with a rung-3 hit and odd trees path-marked, (iii) the restart, every window's repository null, (iv) `agent-*` trees that link at rung 2 and plain trees that link at rung 3, and (v), added after review 631 (F1), (i) without the lane's two lost windows, a list the lane never builds: with no lost window the partition hands the counted list itself back as `held`, so a per-tree scan of `held` that reads no window field (M14 and M15 in their field-free forms, below) counts as array reads, which (i) to (iv) cannot see, because there `held` is a plain copy and `listingDeltaOf` is a memo no Proxy sees; (v) relies on the partition's identity path (limit three below; review 634 F1); each array's count, and the largest count of any one element per family, must be the same at 20 trees as at 40, with a floor (> 0 at 20 trees) for the arrays a shape reaches at main; a scenario row per shape pins every tree's verdict, forced kind and lost flag. Three limits, stated in the row's header (review 631 F3, review 634 F1): no shape reaches a rung-4 candidate, so the candidate path, which still scans per tree, is not measured (residual (1)); and read counting cannot see a per-tree rebuild of the span and start indexes (`spanIndexOf`, `startIndexOf`; for example `nullAddingStartsOf` without its cache), which are built from data already parsed and memoised; and R1 sees the field-free M14 and M15 only while the partition hands back the input list itself as `held` when no window is lost (`delegation-correlation.ts:1236`): a refactor that always copies `held` (`const heldList = held;`) keeps every answer and every cost and leaves them unseen, and no row can red it without a counter or seam in the module, because `partitionOf` is not exported. At main, arrays at 20 / 40 trees (listing windows, activities, evidence, metas): (i) 911/1811, 0/0, 120/240, 0/0; (ii) 1181/2351, 460/920, 7560/15120, 180/360; (iii) 1245/2475, 550/1100, 3600/7200, 90/180; (iv) 251/491, 850/1700, 1680/3360, 640/1280; (v), with main's module overlaid on `7dafafe46`, 789/1569, 0/0, 120/240, 0/0; and the busiest element per family (window, evidence row, event, activity, meta): (i) 464/924, 120/240, 60/120, 0/0, 0/0; (ii) 674/1344, 360/720, 565/1105, 2190/4380, 180/360; (iii) 424/839, 240/480, 355/685, 1095/2190, 90/180; (iv) 64/124, 80/160, 125/245, 300/600, 68/128; (v) 464/924, 120/240, 60/120, 0/0, 0/0. At the tip every count is the same at 20 and at 40 trees: arrays (i) 11, 0, 2, 0; (ii) 11, 2, 42, 2; (iii) 15, 6, 30, 2; (iv) 11, 22, 42, 21; (v) 9, 0, 2, 0; busiest element (i) 11, 2, 13, 0, 0; (ii) 11, 2, 14, 6, 7; (iii) 14, 2, 14, 6, 7; (iv) 7, 2, 14, 6, 7; (v) 11, 2, 13, 0, 0. R2, "D-5642 R2: the answers of rungs 2 and 3 are the same before and after the bound": D-5604 R2 never reaches rung 2 (every record is `wt-raw-*`) and holds no metas, so a second seeded stream (its own mulberry32 seed, about 580 passes, 2,336 inputs) answers `agent-*`, `wf_*` and plain trees through the lane's calls, with activity rows built by hand where the checkpoint cannot make them, and asserts the digest `726f5907838d385e514957818a619de9b375ec10d97f8a9e4b3b967ba8494029` of `{verdict, mark, unknown, lost}` and a histogram whose every item is above zero: claims made by a SubagentStart only, by an Agent or Task launch response only, and by a meta only; a SubagentStop alone and other tools' launch objects, which claim nothing; a `wf_` tree with a meta and no activity row; one parentId under two incarnations; claims by two parents; one upstream id under two sourceKinds (an `agent-<x>` tree beside a `bash-worktree` or `workflow` row or meta for `<x>`, and an other-kind meta under the claimant itself); parent-reported paths, including a report from the claimant's sibling incarnation; SubagentStart and SubagentStop at the path; metas that disagree on the path; rows sharing a key with different activityIds and rows sharing an activityId with different identity records, in front of and behind the original; two hits on one path from one parent and from two; a parent claiming by both a start and a launch; rung-3 and rung-4 links side by side, from the same parent and from two (`rungs-disagree`, which is what reaches `parentOf`); and every rung-2 and rung-3 reason, with `linked` at both rungs. It is green at main and at the tip. R3, "D-5642 R3: a candidate meets its project's lost windows in list order", added because no row saw that order (mutant M26 below): one candidate, an every-repository lost window with a refused instant and a lost window named for the project that overlaps it; in that list order the answer is `listing-window-instant-unreadable`, and in the other `evidence-horizon`, at main and at the tip. D-5604 R1 and R2 are unchanged, and D-5604 R2's digest `f3048fef6dd780fb6ca4293da685e698e4d47c22ef50cd33153f31934c88c051` is unchanged; its F2 counts were taken at main in the rows commit. **Mutation table, run** against the tip, each mutant applied and restored: M14 (candidates from a per-tree filter over the memoised held list), in two forms: reading a window field in the filter (`w.repository`), it reds R1 in all five shapes, on the busiest window in (i) to (iv) and on the window list's reads in (v); field-free (the delta from `listingDeltaOf` alone, the repository filter after it), it reds R1 in (v) only, on the window list's reads, and survived every row before (v) (review 631 F1 and F2); M15 (the sole-added index rebuilt per call from the held list), in the same two forms: reading `w.lost` in the build, it reds R1 in all five, on the busiest window in (i) to (iv) and on the window list's reads in (v); field-free, it reds R1 in (v) only; M16 (the forced reruns build fresh derived lists again, in all three of `delegationMarkForced`, `delegationUnknownWindowForced` and `delegationLostEvidenceForced`), with `filter` and with an indexed `for` loop, reds R1 in (i) to (iii) and (v) ((iv)'s trees all link, so no rerun reaches the ladder); M23 (the named-repository list built per call) reds R1 in (i) to (iii) on the busiest window and in (v) on the window list's reads; M17 (rung 3's index drops events without an `agentId`) reds R2, D-5604 R2 and six rows of `delegation-correlation.test.ts`; M18 (a path's events reversed) is EQUIVALENT, for the reason under the order above, and no row reds it; M19 (`claimsOf` stops reading metas) reds R2, R1's floor in (ii) and (iii), and two correlation rows; M20 (the activity index without `sourceKind`) reds R2 only; M22 (a partition memo that serves the last partition built for a list of the same length) reds R1 (iv), R2 and 21 correlation rows, but not D-5604 R2, whose own reruns over an empty list refresh that last partition between passes; M24 (an index that keeps the last row of a key) reds R2's digest only; M25 (the launch-response claim dropped) reds R2 and one correlation row; M26 (the every-repository lost windows after the named ones) survived every row until R3, which reds it; M21a, M21b and M21c (the window, listing and list freezes removed) each red D-5643 R1, and M27 (reconcile's window list back to main's inline spread) and M28 (`listingWindows: [...windows]` in reconcile's tree input, an unfrozen copy per tree, which defeats the memos keyed by the list and the list's freeze; review 634 F2) each red D-5643 R1's call row and D-5643 R2 (review 635), and no other row; M29 (a copy at the binding), M30 (a copy per tree behind a renamed binding), M31 (the call's result discarded beside a list spread from locals) and M32 (`listingWindows: windows .slice()`) each red D-5643 R2 alone, and M21c reds R2 beside R1. 23 of 24 killed, M18 equivalent (the 16 first run, M18 among them; the field-free forms of M14 and M15; M27 to M32; M14 to M16, M23 and M27 run again at `3b93187d3`, and M27, M28 and M21c against R2). Three controls: `parentOf` back to `find` reds R1 in (ii) to (iv); rung 3 back to scanning the evidence reds R1 in all four of (i) to (iv) (run before (v) was added); `creatorMayBeLost`'s lost loop back to iterating the per-project lost list with `spanOf` SURVIVES: after the first parse the loop reads only memoised data and no element field, so neither R1 nor any row sees a per-tree loop over memoised data that reads no field. That is a limit of read counting, recorded as such: timed in shape M (2,002 lost windows), it costs about 0.05 s a pass (the tip 0.22 and 0.19 s at 367 and 734 trees, the control 0.27 and 0.24 s, three runs each; a gap near the run-to-run noise that does not grow with the trees), which no bench target catches. **The bench** (reported, not a test; a throwaway script outside the tree, D-5604's bench with a tree count): 9,000 held windows of 126 names (40 sessions round robin, 50 ms each, none overlapping), one of them closed with a null repository, the lane's two lost windows, a 20,000-event slice, a measured dropped index with no keys and no marks; tree i < 76 keeps its name and, in B, C and D, its candidate, and tree i ≥ 76 is `wt-x<i>`, in no listing, with no candidate; shapes (A) unresolved, (B) a candidate per tree i < 76 that links at rung 4, (C) B under a horizon before every window, (D) B with one other-session window last, refusing every candidate as an overlap only after a full scan, (R) the restart, every repository null, the trees over 6 projects with no (project, name) pair repeated, (M) A's windows plus 2,000 lost hours on null repositories, every tree's first sighting known and recent, by i % 3 a plain tree linking at rung 3 through its own agent, an `agent-*` tree linking at rung 2, and a `wf_*` tree with a workflow activity and a meta (these events replace Bash events, so the slice stays at 20,000); the tip's runs freeze the windows, their listings, the list, the evidence and the metas as the lane does. Each run is the lane's calls in its order under a 2 GB scope, only while `free -m` showed at least 4000 available; the sha256 of every verdict, forced kind and lost flag is equal at main and at the tip in every cell. Medians in seconds at 76 / 367 / 734 trees, main (three runs at 76, one each at 367 and 734, load 10.2 to 11.8) then tip (five runs each, the 367 and 734 runs interleaved, load 8.5 to 9.9): A 0.49 / 1.05 / 2.43 and 0.22 / 0.20 / 0.20; B 0.33 / 1.13 / 2.51 and 0.43 / 0.39 / 0.39; C 1.52 / 1.97 / 2.69 and 1.01 / 1.00 / 0.96; D 0.58 / 1.50 / 2.46 and 0.42 / 0.41 / 0.43; R 0.50 / 1.36 / 3.30 and 0.18 / 0.18 / 0.20; M 0.26 / 0.42 / 0.68 and 0.20 / 0.21 / 0.21. Against the target: in every shape the median at 734 trees is at most 1.10 times the median at 367 (R, 0.18 to 0.20 s; D 1.06; the others at most 1.01; the target is 1.3); A, R and M are 0.20, 0.20 and 0.21 s at 734 trees, under 1 s; B, C and D are under 1 s too. C's 0.96 s is almost all candidate-only work: its 76 candidates each run the ladder four times over the overlap loop, the horizon `.some` and the evidence filter, about 0.76 s over A's 0.20 s at 734 trees (B's is about 0.19 s and D's about 0.23 s over A). B at 76 trees is slower at the tip than at main (0.43 against 0.33 s), the once-per-pass build that a pass with few trees does not amortise. The live mix (coordinator mail 6631; shape M at 367 trees, 85 `agent-*`/`wf_*` trees and 282 plain): main 0.58 s, tip 0.22 s (three runs each), the same sha256; with 10,000 more unmatched activity rows, 0.60 and 0.21 s (three runs each). So this bench does not reproduce the 4 to 5 s the live server measured at 367 trees on v0.0.331: the live block depends on something the bench lacks, and only a live profile of the release that carries this fix can say what remains. **The ruling**, quoted: "A third wave-3 follow-up builds the shared work once per pass, so a pass no longer scales with checkouts × listings. It also takes the review's minor items (a test that guards the lookup, protection against a changed listing, doc fixes). It needs watch.ts, so I agree that with the scrub run first. Soak starts on the release that carries it; v0.0.328 gives relief meanwhile." The operator declined the option that also bounds the flush and repair projections, so this run leaves them. **Residual, on wave 4's list:** (1) the candidate-only work, per candidate tree and O(held windows + slice): the overlap loop, the horizon `.some`, `activities.find` and `acceptedEvidence.filter`, about 10 ms per candidate per pass in shape C; (2) the bounded and link-only traversals of the census, left as they are; (3) the flush and repair projections; (4) what a restart does to verdicts, unchanged; (5) no yield in the pass; (6) out-of-type inputs (a non-string id or instant, an absent listing) that main answered lazily now throw when an index is built, which no producer can make; (7) a per-tree loop over memoised data that reads no element field, which read counting cannot see: the control that keeps `creatorMayBeLost`'s lost loop as a per-tree loop over the per-project lost list survives every row, at about 0.05 s a pass in shape M (the controls, above). **Bounds:** `delegation-correlation.ts`, with its L1 header clause updated, and, for D-5643, `watch.ts`'s window builders and reconcile only; no exported signature or type change in the module and no wire change (D-5643 adds one exported function to `watch.ts`), and no README or spec change; the module stays free of the filesystem, the clock, randomness and the store, and its only state is module-level WeakMaps keyed by the pass-wide arrays, the windows and the derived lists. Commits `9796d8381` (the rows, red at main), `05069d3ad` (the bound), `d64c3e312` (R1's freeze row reads the events' listings through their own type), `7e34549d9` (the input's docstring), `5b7bb3a91` (R3), and this entry's commit. After review 631: `7dafafe46` (R1's shape (v), its narrowed title and its two limits, F1 and F3), `6bbc92a8f` and `b88f39a38` (the docstrings on what is frozen, F5) and this entry's edits (F1 to F3, F5 and F6, and residual (7)); still tests, comments and the plan only. After review 634: `823fa0220`, with R1's header ("relies on", limit three), D-5643's extended call row (M28) and this entry's edits; tests and the plan only. After review 635: this entry's commit, with D-5643 R2 and this entry's edits; tests and the plan only.
- **D-5643** — `the-lane-freezes-what-it-hands-over` (review 628 F8, M13): the memos of D-5604 and D-5642 trust that no caller changes a window, its listings, the list, or a field an index reads after handing it over, and a push into a handed-over list gave a stale answer (M13). `watch.ts` now freezes them where the lane builds them: every window from `delegationListingWindowsOf`, `delegationLostHourWindow`, `delegationNotReadyWindowOf` and `delegationLostWindowsOf`'s retention floor is `Object.freeze`d, and so are its `before` and `after`; reconcile builds its window list in one new exported pure function among the builders, `delegationPassWindowsOf(listing, lost, notReady, pending)`, which returns `Object.freeze([...])`; and the evidence array and its `{journalId, event}` rows are frozen too, though not each row's `event` (the hook's envelope, kept in the lane's evidence across passes), and so are the point metas and their array (the lane's map values are fresh literals it never writes, so freezing them in place is safe); each pass builds those two arrays afresh (the meta rows are frozen in place and kept), no row pins their freezes, and they rest on the no-write citation below (review 631 F5). A listing window's `before` and `after` ARE the hook events' `listing` arrays (`watch.ts:9543-9544`), kept in the lane's evidence across passes, so the freeze freezes those listings in place: `git grep` finds no write to a listing, a window field or a window list in `server/src` or `server/test` (beside D-5643 R1's own writes, each made to throw, and writes to a test's own answer records, the grep finds only the ten statements of R27's compile-only block, `delegation-ingest.test.ts:1520-1538`, a function never called, because `tsc` is the test: the checkpoint's `revision`, a session's `lastObservedAt`, a cursor's `cursor`, a key of `activityIdsByNaturalKey`, the phase, a lease's `releasedAt`, an appended record's `acceptedAt`, an opened activity's `endedAt`, a changed session's `sessionId` and the change's `revision`; none runs, and none is a window, a listing, a window list or a field an index reads; review 631 F7). Row, in the rows commit `9796d8381`, `server/test/delegation-pass-windows.test.ts`, "D-5643 R1: the lane's windows, their listings and the pass's window list are frozen": a window from each builder and from the pass function, its `before`, its `after` and the returned list are each `Object.isFrozen`, and a push into the list, a write to a window and a push into a listing each throw; red at main (nothing frozen, and no pass function). Mutants M21a, M21b and M21c (each freeze removed) each red it, and M21c reds R2 below as well. After review 631 (F4), a second row in that file, "D-5643 R1: reconcile's body makes one `delegationPassWindowsOf` call, spreads none of the builders, and hands its trees `listingWindows: windows`", reads `watch.ts`'s source text, finds reconcile's body by its signature, and holds its window list to one `delegationPassWindowsOf(` call and no inline spread of the builders, and its trees' input to exactly one `listingWindows: windows` (review 634 F2), anchored on those two texts and never on where the call's arguments are built or on any neighbour; M27 (reconcile's list back to main's inline spread) and M28 (`listingWindows: [...windows]`, an unfrozen copy per tree) each red the call row and R2 below, and no other row. The text pin cannot see every way to copy a list: it stays green under a call whose result goes nowhere beside a list spread from locals (`const listing = delegationListingWindowsOf(...)`, then `const windows = [...listing, ...]`; M31), under a copy at the binding (`const windows = [...delegationPassWindowsOf(...)]`, review 635 (a); M29), under a copy per tree behind a renamed binding (`const passWindows = delegationPassWindowsOf(...)`, and `inputFor` opening with `const windows = [...passWindows];`, review 635 (b); M30), and under the regex's whitespace forms (`windows?.slice()`, `windows .slice()` (M32), and a newline before `.slice()`); `windows ?? [...]` is no copy, because `windows` is never nullish. So after review 635 the copies are guarded by behaviour: R2, in `server/test/delegation-watch.test.ts`, "D-5643 R2: one census pass hands every tree the same window list, and that list is frozen", drives one census pass of two trees (`{ demo: [OLD, RAW] }`) through that file's harness with a spy on `correlateDelegation`, which sees reconcile's call for each census tree and nothing else (the forced helpers call the module's private `correlate`), and asserts one call per tree, the same `listingWindows` array (`toBe`) in every call, and that array `Object.isFrozen`; M27, M28, M29, M30, M31, M32 and M21c each red it. Commits `53a89f152`, `3b93187d3` (the call row), `509aecdea` (the call row anchors on the call and no spread, not on where its arguments are built, so timing around reconcile's statements leaves it green) `823fa0220`, after review 634 (the row also anchors on the one `listingWindows: windows`, which sits outside the reconcile statements run 632's phase timing touches; its title narrowed to what it checks), and, after review 635, this entry's commit (R2, and the call row's message and header narrowed to the text it checks).
- **D-5644** — `unused` (run 629): issued to this follow-up; no departure took it.

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 4 (run 632, block D-5692..D-5694, taken in order; defined together after run 629 landed, the plan being claimed until then):
- **D-5692** — `a-slow-pass-says-where-its-time-went` (the operator's ruling of 2026-10-19, "Timing line + 629"): after D-5604 and D-5642 the live server still blocked its event loop for about 4-5 s once a pass, which the bench did not reproduce and the live process could not be profiled for. The ruling, the option it chose, verbatim: "Land 629 as soon as its review passes. Alongside it, a small run adds one log line to each pass, written only when a pass blocks over 1 s, giving the time spent in each phase (matching, re-runs, decisions, effects, table rewrites). Roll out both; the line shows where the rest goes. No behaviour change; the soak starts once the block is small or you accept it." **The line.** At most ONE `console.warn` per pass, `ccrc-server: delegation pass slow: key=value …`, decided once in `DelegationLane.run`'s `finally` on every path (a normal end, an early return, a throw, and a cut pass's late settle, whose line carries `cut=1`), and written only when `block` is MORE than `DELEGATION_PASS_SLOW_MS` (1000; exactly 1000 writes nothing). It is built by one exported pure formatter, `delegationPassSlowLine(fields, bound)`, in the fixed order of `DELEGATION_PASS_SLOW_KEYS`: ASCII, one space between pairs, every value a non-negative integer (`Math.round` of elapsed ms, 1/0 for a yes/no, clamped to `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, `+Infinity` the largest and any other non-finite value 0), the whole string at most `DELEGATION_PASS_SLOW_LINE_BYTES` (2,048) bytes, prefix included; a pair that would cross the bound is dropped whole, the next key is still tried, and a final `dropped=<n>` (its room reserved first) counts them. No path, session id, project or record name can enter it. Nothing else changes: no verdict, effect, journal, checkpoint, wire, health field, or the timing of any act. **The clock** is the watcher's own, `deps.monotonicMs?.() ?? performance.now()`, read through one lane method; the one new test seam, `DelegationLaneSeams.onPassPhase`, is called synchronously right after the clock is read at the start of each timed phase and part, with its key, and a row advances a virtual clock from it. **The phases**, each present iff it ran (a phase that did not run is absent, never 0), elapsed ms, waits included: in `run`, `noteSessions`, `open` (the applier), `recover` (the not-ready branch's recovery), `root`, `load`, `expire`, `owners` (`ownersOf` and every observation), `flush` (`flushPass` and `forgetRead`), `upkeep` (its check included), `prune`, `reconcile`, `total` (entry to the line) and `other` (`total` minus `run`'s top-level phases); inside `reconcile`, consecutive stretches `pre` (before `readMetas`), `readMetas`, `prep` (the snapshot to `droppedIndex`), `build` (the evidence, the attribution trees, the windows through `delegationPassWindowsOf`, the point metas), `pairs`, `forced`, `lostForced`, `policy` (`planDelegationReconciliation` up to `applyEffects`) and `effects`; inside `effects`, the parts `attempts`, `leases`, `intervals` and `absence` (each summed over the pass) and `repair` (the `repairProjection()` call, its wait on the applier queue included; the `repair-report-projection` case only sets the flag). `rewrite` is the time inside the coordination store's projection port (`project`, `insertEvents`, `eventStats`, wrapped where the applier is opened, returning and throwing exactly what the port does), summed into the pass that is running when a call returns, with `rewrites` (`project` calls) and `rewriteEvents` (rows inserted); after a cut two passes can overlap, and a rewrite then lands in the later-started pass's record, or in none once that pass has settled (review 642 F4). **`block`** is the sum of `noteSessions expire flush upkeep prune pre prep build pairs forced lostForced policy effects`. The read phases (`open recover root load owners readMetas`) are printed and never summed, so a slow read alone never writes the line; `rewrite` is printed and never summed (it overlaps its callers); `reconcile` is summed through its stretches. `pre` and the stretch from `prep` to `policy` hold no await, so their time is the lane's own; the awaited blocking phases (`expire flush upkeep prune effects`) count their writes' waits (a journal fsync, the repair's yields between chunks). **The sizes**, each an integer measured where the pass holds it and present iff that point ran: `trees`, `treesPastWait`; `win`, `winLost`, `winHeld`, `winHeldNull`; `evidence`, `activities`, `pointMetas`; `cpSessions`, `cpAttempts`, `cpLeases`, `openLeases`, `cpLineage`, `cpIntervals`, `cpCursors` (at the line); `ownerCount` (D-5693), `pendingOwners`, `staged`, `appended`; `claimants`, `launches`, `pathMap`, `droppedKeys`; `markPaths`, `markIds`, `lostProjects`, `lostPrefixes`, `lossy`, `droppedMeasured`; `ladder` (the `correlateDelegation` calls in `pairs`) and `markCalls`, `windowCalls`, `lostCalls` (calls into the three rerun helpers that pass the lane's own gates; each may run the ladder up to twice inside `delegation-correlation.ts`, so these count calls, not reruns); `vLinked`, `vRunChild`, `vUnresolved`, `vConflicting`, `vUnmeasured` (always written once `pairs` ran); `eLease`, `eRunChild`, `eUnowned`, `eAbsence`, `eRepair`, `eInterval` (once policy ran); `repairPasses`, `repairInserted`, `repairFull`, `repairWiped`, `repairHealed` (read from the report `repairProjection()` returns, never acted on; a null report or a throw leaves them absent); and `censusAge` (`pass.now` minus the census's `measuredAt`). **The ruling's words, as keys:**

  | the ruling's word | the keys |
  |---|---|
  | matching | `pairs` |
  | re-runs | `forced` + `lostForced` |
  | decisions | `policy` |
  | effects | `effects` and its parts |
  | table rewrites | `rewrite`, `repair` and `attempts` |

  **Reading the line against a live block.** A line's window is [its write time minus `total`, its write time]. A measured block inside that window and near `block` is the lane's: the largest stretch names it, and the sizes beside it say what it was over (set them against the bench's mix). Set the await-free stretches (`pre`, `prep` through `policy`) against the awaited blocking phases before calling a time CPU: a slow fsync in `flush` or a long repair in `effects` is elapsed time, not necessarily a blocked loop. A measured block that overlaps no line's window, or runs well past the line's `block`, lies outside the lane's blocking phases, not necessarily outside the lane (review 642): it is either in a read phase (`open recover root load owners readMetas`, timed but never writing the line; `open`'s boot recovery and `load`'s whole-journal read run after every restart and again after a failed load, so discard the first two passes after a restart and any window holding a "delegation evidence could not be read" warn), or outside the lane (the census sweep, `sweepDivergences`, or another 60-second lane). The reverse also holds: an awaited blocking phase (`expire flush upkeep prune effects`, and `repair` inside `effects`, which yields between sessions) holds whatever the event loop ran while it waited, another lane's block included, so only the await-free stretches and `rewrite` are certainly the lane's own time; read `repairPasses` and `repairInserted` before blaming the repair. On a `cut=1` line, and the line after it, `rewrite` may under-read and `pendingOwners` counts owners the cut left unread. Either way the next step is a ruling, not a wider line. **The rows** (`server/test/delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`, a virtual clock advanced from `onPassPhase`; R1-R7 committed red at main in `3b2223540`, strengthened in `aa97df329` after a review of the rows, R5 (iii) in `7ad1c9b96`):

  | row | what it pins |
  |---|---|
  | R1 | a full pass with 1,500 ms in `pairs` writes exactly one line: `pairs=1500`, `reconcile=1500`, and the key set is exactly the phases that ran (no `repair`, the policy's repair effect filtered by a spy; no effect part with no effect); every size equals a count measured apart from the lane's new code (spies on correlation and policy, the applier's results, the store's port calls, `delegationReport()`, the checkpoint on disk), the non-zero ones pairwise distinct where the fixture allows; further R1 rows pin the five `repair*` sizes against the report (a null report and a throw leave them absent), the mark and dropped-key sizes through the `markCap`/`evidenceCap`/`droppedPruneEvery` seams, and an unknown-repository window after a restart (`winHeldNull`, `windowCalls`) |
  | R2 | `block` exactly 1,000 writes no line (1,001 does); 1,500 ms in any read phase alone (`open recover root load owners readMetas`) writes none, and leaves `block` as it was |
  | R3 | a table over every phase and part: 1,500 ms from its hook gives 1,500 to it and to each phase enclosing it, 0 to every other that ran; 1,500 ms inside the port's `project`, from the flush and from the repair, gives 1,500 to `rewrite` and to the caller and its enclosers, and to no other |
  | R4 | `pre` 400 + `pairs` 400 + `repair` 400 writes the line, `block=1200` |
  | R5 | (i) through the lane, census strings (`zq-proj`, `agent-zqrec`, `/zqpath/x`) never reach the line, which matches `^ccrc-server: delegation pass slow:( [a-z][a-zA-Z]*=\d+)+$` over the fixed keys; (ii) the formatter at every field `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` stays within 2,048 bytes, under a small bound drops whole pairs, keeps a later short pair after a long one is dropped, counts the drops, and rounds and clamps each value; (iii) the lane's one call passes the fields alone, so the formatter's own bound holds |
  | R6 | a census that is not newer (no reconcile), 1,500 ms in `flush`: one line, `flush=1500`, no reconcile phase or size |
  | R7 | a pass cut by the budget, 1,001 ms in `noteSessions` and 1,500 ms in the flush that runs after the cut: nothing before the late settle, then exactly one line, `cut=1`, `block=2501` |

  **The mutation table**, each mutant a temporary edit of `watch.ts` run against `083e9f8d0` (M12's first arm again against `7ad1c9b96`, where R5 (iii) reds it; the absorb that followed touched none of the mutated lines), all red:

  | mutant | what it does | red |
  |---|---|---|
  | M1 | the line is removed | R1 (and every row that expects a line) |
  | M2 | `>=` in place of `>` | R2 |
  | M3 | the line is written every pass | R2, R1, R3-R6 |
  | M4 | `pairs`' time recorded under `build` | R3, R1, R2, R4 |
  | M5 | `block` drops `pre` / drops `effects` | R4, R3 / R4, R3 |
  | M6 | a record name is appended to the line | R5, R1 |
  | M7 | 0 written for every phase that did not run | R1, R6, R7 |
  | M8 | `owners` / `readMetas` counted in `block` | R2, R3 / R2, R3 |
  | M9 | `rewrite` not accumulated (adds 0 / set, not add) | R3 / R3 |
  | M10 | times in seconds | R1 (and every row that expects a line) |
  | M11 | `performance.now()` read past the deps clock | R1 (and every row that expects a line) |
  | M12 | the bound removed at the lane's call / the line cut by `slice` / the formatter's default bound removed | R5 (iii) / R5 (ii) / R5 (ii) |
  | M13 | the line written only from inside reconcile | R6, R7 |
  | M14 | a cut pass writes twice (also from the cut arm) / never | R7 / R7 |
  | M15 | the inner stretches subtracted from `reconcile` | R3, R1, R2, R4 |
  | M16 | `cpSessions` and `cpAttempts` swapped / `markPaths` and `markIds` swapped | R1 / R1 |

  The bound at the lane's call (M12's first arm) red no row as first written, because no lane pass builds a line near 2,048 bytes; R5 (iii) pins it at the source. Residuals, measured: two sizes in R1's full pass equal by coincidence (`staged`, `vLinked` and `eUnowned`; `winLost` and `vUnmeasured`), so a swap inside either pair shows only where another row separates them; `vRunChild` and `eRunChild` are 0 in every row (no wave-3 datum names a run's child), and `pendingOwners` is 0 in every row but R7's cut pass, where it is 1 (a pending owner needs a refused bucket, a failed flush, or a cut that left its listing unread; review 642 F5); `launches`, `vConflicting`, `eAbsence` and `eInterval` have no non-zero size check. The throw path and the not-ready returns are covered by construction (one `finally`) and pinned by no row: review 642 measured MX1 (no line on a throw) and MX2 (no line on the not-ready returns) surviving every row, and the coordinator waived that row for this landing (the line is a diagnostic, not a guard of the shipped bound), owing it to the next dispatched delegation run. The size counts run on every reconcile (a few linear passes over the trees, windows, pairs and effects), the line's own reads only on a slow pass. Edited only inside `DelegationLane`, `DelegationLaneSeams` and beside `DELEGATION_PASS_BUDGET_MS`, unclaimed beside the public-tree scrub's claim on `watch.ts` by its coordinator's consent. Commits: `3b2223540` (rows, red), `c18254dc1` (the line), `aa97df329` (rows strengthened), `083e9f8d0` (review: keys typed as the key list, `recover` ends after its settle bookkeeping, the waits said), `7ad1c9b96` (R5 (iii)); the absorb of #698 kept both sides of the two reconcile hunks: #698's `delegationPassWindowsOf(…)` call and its frozen evidence rows and point metas, with the `build` stretch and its size lines around them.
- **D-5693** — `the-owner-count-is-not-the-owners-phase` (run 632): the task text named the size of the pass's owner list `owners`, which is also the key of the phase that reads them; one key cannot carry both, so the size is `ownerCount`.
- **D-5694** — `unused` (run 632): issued to this follow-up; no departure took it.
- **Unused:** D-5694 was issued to this follow-up and is unused.

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 5 (run 645, block D-5832..D-5837, taken in order):
- **D-5832** — `the-slow-line-measures-cpu-and-the-loop-stall` (the operator's ruling of 2026-10-19, "Measure and cut, then soak"; the first of its three parts): the live line of D-5692 showed `flush` as the largest blocking phase on every slow pass (a mean of 3.1 s, growing by about 10 ms per appended event), `rewrite` at 0.7 to 1.8 s with two rewrites on most passes, and the repair at 0.65 to 0.9 s, but elapsed time cannot say how much of a phase was the lane's own CPU and how much the loop spent waiting on a disk. **The ruling**, the option it chose, verbatim: "A fifth wave-3 follow-up, opened now with fresh D-numbers. (1) The line also reports real CPU time and the server's stall per phase, so the split is measured, not estimated. (2) Safe cuts that change no result: stop copying the whole activity-id map for each new activity, skip the attempts write when nothing is new, and let the server serve other work between phases. (3) The repair skips its full table rewrite when the table is known current, with a full repair forced about hourly; this replaces the every-pass repair. Estimate: about 1-1.6 s less blocking a pass, and no single stall much over 1 s. The one-disk-sync-per-event rule (O5) is untouched. The soak starts on the release that carries it." This number is part (1); D-5833 to D-5836 are part (2) and part (3), and each cut is a number of its own. **Nineteen new keys**, all in `DELEGATION_PASS_SLOW_KEYS` and all non-negative integers like the rest. *Right after `other`:* `blockCpu` (the CPU summed over exactly the phases `block` sums), `stallMax`, `stallP99` (the pass-wide longest stall and its 99th percentile) and `cpuThread`. *After `censusAge`, before `dropped`:* `flushCpu`, `upkeepCpu`, `effectsCpu`, `repairCpu`, `reconcileCpu`, `ownersCpu`, `stallFlush`, `stallMid`, `stallRepair`, `repairRewrite`, `yields`, `cpActivities`, `cpMapping`, `repairSkipped` and `attemptsSkipped`. **Why that place.** The formatter fills in list order and drops a pair that would cross the 2,048-byte bound, so the keys at the END of the list drop first; the four that answer the ruling's question directly sit near the front, and the fifteen that explain them sit at the end. Presence follows the line's one rule, a key is absent when what it measures did not happen: a `*Cpu` key is present iff its phase ran, `yields` iff a turn ran (D-5835), a stall stretch key iff that stretch opened, `repairSkipped` iff the repair answered with a report (1 when the report says `skipped`, else 0), `attemptsSkipped` iff the `attempts` part ran (1 when D-5834's pre-filter left nothing and no call was made, else 0), and `cpActivities` and `cpMapping` (the checkpoint's `activities.length` and the key count of `activityIdsByNaturalKey`) only when the applier answered, beside `cpSessions`. **The CPU source and its seam.** `phase()` reads the monotonic clock, then the CPU clock, then calls `onPassPhase`, and reads both again at its end; CPU per phase is kept for every phase and written in ms. The clock is one new lane seam, `DelegationLaneSeams.cpuMicros?: () => { micros: number; thread: boolean }`; production, with no seam, uses `process.threadCpuUsage` when present (feature-detected) and `process.cpuUsage` otherwise, `micros` being user plus system and `thread` true only for the first. `threadCpuUsage` exists from Node 23.9 and the floor is 22.16, so on a 22.x server `cpuThread=0` and every `*Cpu` key is PROCESS-wide; `cpuThread=1` says main-thread. The rows ran on Node 24.14 with `threadCpuUsage` present. No file outside `watch.ts` changed for it, and `Deps` in the server's wiring is not edited. **The sampler and its seam.** The stall is measured by ONE lane-owned sampler: an unref'd `setInterval` at `DELEGATION_STALL_RESOLUTION_MS` (20), started at the lane's first pass and cleared in `close()`. At each fire it records the gap since its previous fire (in whole microseconds, at least 1) into a pass-wide histogram (`createHistogram()` from `node:perf_hooks`, named in the import on line 1) and into the running max of the stretch open at that fire. A read (`stretch()`) answers the open stretch's max, zeroes it, and opens the next WITHOUT touching the previous-fire time; `startPass()` makes a NEW histogram. Nothing is ever `reset()`: the recheck measured that `monitorEventLoopDelay`'s `reset()` zeroes its previous-fire time, so the interval spanning the first stall after a reset is never recorded (a reset, a 50 ms block and a 25 ms wait read a max of 20 ms). One sampler feeds every key, so for a pass whose sampling window no other pass overlapped, `stallMax` is at least every stretch key. An overlapped line does not keep that invariant (review 677 F2): the lane has ONE sampler with one histogram and one stretch max, so the later pass's `startPass()` replaces the histogram and its reads zero the stretch max. The pass-wide keys of the earlier pass's line can then describe the later pass's partial window, and its `stallMax` can be below a stretch it had already read. That holds for the `cut=1` line and for the `cut=0` line of a pass that overlapped it alike. No overlap isolation is claimed; the reading rule below calls both lines' stall keys partial. The seam is `DelegationLaneSeams.loopDelay?: DelegationLaneStall` (`startPass`, `stretch`, `pass`, `stop`, exported from `watch.ts`); a row replaces it with a virtual sampler, or passes the key as `undefined` to take production. The timer functions the sampler and the turns use (`setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `clearInterval`, `performance.now`) are references captured at module load, in the constants region (D-5835 says why). **Other ways to measure the longest stall in the pass window**, and why not: `monitorEventLoopDelay` reset at each boundary (loses the spanning interval, above); the same unreset, one per stretch (no per-stretch read, only a pass-wide figure, which the sampler's histogram gives); `perf_hooks.eventLoopUtilization` (a busy ratio over a window, so a 50 ms block in a 5 s window is invisible); `async_hooks` (resource lifecycles, not a turn's lateness); a `setImmediate` ping (runs in the check phase, before the next timers phase, so a read after it misses the stretch just ended: mutant M19 reads 20 where 50 was made); the inspector or a profile (a sampled profile of the whole process, heavy, and it answers where, not how long the loop was blocked); an external probe (a thread or process the server does not have). **Per stretch.** "Per phase" is honestly "per stretch between the loop turns D-5835 adds", because the sampler records a stall only when its timer fires AFTER the stretch ends. One stretch opens at flush's start (a discarding read just before `phase('flush')`, so a row's `onPassPhase('flush')` stall lands in it). No turn precedes that discarding read, so an overdue sampler fire from the phase before flush (a synchronous tail of owners or expire) can land after it and be charged to `stallFlush`: a `stallFlush` may hold up to one stall from the phase before flush (review 705 F2; documented, not changed, because a turn would change timing). `stallFlush`: flush, up to the read after the turn after flush. `stallMid`: upkeep through effects, up to the read after the turn before the repair. `stallRepair`: the repair, up to the line. A pass that ends early charges its open stretch at the line, and a stretch that never opened is absent. `stallMax` and `stallP99` are read at the line from the pass-wide histogram, which opened at `startTiming`, so a stall before flush (open through owners) is in those two only. **The turn before each read.** Every read but that opening discard follows a timers-phase turn: a `setImmediate` runs in the check phase, before the next timers phase, so a read after one would miss the stretch just ended. The three turns of D-5835 each end in one; at the line, `run`'s `finally` computes `block` and `total` and clears `passTiming` FIRST (so the wait lands in no time key and no other work's rewrite lands in the record after `total` is read), then takes one more turn through the same `loopTurn` seam, at least the resolution long, then reads the open stretch and the pass-wide figures. **The worker's ruling on that wait:** it is taken ONLY WHEN THE LINE WILL BE WRITTEN (`block` over the threshold; `block` cannot change during the wait). An unwritten line reads no stall, so a fast pass pays no extra turn. If the ruling is wrong, reverting it costs one 20 ms turn per pass. A rejected turn never throws out of `run`. **The line's wait is a second budget window.** The 45 s pass budget is a timer, and if it fires during the line's wait, a pass whose work has finished is reported cut (the warn line and `cut=1`). The wait also holds the pass's settle, and so `sweeping`, for one extra 20 ms, on a slow pass only. **The trigger is unchanged**: the line is written iff `block > DELEGATION_PASS_SLOW_MS`, as D-5692 ruled, and the new keys ride on it; a stall alone, however long, writes nothing. Whether it should is not ruled here. **The bound, as measured.** The task text counted 83 keys at main and 2,206 bytes with every value `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, and 2,735 bytes unbounded over the 102 keys the list now holds. Measured at the tip (the bound row): at realistic values (7 digits each) the whole line is 1,817 bytes and drops nothing; at `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` for every key it is 2,042 bytes, 77 keys kept (78 pairs with `dropped`) and `dropped=25`, the drop a suffix of the list (`eUnowned eAbsence eRepair eInterval repairPasses repairInserted repairFull repairWiped repairHealed censusAge` and then all fifteen new tail keys), so `blockCpu`, `stallMax`, `stallP99` and `cpuThread` stay, and ten old keys that were already at risk at main drop too. The line never exceeds 2,048 bytes. A sample, from D-5832 R1's armed pass on the virtual clocks: `... censusAge=0 flushCpu=101 upkeepCpu=7 effectsCpu=84 repairCpu=43 reconcileCpu=298 ownersCpu=59 stallFlush=211 stallMid=223 stallRepair=227 repairRewrite=700 yields=0 cpActivities=4 cpMapping=4 repairSkipped=0 attemptsSkipped=0`, after `block=1701 cut=0 total=1701 other=0 blockCpu=380 stallMax=307 stallP99=293 cpuThread=1`. **The projection's two callers apart.** `rewrite` stays; `repairRewrite` is the time of every port call (`rewritten`) charged while the `repair` part is open, `eventStats` included, so `rewrite - repairRewrite` is every other caller's port time, flush's included (upkeep's and the attempts part's change projections, a lease's projection), not flush's share alone (review 705 F3). Its presence mirrors `rewrite`'s (a worker's ruling): present iff a port call was charged while the repair part was open. A repair that skipped (D-5836) carries it too, small, beside `repairSkipped=1`, because the short path's one `eventStats` read goes through the same timed port; it is absent only when the repair part made no port call (a null report from a repair that never reached the port, or a throw before any call). **The reading rule** (this updates D-5692's, which reads the line's time as elapsed time): *A phase's `*Cpu` is main-thread CPU (`cpuThread=1`) or process CPU (`cpuThread=0`) while the phase was open. That includes other work run at its awaits and yields: other routes, other sweeps, applier work queued ahead, and an overlapping cut pass. Only `pre` and `prep` through `policy` are certainly the lane's own. `repairRewrite` likewise may include other applier work run while the repair waited on the queue. A stretch key holds a stall when the sampler fired inside its window. A stall that spans a read is charged to the stretch open when the sampler's next fire lands. A `stallFlush` may hold a stall from the phase before flush (review 705 F2). Every stall value includes up to the 20 ms resolution. After a cut, two passes overlap, and each one's reads cut the other's windows. So the stall keys of a `cut=1` line are partial, and so are those of any pass that overlapped it, whose line carries `cut=0`. A reader recognises the second kind by a `cut=1` line in the same window. A late `cut=1` line's pass-wide stall figures (`stallMax`, `stallP99`) can be the newer pass's, and its read zeroes the newer pass's open stretch (review 705 F4; documented, not changed), and under overlap the cut pass's in-flight repair port time lands in the newer pass's record, counted as `repairRewrite` only if the newer pass has its own repair part open (review 716 G4). The `DelegationLaneStall` docstring now says the sampler is shared across overlapping passes (`8bdf59c46`). Still documented, not changed. `rewrite` now includes the delta's time (D-6167), and `encodes` and `cpBytes` cover the pass's window.* **CPU while open, and turn 3** (review 677 F3): turn 3 (D-5835) sits inside `effects` and `reconcile`. Its elapsed time leaves those two and `block` for `yields`, but its CPU stays in `effectsCpu`, `reconcileCpu` and `blockCpu`, because a `*Cpu` key is the CPU while the phase is open. So a paired elapsed key and `*Cpu` key need not cover identical spans. `LANE_CPU_KEYS`'s docstring says "while open", not "own" (`db52ea0a2`), and D-5835 R5 pins the reading. **The rows** (`server/test/delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`; `world()` installs a virtual CPU clock and a virtual sampler that read 0 unless a row sets them, set from `onPassPhase`, and `rig()` merges those defaults back under the seams it replaces; committed red at main in `77659ed6b`, strengthened in `e0a579eb6` after a review of the rows):

  | row | what it pins | at main |
  |---|---|---|
  | R1 | every one of the new `*Cpu`, stall, `repairRewrite`, `yields`, `cpActivities`, `cpMapping` and `cpuThread` keys equals what the virtual clocks were set to (distinct primes per phase, `effectsCpu` carrying the repair's and `reconcileCpu` the enclosed phases'); `blockCpu` is the sum over exactly `block`'s phases; the sizes equal the checkpoint's own counts | red (`blockCpu` absent) |
  | R2 | the trigger is unchanged: `block` 1,000 with a virtual stall of 2,000 in every stretch and the pass-wide histogram writes no line, 1,001 writes one | green by design; M2 reds it |
  | R3 | `repairRewrite` holds only the port time charged while `repair` is open: 1,500 ms in flush's own `project` and 700 ms in the repair's give `rewrite` 2,200 and `repairRewrite` 700; presence pinned present in a drifted repair and absent in the null-report and throw rows | red (key absent) |
  | R4 | owed from review 642 (F6, MX1 and MX2): a pass that throws out of `run`, and each not-ready return (a failed applier in its retry interval, recovery running, a recovery that leaves it failed, no applier), with `block` over the threshold, still writes its one line. Fix round 1 (review 677 F6, `27b79003a`) adds the two R109 cut-before-owners exits. One cut lands while the projects root resolves (a held `realpath`) and takes the exit after `root`. The other lands while the evidence loads (a held `visitRetainedRecords`) and takes the exit after `load`. With `noteSessions` 1,001 each keeps the cut contract: nothing is written when the sweep returns at the cut, then exactly one line at the late settle, `cut=1`, with no `reconcile`. Reachability is read from the phases announced and `passes`: the rows prove each exit by the phases announced and the one line. They carried a `notReady` assertion too, but a fresh lane starts not ready, so it could not fail, and fix round 2 dropped it (review 705 F11, whose mutant MR109-vacuous, both exits' `this.notReady = true` removed, left every row green). `sweep` also sets `notReady` for a cut before the owners, so these rows cannot tell `run`'s own assignment from `sweep`'s; they pin the line | green by design; MX1 and MX2 red it, and MX2 reds each R109 row on its own line count (`one line, at the late settle: expected [] to have a length of 1 but got +0`) |
  | R5 | the real loop, with the production sampler and turns: a 50 ms synchronous stall made inside the store's `projectDelegationCheckpoint` during the repair (the full path, reached by advancing the forced-full clock past `DELEGATION_REPAIR_FULL_MS` with the counts equal, so no replay's I/O gives the sampler a turn inside it) reads `stallRepair` of at least 50 with `0 < stallP99 <= stallMax`; one made during flush's `project` reads `stallFlush` of at least 50 | red (`stallRepair`, `stallFlush` absent) |
  | R6 | the production CPU source (review 677 F5, `844dfbf97`): (i) with `cpuMicros` unseamed, `cpuThread` equals the feature detection (`typeof process.threadCpuUsage === 'function'`) and, when `process.threadCpuUsage` is present, the row spies it with call-through and asserts at least two real calls (review 705 F7, `9f96c4a6a`); (ii) with `process.threadCpuUsage` deleted for the pass (its exact descriptor restored in a `finally`, then checked), the real `process.cpuUsage` fallback runs (a call-through spy counts its calls) and the line reads `cpuThread=0`. Measured on Node 24.14.1, where `threadCpuUsage` is an own, writable and configurable property of `process` and the detection runs on every call; on a runtime without it, (ii)'s delete is a no-op and the fallback runs anyway | green by design at `bdc3baebb` (red at main: the keys are absent); MCPUthread reds (ii), and on a runtime without `threadCpuUsage` (i) too; MCPUsrc reds both (i) and (ii), (i) since fix round 2 (`9f96c4a6a`), and both titles now say so (`9e2e45fee`; review 716 G5) |

  **Residuals.** `cpActivities` and `cpMapping` are equal by the checkpoint's invariant (every activity has one mapped natural key), so a swap of the two is shown by no row. A stall value includes up to the 20 ms resolution; an idle stretch reads about 20. The three turns cost about 20 ms each, about 60 ms of wall time per ready pass and in every test that runs a pass; a test world's `loopTurn` seam is the lever if that budget is tight. Each unclosed lane in another test file leaves one unref'd 20 ms interval. Review 677 measured the delegation files closing their worlds in `afterEach`, and one such lane in the suite, `fleet-health.test.ts`'s; that is that review's reading, not a universal claim. A pass that runs after `close()` restarts the sampler, because `startPass` starts the timer whenever none runs. No production code calls `close()` (`closeDelegation` has no caller in `server/src`, and shutdown does not reach it), so in production the sampler's unref'd 20 ms interval lives for the process: harmless, unref'd, one per lane (review 705 F8). The restart-after-close nit is therefore test-only, deferred to wave 4 (review 677 F13). Since fix round 3 the code says so too: the `closeDelegation` and `DelegationLane.close` docstrings no longer say a clean shutdown calls them, and the two census comments now say that only tests call `close()` (review 716 G3, `9e2e45fee`, `8bdf59c46`). A `loopTurn` seam that rejects ends the pass (only the line's own wait is guarded); production's cannot.
