- **D-5833** — `the-fold-allocates-an-id-in-place` (part (2) of the ruling; the first cut): the fold copied the whole activity-id mapping for every activity it opened (`openFromCandidate` through `restoreOrAllocateActivityId`, a wrapper checkpoint and a spread), which is O(mapping) synchronous work per open, estimated at 18 to 34 ms an open at 16 to 22 thousand keys. **The change.** `delegation-model.ts` gains `allocateDelegationActivityIdInto(mapping, key, identityJournalId): string`: the same mapping-first own-property read (`Object.hasOwn`), the same `identity-journal-id-required` refusal and the same `deriveDelegationActivityId` (with its `bad-identity-journal-id` refusal) as before, then `mapping[encoded] = id` and the id returned; a refusal writes nothing. `openFromCandidate` calls it on the builder's own `this.mapping`, with no wrapper and no re-copy. **`restoreOrAllocateActivityId` is re-implemented over it**, with its signature and results unchanged, so the mapping-first rule exists once and the O4 rows cover the code the fold runs: it copies the mapping once, allocates into the copy, and returns the SAME checkpoint object on a hit (pinned with `toBe` in the O4 rows) or a new checkpoint carrying the copy. It tells a hit without a second mapping-first read by the copy's own-key count: a hit writes nothing and a miss adds exactly one key, so an unchanged count is a hit. That is sound because an encoded key never names an inherited property, and a decoded mapping holds no `undefined` value (an own `undefined` would read as a miss, be overwritten in the copy, and then count as a hit; the docstring says so). **After this change its only callers are the O4 rows** (`git grep` finds the definition in `delegation-model.ts`, the rows in `delegation-model.test.ts`, and the new fold file's spy asserting it is called zero times); production has none, and the count is O(n), acceptable for that. **Why in place is safe.** A builder's mapping is private to the builder. The reads of it (`foldEvidence`'s mapping-first read, `openFromMeta`, the workflow-worker open's own key and run key, and the candidate sweep) are each a read of an own property inside one synchronous builder method, and none stores the object (the values are strings). Its assignments are `adoptRows()` (a copy of the checkpoint's map), `clone()` (a copy), and the old re-copy in `openFromCandidate`, now removed. `build()` passes `sortedMap(this.mapping)`, which is `Object.fromEntries` of the sorted entries, a fresh object, so a built checkpoint never aliases a builder's map; the remaining grep hits are comments. So no reference to a builder's mapping object leaves the builder. A builder is made by `from(cp)` (`adoptRows` copies), by `clone()` (copies), or by recovery's own `from(this.decoded)` (a copy), and `commitLocked` folds into `next = this.builder.clone()`; the meta doors probe on `this.builder.clone()`. `this.builder = next` happens only after the durable replacement, and recovery's builder, handed to the applier, is held by nothing else. So an in-place write only touches a `next`, a probe clone or a recovery builder, and a refused or failed `commitLocked` discards it while the live builder is untouched. **O4 holds**: the same id is derived the same way, and nothing re-derives or drops one; the only new write is the one `mapping[encoded]` that the old copy-then-spread also made (an encoded key is length-prefixed and starts with a digit, so assignment and the old computed-key literal differ for no key, `__proto__` included). **O1 holds**: the id lives only in `next` (or the recovery builder) until the checkpoint holding it is durably renamed; nothing returns, inserts, logs or reports it earlier. **The rows.** D-5833 R3 in `delegation-model.test.ts` (five rows): a key already in the mapping returns its mapped id and leaves the mapping unchanged, with a different `identityJournalId` and with null; an unmapped key allocates from the accepted id, writes `mapping[encoded]` and a second call is a hit; an unmapped key with a null id throws `identity-journal-id-required` and writes nothing; a malformed id throws `bad-identity-journal-id` and writes nothing; an inherited name is never a mapping. Red at main: `allocateDelegationActivityIdInto is exported by delegation-model.ts`. D-5833 R1 (parity) and R2 (no copy per open) are in a file of their own, `server/test/delegation-fold-alloc.test.ts`. **The worker's ruling on where they live:** they were first written in the recovery file (`9c0c9d74c` carried R1 and R2 there, until `628a1024c` moved them), but that file's fixtures run in `describe` bodies, so under M21 it fails at collection and R1 was never measured catching M21 as a row; a file of its own makes the mutant's kill a row's, at the cost of one more test file. R1: a fixed ten-event journal (opens by `SubagentStart`, a bare tool event opened later from its meta, an async launch and a completed one, a workflow run with two workers, a stop, a second session) folded through the builder and built at revision 1 gives 8 activities and 8 mappings, with the SHA-256 of the canonical bytes pinned at `c777160bc4b6...0f81` and that of the activity ids in checkpoint order at `32d8c411a689...6d26d68`, both taken at main, green at main by design. R2: 1,000 opening records folded into a builder holding 40,000 mappings call `restoreOrAllocateActivityId` zero times and `allocateDelegationActivityIdInto` 1,000 times (counting call-through spies on the model namespace, which clear their own call log: a bare `vi.spyOn` retains each result, each result of the old function carries its own 40,000-key copy, and that exhausted the worker heap), the builder's mapping keeps its object identity and ends at 41,000 keys. At main it takes 50 to 68 s and reds on `expected 1000 to be +0`; at the tip about 1 s. **The mutation table**, run against the tip:

  | mutant | what it does | result |
  |---|---|---|
  | M5 | `openFromCandidate` back to the wrapper and spread | R2 red (`expected 1000 to be +0`); R1 stays green, as a parity guard |
  | M6 | the in-place function derives the id without reading the mapping first | R3 (two rows) and the O4 rows (`identity-journal-id-required`; a natural key minting a second activity), four red in all |
  | M21 | the in-place function returns the derived id without writing `mapping[encoded]` | R1 (`expected 'waiting-for-evidence' to be 'opened'`) and R2 (41,000 keys expected, 40,000 found) as rows in the fold file, R3 (two rows), and the O4 rows in `delegation-model.test.ts` through the re-implemented `restoreOrAllocateActivityId` |

  Edited in `delegation-model.ts` (the function, the re-implementation and the header) and `delegation-checkpoint.ts` (the fold: `openFromCandidate` and its import). Commits: `9c0c9d74c` (R3 and the D-5836 rows, red), `c046957df` (the code), `628a1024c` (R1 and R2 moved to their own file; the model header and the hit docstring).
- **D-5834** — `no-attempts-write-when-nothing-is-new` (part (2) of the ruling): `fileAttempts` called `applyCheckpointChange` on every pass even when every attempt was already filed, and that change builds twice, `structuredClone`s once, encodes twice and writes nothing. **The change.** Before the call, `fileAttempts` drops the attempts whose `attemptId` is already in `durable.state().checkpoint.attempts`, and the duplicates within the list (the first kept, as `add` does); if nothing is left it makes no call and returns the same set it returned before. The `add` closure and its one-at-a-time fallback stay as they are for what is left, because the state can move between the read and the queued change. It sets the `attemptsSkipped` size (1 when no call was made, 0 when one was; absent when the `attempts` part did not run). A pass whose attempts part did not run carries no `attemptsSkipped`, pinned since fix round 2 in D-5836 R1's armed pass (its `fileAttempts` is not called and its `attempts` phase is not announced) (review 705 F9, `9f96c4a6a`). `fileAttempts` takes `pass` as a new first parameter (private; its one caller is `applyEffects`). **No durable outcome changes**: `applyCheckpointChange`'s own `have` filter and its no-op compare (`delegation-checkpoint.ts`, the equal-bytes check before the write) already made those calls write nothing, so the removed call could only have produced the same checkpoint, revision and bytes. **The rows** (`delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`, `77659ed6b` and `e0a579eb6`): (a) every attempt already on file (the pass that files it has its `createLease` rejected once, and the next pass re-plans the same request): zero `applyCheckpointChange` calls from `fileAttempts`, the same attempts returned, the lease request still reaching `createLease`, `attemptsSkipped=1`, and the checkpoint's revision, canonical bytes, file bytes and ids on file equal to what they were before the pass; red at main (`fileAttempts made no change call: expected 1 to be +0`). (b) one new attempt beside a filed one: exactly one call, and the attempt is filed, `attemptsSkipped=0`; green at main in its call and filing assertions, red there only on `attemptsSkipped` (the key is absent). **Parity against the review base** (fix round 1, review 677 F4, `72bee5c12`). Both rows also pin, at the attempts boundary, the set `fileAttempts` returns (a call-through spy) and the revision. They pin the SHA-256 of the canonical checkpoint and of the file bytes too, at the part's start (the `attempts` announcement) and its end (the `leases` announcement). Each value is a constant computed by the same fixture with production at the review base `ee03056ab`, the same rows run in a plain `git archive` export of that commit (`server`, `shared` and `agent`) with this file copied in, and a temporary probe there recorded the values before the candidate-only assertions, which fail at the base (the probe was never committed). No tracked file was overlaid. The values were identical over two base runs and two runs at the tip, because the fixture's clock is mocked, its ids are content hashes, and its temporary home does not enter the checkpoint. (a) stays at revision 3 with one id; (b) goes from revision 3 to 4 with the two ids. At the base only the candidate-only assertions fail: (a)'s call count and (b)'s `attemptsSkipped`. (b) no longer argues its bytes from (a)'s. **The mutation table**, run against the tip:

  | mutant | what it does | result |
  |---|---|---|
  | M7 | the pre-filter removed | (a) red (`expected 1 to be +0`); re-measured in fix round 1, unchanged |
  | Mcorrupt | the attempts filed with one field altered (`detail`), the same ids and the same calls | (a) and (b), on the base-computed checkpoint hashes; it survives the rows as they were before fix round 1 |
  | M8 | the pre-filter drops every attempt | the row with one new attempt (b) reds it: its one call and its filing assertions fail. (a) and D-5692 R1 and R3's lease rows red too, through their fixtures' sanity checks (no lease request is ever made) |
  | MattSkip | `attemptsSkipped` sized 0 on every pass before the attempts check | red: D-5836 R1 (`attemptsSkipped is absent: the attempts part did not run`) (fix round 2, review 705 F9) |

  Edited in `watch.ts`, `class DelegationLane`. Commits: `77659ed6b`, `e0a579eb6` (rows), `654ee950d` (the code), `72bee5c12` (the base-computed parity, fix round 1), `9f96c4a6a` (the absent `attemptsSkipped`, fix round 2).
- **D-5835** — `the-lane-yields-to-the-loop-between-its-phases` (part (2) of the ruling): the pass's phases were joined by microtasks only (the applier's queue is `promise.then`, and its `edge()` awaits an undefined seam), so once flush's last fsync returned nothing gave the loop a macrotask turn until the repair's `project()` committed. **Three turns, each followed AT ONCE by an abort check that ends the pass.** (1) After flush: after the existing check, the turn, then `if (pass.signal.aborted) return;` before upkeep's phase starts. (2) Before reconcile: after `endPrune` and before the existing check, which then follows the turn at once. (3) Before the repair: in `applyEffects`, when `repair` is set, before the existing guard `if (repair && !pass.signal.aborted)`, which is the check that follows it; this turn is outside the `repair` part. No turn sits between an abort check and the work that check guards: a cut pass keeps running after `sweep`'s race, and `reconcile` writes `reconciledAt` and the lane's memory before its first check, so a cut that lands during a turn must end the pass there. Each turn sits outside every applier call, never inside `commitLocked` and never inside the applier's queue, so the O5 order, the queue's serialisation and every `durable.state()` read are unchanged; nothing yields inside `delegation-checkpoint.ts`. **The turn's form.** A `setTimeout` of `DELEGATION_LOOP_TURN_MS` (the sampler's resolution, derived, not respelled), awaited, through the lane seam `loopTurn?: () => Promise<void>` so that a row can run a pass with the turns off (D-5835 R2) or fire a cut inside one (R3). It is a `setTimeout`, not a `setImmediate`, because a `setImmediate` runs in the check phase, BEFORE the next timers phase, so the stall read after it (D-5832) would miss the stretch just ended; a timer fires only in a timers phase, so the sampler's own late fire, if the stretch stalled the loop, has been delivered by the time the turn returns. The reference is CAPTURED AT MODULE LOAD, in the constants region, so that `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ['setTimeout'] })` does not replace it (vitest's fakes also patch `node:timers/promises`, so that module is no escape). That is why the rows that fake `setTimeout` stay green unchanged: D-5692 R7, `delegation-watch.test.ts`'s whole uncut pass under faked `setTimeout` (never advanced) and its cut helpers (about :3897-3913, :4052-4064, :4155-4166, :4304-4318, :4374-4386 and :4496-4518 as cited at the base); each ran green at the tip, and no test world needed a seam. **The `yields` timing.** `turn(pass, part)` reads the clock, calls `onPassPhase('yields')`, awaits the turn and adds the elapsed ms to the part key `yields`, summed over the pass; turn 3's share is also kept apart, and `passLine` subtracts it from `effects` and from `reconcile`, the two phases whose elapsed time holds it. `block` then excludes it, since it sums `effects` (nothing is subtracted from `block` by hand), and turns 1 and 2 are in no phase and never were. `other` is `total` minus the top-level phases (after that subtraction) minus ALL of `yields`, so taking turn 3 out of `reconcile` does not move it into `total`'s remainder. `total` keeps the turns: it is the pass's span. **Without the subtraction a turn's wait would be charged to its phase**, and `block` would count the loop's own free time as blocking (M20). **The one result a turn can change.** The pass budget is a `setTimeout`, and it can fire during a turn, so a pass near its 45 s budget may be cut at an existing check where the old chain ran on. That is the cut path the lane already has; no verdict, effect, journal or checkpoint changes otherwise (R2). **The rows** (`delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`):

  | row | what it pins | at main |
  |---|---|---|
  | R1 | each turn, pinned by a real `setImmediate` queued after the last real I/O before its boundary, with no faked timer: (i) one queued inside the store's `projectDelegationCheckpoint` while `flush` is the last phase has run by `onPassPhase('upkeep')`; (ii) one queued at the start of `prune`, in a pass whose prune and upkeep write nothing, has run by `onPassPhase('reconcile')`; (iii) one queued at `onPassPhase('effects')`, in a pass whose only effect is `repair-report-projection`, has run when `repairProjection` is entered | red |
  | R2 | parity: the same lease-free fixture pass (a lease takes `randomUUID`, which leaks into the hashes), with the turns and with them seamed off, leaves identical checkpoint bytes, revision, journal, `delegation_*` rows and effects | green by design; M22 reds it |
  | R3 | a cut fired during each turn runs nothing after it: no `upkeep` (turn 1), no `reconcile` with `reconciledAt` unchanged (turn 2), no `repairProjection` (turn 3) | red (no turns) |
  | R4 | 1,500 ms in the turn after flush, and in the turn before the repair, show in `yields` and in `total`, and in no other time key; `block`, `other`, `effects` and `reconcile` exclude them | red |
  | R5 | (fix round 1, review 677 F3, `844dfbf97`) CPU while open: 61 ms of virtual CPU and 97 ms of elapsed time made inside turn 3 alone. `effectsCpu`, `reconcileCpu` and `blockCpu` include the 61, while `effects`, `reconcile` and `block` exclude the 97, which is in `yields` and `total` | green by design at `bdc3baebb` (red at main: no turns, no CPU keys); MturnCpu reds it |

  **The mutation table**, run against the tip:

  | mutant | what it does | red |
  |---|---|---|
  | M9 | each turn removed, one at a time | R1 (i), (ii), (iii): each its own arm (and R3, R4 and the timing rows that count turns) |
  | M15 | a turn moved after its abort check, one at a time | R3, exactly one row each: turn 1, 2 and 3 |
  | M19 | the turns made a bare `setImmediate` | D-5832 R5, the flush arm only |
  | M20 | turn 3's time left in its phase and in `block` | R4, the before-repair arm and both; the after-flush arm does not red it, correctly |
  | MturnCpu | turn 3's CPU subtracted from `effects` and `reconcile`'s CPU (the alternative the coordinator rejected) | R5 (`effectsCpu holds the CPU spent in turn 3 ...: expected +0 to be 61`) |
  | M22 | the check after the after-flush turn ends the pass unconditionally | R2 (`the fixture planned at least once: expected 0 to be greater than 0`), and every row that needs the pass to continue |

  Edited in `watch.ts`, `class DelegationLane` and the constants region. Commits: `77659ed6b`, `e0a579eb6` (rows), `654ee950d` (the code), `844dfbf97` (R5, fix round 1).
- **D-5836** — `the-per-pass-repair-skips-the-rewrite-when-the-projection-is-known-current` (part (3) of the ruling): the policy plans `repair-report-projection` on every ready pass, and `repairDelegationProjection` began with an unconditional `projection.project(checkpoint)` plus `eventStats()`, so the repair was a second full synchronous rewrite of five tables on every pass (0.65 to 0.9 s on the live line, `repairPasses=0` on every pass). **The `perPass` option.** The applier's existing method takes an option, `repairProjection({ perPass: true })`; it is not a port method. The lane's `applyEffects` is its only caller with `perPass: true`, and it passes the lane's own monotonic clock (`this.mono()`) as the new optional dep. EVERY other repair still takes the full path, never the short one: a bare `repairProjection()` (the coordinator-intent route, and every test), recovery's `repairLocked`, and `projectLocked`'s stale path. Since fix round 2 that full path, the shared `repairDelegationProjection`, also heals a max the journal holds no evidence at (a full replay, and for a foreign row the wipe; see Max-only drift below), so the bare/intent repair, recovery's `repairLocked` and the stale path now do that too, where they used to report success without reading the journal when only the max differed. That change is the heal review 705 F1 asked for, and `coordinator-intent-route.test.ts`'s rows are unchanged (64 of 64 green, that file not edited) (review 716 G1). **The policy still plans the repair on every ready pass and the lane still calls it every pass**; what changed is what the call does, said in `delegation-policy.ts`'s header comment and `repairProjection`'s docstring. **The short path's exact condition**, taken at the top of `repairLocked`'s existing `try` only when ALL of these hold: `perPass` is true; this is not the lane's first per-pass repair since `open` or a `recover()` (`first = perPass && !perPassSeen`, the flag set at the top of every per-pass call and cleared at the start of `recover()`, so recovery's own repair never counts toward it and the first one is always full); `!projectionStale`; `drifted.size === 0`; `projectedRevision === current.checkpoint.revision`; the forced full is not due (below); and `projection.eventStats()`, read inside that `try` so that a throw marks the projection stale and returns null as before, equals the evidence index: `count === evidence.total` and `maxSequence === evidence.last` (null on both sides when empty; `DelegationEvidenceIndex` gains `get last(): number | null`). That condition implies the full path's own nothing-to-read test, so the short path is never looser than the full path's event branch. The full path's line that begins `projection, checkpoint: this.current.checkpoint, journal: () => readDelegationJournalRecords(this.port, this.journalPath), ` stays on its own line after the `repairDelegationProjection({` line, because the wave-3 integration test anchors a regex at its start. **Where `projectedRevision: number | null` is set and cleared.** Set to the checkpoint's revision after a successful `projectLocked` (the project and the events insert with no conflict, inside the `project` edge) and after a successful full `repairLocked`. Cleared wherever `projectionStale` is set (`repairLocked`'s catch and both sites in `projectLocked`), and at the START of every `recover()` (the boot open and any later one), before its `repairLocked`. **The forced full and its clock.** The full path runs at least once per `DELEGATION_REPAIR_FULL_MS` (exported, `60 * 60_000`); every successful full repair restarts the clock, and so does `recover()`. `delegation-checkpoint.ts` reads no clock of its own (`delegation-recovery.test.ts` forbids `Date.now` and `performance.now` in it and pins its imports), so `DelegationDurabilityDeps` gains an OPTIONAL `monotonicMs?: () => number`, documented as the forced-full clock only; when it is absent (every other opener) the forced full is measured on `deps.now`, and `now`'s docstring says so. The clock read is `monotonicMs?.() ?? now()`. **The report.** The short path runs no `project()` and no replay and returns a full `DelegationProjectionRepairReport`: every count 0, `fullReplay` and `wipedAll` false, `resumedAfter` the maximum sequence SQLite reports (0 when null, as the full path's `resume`), and `skipped: true`. Every full repair carries `skipped: false`; a reader tells the two apart by `skipped`, never by the zeros, and the line's `repairSkipped` is that field (D-5832). **The residual.** In a running server, out-of-band damage to the five non-event delegation tables, made other than through the applier, now heals at the next durable application that projects (its own full `project()`) or at the next forced full repair (within about an hour), whichever comes first (widened by D-6166). It no longer heals at the next pass. A run of passes that write nothing leaves it until the forced full. Event-count or max drift is still caught on every pass, by the `eventStats` compare. Event-content drift with an equal count and max was caught by neither path before, and still is not. A coord.db lost or rolled back across a restart is still repaired by recovery's full repair. **Max-only drift (fix round 2, review 705 F1).** Before, the full path decided its replay from the count alone, so a SQLite max the journal holds no evidence at, with an equal count, was read as current: nothing was read or wiped, the report said success, and every later per-pass repair ran full for good (the short path's max compare could never agree), so the bad row never healed. Now `repairDelegationProjection` treats it as a count disagreement, with recovery's counts when `countAtOrBelow(max - 1) === countAtOrBelow(max)` (sequences are increasing integers, so no evidence sits at `max`), and without them when the counting pass sees no evidence record at the max, so it goes to the one full pass, and the existing ladder heals it: a moved row (same `eventId`, another sequence) is a conflict and its session is healed; a foreign row survives the replay as an extra row and the count check wipes and replays. Then the per-pass short path returns. The change is in the shared full path, so it reaches every full repair (the bare/intent repair, recovery's `repairLocked` and the stale path), not only the lane's (review 716 G1). **Why M14 has no row.** `projectionStale`, the clear of `projectedRevision` and `drifted.size === 0` back each other up: `drifted` is filled only together with setting `projectionStale`, and the one clear of `projectionStale` (a successful full repair) sets `projectedRevision` again. So `projectionStale === true` implies `projectedRevision === null`, which never equals a revision; each alone is an equivalent mutant (M14: the stale term alone, the three clears alone, `drifted.size === 0` alone), measured surviving all 15 applier rows. **M16**: the clear at the start of `recover()` is likewise equivalent: recovery's own full repair sets `projectedRevision` on every path that returns ready, and `requireReady` refuses every repair on every other path (and a recovery with no projection makes every repair return null before the short path). It stays as a backstop, with the first-per-pass rule reset in the same place. **The further equivalent backstop the review found**: the clock restart at `recover()` is the same kind, because recovery's own full repair restarts it again moments later (after the file reads and any replay), so deleting the first restart changes no outcome a row can see (review 705 F10); the two recover-clock rows pin the behaviour, and no row can red the restart at `recover()` alone. **The rows.** D-5836 R2 (`delegation-recovery.test.ts`, at the applier, with a controllable projection port and a virtual `monotonicMs`; `9c0c9d74c` and `9b9009d7f`): each case runs a `lane()` and a `settle()` (the first per-pass repair full, the second the control, short) and then perturbs; its control reds every case at main (`a short per-pass repair makes no port project call: expected true to be false`). Cases: `projectionStale` with no revision change (a per-pass repair whose port `project` throws once returns null and marks the projection stale, the revision does not move, and the next, port healthy, is full, then short); a failed application's projection, and a conflicting application (a planted `SessionEnd` row on the next sequence; the journal wins); `eventStats` count disagreeing; max disagreeing with an equal count (the full path heals it by the conflict heal, `healedSessions` 1 and no wipe, SQLite back to the journal's `(2, 2)`, then short; review 705 F1); the lane's first per-pass repair after `open` and after a `recover()`; a `recover()` after a fault at `journal-write` 'before'; the forced full on the virtual clock (short at the interval less 1,000 ms, full at the interval plus 1,000 ms, short right after, and the same again counted from the forced full); two cases for `recover()` restarting the clock; out-of-band deletion of `delegation_activities` rows on the real store (the pass before the forced full stays short and the table stays empty, the residual above; the forced full restores it); after the first per-pass repair, a bare `repairProjection()` after an out-of-band delete of `delegation_sessions`, which restores it; `eventStats` throwing on the per-pass call, which returns null and marks the projection stale; and a row that pins the exported period at 3,600,000. Two direct rows in the same file's `the repair reads the journal as few times as it can (fix round 1, M3)` describe (review 705 F1), with and without recovery's counts: an equal count with max 99 against a journal of six (SQLite on `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 99]`, after a control in sync); each reads in full and wipes, ending at `(6, 6)` (journal passes 2 with the counts, 3 without). Two more direct rows in that describe (fix round 3, review 716 G2, `4410989ca`), each with recovery's counts and each after an in-sync control: (a) a foreign max BELOW the journal's last: the journal is evidence at 1, 2, 3 and 5, with a real `lease-created` record at 4, and SQLite holds {1, 2, 4}; the repair reads in full and wipes (`journalPasses` 2, `fullReplay`, `wipedAll`), and SQLite ends holding exactly {1, 2, 3, 5}, where before fix round 2 a resume replay would have stopped at {1, 2, 4, 5}, whose `(count, max)` the short check accepts; (b) a foreign max at the journal's last + 1: SQLite holds {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7} against evidence 1 to 6; the repair reads in full and wipes, and SQLite ends holding exactly 1 to 6. Each row also pins the short path's own condition after the repair: SQLite's `(count, max)` equals the evidence index's `(total, last)`. D-5836 R1 (`delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`, `77659ed6b`): after one ready pass, further passes that each append one new spool line and file no attempt or interval call `projectDelegationCheckpoint` exactly once (from the flush) and the repair's report has `skipped: true`; a pass armed with `noteSessions` 1,001 carries `repairSkipped=1`. Red at main: twice a pass; its last assertion went green with D-5832's keys. **The mutation table**, run against the tip (`delegation-recovery.test.ts -t 'D-5836 R2'` unless said):

  |---|---|---|

  | MrecClock | the restart of the forced-full clock at `recover()`'s start | survives 15 of 15 (review 677 F8): equivalent, because every successful full repair restarts the clock again: recovery's own, and the first per-pass repair after it (full by the first-after-recover rule). Two lines restart it, and only the one at `recover()`'s start is equivalent alone. The two recover-clock rows' comments now say so, and the stale-no-revision row's comment names both invalidations the failed repair leaves (review 677 F9) |

Fix round 2 (review 705): `344d07582` (rows, red), `16a6faefa` (the code). Fix round 3 (review 716): `cf6cfde88` (the repair's doc line), `4410989ca` (the two rows).

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 6 (run 728, block D-6162..D-6169, taken in order):
- Not defined here: D-6163 and D-6164 were issued to this follow-up, but the named-workspaces plan (#1054) defines both on `main` for its own matter, so this block skips them and takes the rest in order.
- **D-6162** — `the-pure-diff-compares-the-codecs-columns` (the delta projection, part (a); PR #1068): after run 645 cut the per-pass repair, the flush's `project()` was the largest synchronous stretch left, and it is a full rewrite: `projectDelegationCheckpoint` runs in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, deletes the lineage, leases, attempts and activities tables whole and re-inserts every row, upserts every session, and deletes only a leaving session's events, so it is O(all rows) (about 30 to 31 thousand activities on the line of 2026-10-20, in `cpActivities`, and more since) where a flush changes about two activities per appended event plus the sessions it touches (the design pass's estimate: 25 to 400 rows), and an application that changes only intervals, cursors or upkeep changes no projected row. **The rulings.** On 2026-10-20 the operator moved this follow-up ahead of the soak and wave 4 ("Land, then bring delta projection forward"), and on 2026-10-21 ruled three questions. Each is quoted where it lands: ruling 1 (scope) in D-6167, ruling 2 (drift check) in D-6165, and ruling 3 (shape), the one that decides this entry, here, verbatim: **"Additions/updates only"**: any row leaving, or any lease change, falls back to today's full rewrite inside the same call. Wave 4 extends the delta, with its own tests, when it introduces those changes (the operator's ruling of 2026-10-21). **The function.** `delegation-model.ts` gains `diffDelegationProjection(prior, next)`, pure, after `projectionViewOfDelegationCheckpoint` and before the `THE OBSERVATION HEALTH` marker (`delegation-health.test.ts` slices from that marker; `delegation-model.test.ts` bounds the file: no new import, no clock, no randomness). It matches rows by key, per table: sessions by (`sessionId`, `parentIncarnation`), activities by `activityId`, attempts by `attemptId`, leases by `leaseId`, lineage by `lineageId`; the key is spelled once, by each table's `keyColumns`, and the reason texts derive from it. **Change detection.** Two references to the same object skip a row (a fast path only: the builder shares unchanged rows between prior and next, `clone()` being shallow, while `applyCheckpointChange` `structuredClone`s every row, so on that path a value compare is what decides). Otherwise it takes `codec.toDb(row)` for both and compares every entry of the table's `codec.columns`, a list taken from the codec and never typed out, so an encode refusal (`bad-row`, `bad-instant`) fires in the diff exactly where the full projection fires it. **Applicability (ruling 3).** The diff answers `applicable: false`, with a `reason`, when any key `prior` holds is absent from `next` in any of the five tables; when any lease row differs or is new; or when a lineage row whose `leaseId` is non-null in `prior` OR in `next` differs, is new or leaves (a lease and its lineage move together, so a lineage row turning from lease-owned to activity-owned is a lease change). Otherwise `applicable: true` with the rows to UPDATE (the table's name from its codec, the key columns, prior's encoded values and next's), the rows to INSERT, parents first (sessions, activities, attempts, lineage; a new lease is never applicable), and `counts`, next's length for each of the five tables. **The addition the task text did not name:** a key repeated inside `prior` or inside `next` also makes the diff not applicable, so that the full path gives its own answer for a corrupt checkpoint (a repeated session key is such a decline, so the store falls back and the full path, which encodes every row before it checks for the repeat, gives its own answer; the tables are diffed in the full path's order, sessions, activities, attempts, leases, lineage, by a loop that ends at the first table that declines, so a table after it is never encoded and a doubly-corrupt checkpoint is answered by the full path, not by the diff). **Where the types live.** `DelegationProjectChangesResult` (`{ mode: 'delta'; rows: number } | { mode: 'full' }`, `rows` being UPDATEs plus INSERTs written), `DelegationProjectionValues`, `DelegationProjectionUpdate`, `DelegationProjectionInsert` and `DelegationProjectionDiff` are declared once in `delegation-model.ts`, beside `DelegationInsertEventsResult`, because `store.ts` may not import from `delegation-checkpoint.ts` (`coord-store.test.ts` pins that); the store and the applier's port both name them from the model. The pre-BEGIN work that wraps the diff is `prepareDelegationProjectionChanges`, a second exported helper (D-6168). **The rows** (`server/test/delegation-delta-projection.test.ts`, 117 tests at `529e1153a`, committed before any code and all 117 red at main on their own assertion, `diffDelegationProjection is not a function` or `projectDelegationCheckpointChanges is not a function`; reached through a namespace import and a cast so each is red on its assertion and not on a type error):

  | row | what it pins | at main |
  |---|---|---|
  | R6 | a row leaving falls back, one case per table at the store (a session; an activity; a lease with its lineage; an activity-owned lineage row; and an attempt, whose applier case through `applyCheckpointChange` is in D-6166's file): `mode: 'full'` and parity at once (`readDelegationProjection()` canonically equal to `projectionViewOfDelegationCheckpoint(next)`) | red (5 tests, `diffDelegationProjection is not a function`) |
  | R7 | any lease row, or lease-owned lineage row, that differs, is new or leaves falls back, asserted on the returned mode and not on parity alone: a lease differs, is new, leaves; a lease-owned lineage row differs, is new, leaves; a lineage row from non-null `leaseId` to null (the lease's SECOND lease-owned row is turned activity-owned so `assertWave3Leases` still passes, the opening row surviving) and from null to non-null. Turning the only opening row is refused `lease-opening-lineage` by both paths and belongs to R15 | red (8 tests) |
  | R16 | one case generated from each codec column (69 tests): (a) a non-key column of sessions, activities, attempts or non-lease-owned lineage rows writes exactly that one row (a statement trace of exactly `['update <table>']`); (b) a key column, or `lineage.leaseId`, is `mode: 'full'`; (c) a lease column `assertWave3Lease` leaves free is `mode: 'full'`; (d) a lease column it pins is refused with the SAME `DelegationModelError` code the full path gives on a twin store (measured), SQLite unchanged and no BEGIN; (e) `delegation_leases.cleanupPopulation`, whose vocabulary holds one word and whose CHECK pins it, cannot be changed validly: a forced value makes the full path itself fail in SQLite, the delta falls back and the same error surfaces. A column in neither list is a red case titled "IS NOT CLASSIFIED", and the twin measurement must agree with the list | red (the generator itself runs at main; (a) reds at `projectDelegationCheckpointChanges is not a function`) |
  | R17 | rows written, not time: an intent change through `applyCheckpointChange` (every reference new) is `{ delta, rows 1 }` with exactly one `update delegation_sessions`; an interval-only application is `{ delta, rows 0 }` and writes nothing | red (2 tests) |
  | R18 | a new session and its first activity in one delta: `insert sessions` then `insert activities` (the foreign key); and all four kinds, listed child-first in the checkpoint, inserted sessions, activities, attempts, lineage; and (ii) an UPDATE onto a parent that is new in the same delta: an attempt moved onto a new session (`insert sessions` then `update attempts`) and an activity-owned lineage row moved onto a new activity (`insert activities` then `update lineage`), each a delta with parity | red (2 tests); the two (ii) tests added in `a3b63f5b2`, red at the tip before this fix (`FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`) |
  | R6 (pure) | added by `0a1a06b33` after the mutation table: a lease leaving alone (no lineage row, so unreachable through a projected prior) is declined by the pure diff itself, a hand-built prior holding a lease with no lineage row and a next without it | green at the tip by design; MD3(leases) reds it (1 of 119) |
  | R16 (d') | added by `0a1a06b33`: a lease differing in `disposition` alone is declined by the pure diff (the store refuses it earlier, in `assertWave3Leases`) | green at the tip by design; MD8 on `delegation_leases.disposition` reds it (1 of 119) |

  Every successful delta in the file also asserts parity. Edited only in `delegation-model.ts` (the types, the refusal word, `diffProjectionTable`, `diffDelegationProjection`). Commits: `529e1153a` (the rows, red), `d7155132c` (the diff and the store method), `6ebd56d50` and `fdd92900c` (review minors: the key spelled once, `keyColumns` typed as the row's own columns, so the cast went), `0a1a06b33` (the two pure-diff rows), `a3b63f5b2` (the two R15 rows, red) and `4c2993963` (the lazy loop and the early return of the pre-BEGIN helper; the final review's M1).
- **D-6165** — `the-store-writes-the-changed-rows-and-counts-the-tables` (the delta projection, part (b); the store's projection code is in scope by ruling 1): `CoordStore.projectDelegationCheckpointChanges(prior, next)` is synchronous, on the existing `DatabaseSync` (never async: `coord.db`'s invariant), and sits right after `projectDelegationCheckpoint`, which does not change and stays the parity reference. **Before BEGIN, in the full path's own order:** the version, the applied position, `assertWave3Leases(next)`, the diff (where `bad-row` and `bad-instant` fire), all inside the one pure helper of D-6168 and the same in code and message as the full path's; then, in the store, the fallback for a diff that is not applicable, and only then `PRAGMA foreign_keys === 1`, which guards the delta's own writes (since `18446f0b6`, so a declined `next` gets the full path's whole answer on a handle without foreign keys too; R15 (iii), red at `b8a711160`). A diff that is not applicable is returned by the helper at once, before any further check, so a repeated session key (a decline of the diff) is never checked for here: the full path gives its own answer for it, and the helper's old duplicate-session loop was removed as unreachable on the applicable path. So a checkpoint the full path refuses, the delta refuses with the same code and message and SQLite unchanged, or falls back and the full path refuses it. **If the diff is not applicable** the method calls `projectDelegationCheckpoint(next)` and returns `{ mode: 'full' }`. **Otherwise, in one `tx()`:** (1) each new row through `delegationInsertSql(codec)`, a plain INSERT, parents first, a new session included (a new key never exists in prior, so a stray row with that key fails UNIQUE inside the transaction, rolls back and marks the projection stale; `delegationUpsertSql` is still called exactly once, in the full path); (2) each changed row as `UPDATE delegation_<table> SET <non-key columns> = ? WHERE <every column> IS ?`, a compare-and-swap keyed on the primary key whose `WHERE` carries every prior value (`IS ?` so a NULL matches), each of which must report `changes === 1` (the inserts run first because a key never changes in a delta, so no INSERT depends on an UPDATE, while an UPDATE can point at a parent that is new in the same `next`: an attempt moved onto a new session, a lineage row onto a new activity); (3) `SELECT COUNT(*)` on each of the five tables, each held to next's length. Each statement is prepared at most once per table per call (a per-call map, `8bae10497`, as the full path prepares once per table). It returns `{ mode: 'delta', rows: updates + inserts }`. **The tripwire (ruling 2), verbatim:** **"Count check per delta"**: each delta also checks the row count of the five tables, so lost or stray rows are caught at once and trigger the full repair. Edited values in untouched rows wait up to about an hour (the operator's ruling of 2026-10-21). It runs on EVERY delta, a zero-row delta included (an interval-only application writes no row and still counts), and the table names it counts come from the diff's `counts`, so `store.ts` gains no `delegation_leases` spelling. **The refusal word** `projection-delta-mismatch` is in `DelegationModelRefusal` and `DELEGATION_MODEL_REFUSALS` (so `mail-routes.test.ts`'s kebab scan admits it); a miss throws it, the transaction rolls back, and the applier marks the projection stale (D-6166). Its detail names what failed: `<table> row <key>: the compare-and-swap changed <n> rows` for a swap that missed (R8 pins that it names the table and the key and is the compare-and-swap message; the earlier check that the word "count" is kept out of it was replaced by `not.toMatch(/after the delta, expected/)` in `fdd92900c`), and `<table> holds <n> rows after the delta, expected <m>` for the tripwire. **The method has no lease encode, insert or UPDATE path at all.** **The census rows this SQL meets, re-pointed and none loosened:** (1) `delegation-wave3.integration.test.ts`'s lease-writer census had `updatesInDelegationMethods` equal `[]`; it is now the exact four-line list of the new method's `case <codec>.table: return swap('UPDATE delegation_<table>', …` lines (sessions, activities, attempts, lineage, each truncated at the test's existing 80 characters), compared with `toEqual`, so a fifth UPDATE, an UPDATE on another table, or one in another delegation method adds or changes a line and reds it (measured: turning the lineage head into `UPDATE delegation_leases` reds the row through the lease-writer scan). A new assertion reads the lines of `projectDelegationCheckpointChanges` with the test's own method tracker, requires more than 20 of them (so a rename cannot turn the scan into a scan of nothing) and that none names `delegation_leases` or `delegationLeaseCodec`. The `UPDATE ${` ban in every `server/src` file, the per-file `delegation_leases` counts (store.ts 1, model.ts 2), the `delegationLeaseCodec` line list (an exact copy of the store's import line, so the import line stays byte-identical and the new import sits on a line of its own; the row caught my first edit of it) and the one `delegationUpsertSql(...)` call are byte for byte unchanged. (2) `coord-store.test.ts`'s method census is retitled "adds exactly six delegation methods: no health reader, no intent writer, and the compare-and-swap delta projection as the one per-row updater" and is still an exact sorted `toEqual`, so a seventh method, or a missing one, reds it. - **D-6166** — `the-applier-deltas-when-its-witness-holds` (the delta projection, part (c)): `DelegationProjectionPort` gains a REQUIRED fourth member, `projectChanged(prior, next): DelegationProjectChangesResult` (`store.projectDelegationCheckpointChanges`; a required member is a tsc error in every fixture port until it has one), documented beside the other three. `commitLocked` hands `prior` to `projectLocked`, the only call. **`projectLocked`** keeps its stale branch exactly as it was, FIRST (a stale projection takes the full repair and never `projectChanged`); then, inside the existing `project` edge, calls `projectChanged(prior, checkpoint)` when `this.projectedRevision === prior.revision` (the witness: the last thing this applier made the projection say is the checkpoint this one replaces) and `project(checkpoint)` otherwise; then `insertEvents` unchanged; then sets `projectedRevision`. Every application path (`applyAccepted`, the batch, `applyCheckpointChange`, both metas, `pruneBucketCursors`, `createLease`) goes through `commitLocked`. A store refusal inside `projectChanged` lands in the existing catch: the projection is marked stale and the next application's full repair heals it. **Why the witness is enough** (anchors are `server/src/coord/delegation-checkpoint.ts` at the tip, by function and line). Two facts carry it. *Stale implies a null witness.* `projectedRevision` is set at only two places: `repairLocked` (`:1790`, right after `:1788` clears `projectionStale`) and `projectLocked`'s non-stale arm (`:2199`). Every place that sets `projectionStale = true` also nulls it: `repairLocked`'s catch (`:1794-1795`), `projectLocked`'s conflict arm (`:2195-2196`) and its catch (`:2203-2204`); `recover()` nulls it at its start (`:1592`). A null witness never equals a revision, so a stale projection can never reach the witness test. *`recover()` resolves its own clear.* From `:1592` on, `recover()` either reaches `repairLocked()` (`:1639`), which sets the witness (`:1790`) or marks the projection stale (`:1794-1795`), or it returns early (`:1609` tail heal failed, `:1617` the report failed, `:1635` the save failed) with `failureNow` set, and then `requireReady` (`:1580`) refuses every `commitLocked` caller (`:1868`, `:1884`, `:1947`, `:1971`, `:1992`, `:2028`, each behind its own `requireReady`) until a `recover()` succeeds; recovery's own save passes `project: false` (`:1630`) and never reaches `projectLocked`. So once the stale branch has passed, the witness always holds, and **the `project()` arm is defensive**: only a mutant like MD14 reaches it. `drifted` is set (`:2194`) and cleared (`:1789`) only together with `projectionStale` (`:2195`, `:1788`). **Declared equivalents**, each run against the whole applier, recovery and pass-timing files at the tip and surviving every row (applier 26 of 26, recovery 384 of 384, pass-timing 84 of 84; the numbered mutants are in the table below):

  | equivalent | why no row can red it |
  |---|---|
  | the witness always true (`projectChanged(prior, checkpoint)` unconditionally, no `project` arm) | once the stale branch has passed the witness holds (the two facts), so the else arm never runs; the one recovery red of the first run, `D-4658 O1` (the batch door's crash row), was vitest's `STACK_TRACE_ERROR` timeout at 20.9 s under a load average of about 35, green alone and in the five other equivalent runs |
  | the witness tested before the stale branch (`if (witness) delta; else if (stale) {repair; return} else project`) | stale implies a null witness, so a stale projection fails the witness test and reaches the stale branch all the same |
  | an added `!projectionStale` term on the witness | the term is already implied: stale means a null witness |
  | an added `drifted.size === 0` term | `drifted` is set and cleared only together with `projectionStale`, so a non-empty `drifted` implies stale, which implies a null witness |
  | recovery's save also setting `projectedRevision` | the save passes `project: false` and reaches no reader of the witness; `recover()` then reaches `repairLocked()` or returns with `failureNow` set, and `repairLocked` overwrites the witness (set or nulled) on every path before any application can read it |
  | a delta's `projectedRevision` set before the after-edge but stale still marked by the catch, (a) right after the delta or `project` call inside the edge, (b) after the whole edge, catch unchanged | every failure after the set marks the projection stale and nulls the witness in the same catch, and the stale branch is read first, so no reader sees the early value |

  **What stays full:** `repairDelegationProjection`, the per-session heal, the wipe, recovery's repair and the bare `repairProjection()` (the coordinator-intent route) still call `project()`, never `projectChanged`, and D-5836's per-pass short path, its forced full and its clock are unchanged. **The widened residual (ruling 2).** Out-of-band value drift on a row no delta touches, with every count equal, now survives deltas. It heals at the next FULL projection: a fallback application, a repair after a stale mark or drift, the hourly forced full, or recovery, no longer at "the next durable application that projects". Out-of-band inserts and deletes trip the count check at once, but only on a NET change of a table's count: a balanced delete and insert in one table passes it, and the compare-and-swap catches that only if the delta happens to update that row. `repairProjection`'s docstring states this (review 723's follow-up, `6ebd56d50`); D-5836's own entry is left as recorded, with "(widened by D-6166)" beside its residual. **O5 is unchanged:** the delta runs inside the existing `project` durability edge, before `insertEvents`, and no edge is added to `DELEGATION_DURABILITY_EDGES`; R14 pins the log order `projectChanged:delta`, `insertEvents:1`. **The measures (ruling 1).** `commitMeasures(): { encodes, writes, cpBytes }` is cumulative since construction and reads no clock. `encodes` is the whole-checkpoint canonical encodes `commitLocked` made: the no-op compare's two (`+= 2`, only when the application does not append), `encodeDelegationCheckpoint`'s one (`+= 1`), and `verifyDecodable`'s re-encode (`+= 1`, counted before the decoder is called, since bytes it was just given always re-encode). Row and codec encodes, journal-record encodes and `applyCheckpointChange`'s `leaseState` compare are NOT counted. `writes` counts the checkpoint encodes at the write point and `cpBytes` is `bytes.length` of the last of them (the canonical JSON plus its final LF, the bytes written), `null` before any; counted where the work happens, so a commit that throws later keeps what it spent. `canonicalDelegationJson`, `encodeDelegationCheckpoint` and `decodeDelegationCheckpoint` are untouched. **The single writer (R23).** The witness lives in memory and depends on there being one writer of the five tables, so it is pinned by three line lists compared with `toEqual`, never a counted pin over `watch.ts`: the methods of `server/src` that write the five tables are exactly `projectDelegationCheckpoint` (its ten lines pinned literally) and `projectDelegationCheckpointChanges` (structurally: one UPDATE line per table for sessions, activities, attempts and lineage, none on leases; `delegationInsertSql(<codec>)` once for each of the four codecs, no upsert, no lease codec; no other write-shaped line); the callers of the two methods (word-bounded, the old name being a prefix of the new) are the delta's own fallback and the lane's two adapter lines, built from the file and the enclosing method; and the code lines matching `(INSERT INTO|DELETE FROM)\s+\$\{` are the one `delegationInsertSql` line. **The rows** (`server/test/delegation-delta-applier.test.ts`, 25 tests at `10590f574`, 26 now; 22 red at main, 3 green by design; the port is built in the test with `projectChanged` reached through a cast, every call logged as `project`, `projectChanged:delta|full|threw` or `insertEvents:n`; fault controls make the next `project` or `projectChanged` throw once, or crash at a named durability edge; no fsync, virtual clocks, neutral ids):

  | row | what it pins | at main |
  |---|---|---|
  | R2 | stale with no revision change: the next application takes the stale branch's full repair and never calls `projectChanged`, the repair ran (its report, and the next per-pass repair says `skipped: true`) | red (the positive follow-up: the application after the repair calls `projectChanged` once and answers `delta`; main satisfies the negative claim trivially) |
  | R3 | an `insertEvents` conflict leaves `drifted` set; the next application takes the stale branch's full repair, and the drifted session leaves and re-enters (three projections) | red |
  | R4 | the first application after open, after a re-open, and after a successful `recover()` DOES delta: `projectChanged` once, `mode: 'delta'`, `project` not called (recovery's own full repair set the witness) | red (3 tests) |
  | R5 | recovery's save, then a failed repair (the port's `project` throws once): the projection is stale, the next application takes the stale branch's full repair and never `projectChanged`, SQLite is rebuilt from the journal (all 4 events, parity), and the projection is no longer stale | red |
  | R6 | an attempt through `applyCheckpointChange`: adding one deltas, removing it answers `mode: 'full'` (the fallback is the store's, inside the same call) and parity holds at once | red |
  | R8, R9 (i), R9 (ii), R10 | at the applier, the store's refusals: the application reports the refusal, the projection goes stale, SQLite is as the out-of-band damage left it (a rolled-back transaction), and the next repair heals | red (at main the full projection heals the damage and the application reports `projected`) |
  | R11 | the residual: an out-of-band value edit on a row no delta touches survives every delta and heals at the forced full (the virtual clock past `DELEGATION_REPAIR_FULL_MS`) | red (at main the first application would heal the edit) |
  | R12 | the heal and the wipe call `project`, never `projectChanged`, and the drifted session's events are deleted and replayed (two rows) | green at main by design; MD11 reds it |
  | R13 | a bare `repairProjection()` takes the full path | green at main by design; MD22 reds it |
  | R14 | a fault at the `project` edge, before and after, with the delta active: stale then healed (full); on the before fault SQLite is unchanged (`dump()` equal) and nothing was called; on the after fault the log is exactly `projectChanged:delta`, `insertEvents:1` | red (the control: the delta is active before the fault) |
  | R20 | twin applier-and-store pairs on separate fixture homes, one always full and one through the delta, driven by a seeded generator (mulberry32; fixed literal seeds 11, 23, 37, 41, 59 and 73 in `it.each`; 100 steps a seed since `6ebd56d50`, which raised it from 40 because four seeds missed step kinds; an explicit 180 s timeout, about 3 s a seed; no `Math.random`, no clock): a new session (half the time with its first activity in the same application), touch a session, open, touch (through `applyCheckpointChange`) and end an activity, add and remove an attempt, `createLease` with its lineage, set and clear intent, interval-only, cursor drop, a heal that leaves and re-enters, a per-pass repair (sometimes past the forced-full interval). After every step no application reports `failed`, both checkpoints are equal, the five tables by primary key and `delegation_events` without `id` are equal, and both `readDelegationProjection()` canonically equal `projectionViewOfDelegationCheckpoint(checkpoint)`; each message names seed and step; the delta twin returned `delta` at least once, the full twin never; every step kind ran at least once per seed, a `createLease` is `'created'` and a heal step heals | red (`the delta twin's projectChanged returned mode 'delta' at least once: expected false to be true`) |
  | R23 | the single-writer census above, (1) the writer methods and (2) the callers, (3) added in `6ebd56d50` for the one `delegationInsertSql` line | (1) and (2) red; (3) added after the rows commit and not measured at main |

  **Existing rows updated, none weakened.** `delegation-recovery.test.ts`: `delegationProjectionPortOfStore` gains `projectChanged` over the real delta; the logging harness logs `projectChanged` (never as `project`) and returns the real answer; the O5 order row expects `'projectChanged', 'insertEvents:1'` where it expected `'project', 'insertEvents:1'`, its edge-order assertion unchanged; "projects ONCE" asserts that the log holds exactly one projection of either kind and that it is `['projectChanged']` (stronger than before); the four crash filters ("SQLite untouched before the durable point") match both labels, `l === 'project' || l === 'projectChanged' || l.startsWith('insertEvents')`, so a delta before the durable point fails them as a full projection would; the prune row expects the prune to be a `projectChanged` and `indexOf('project')` to be -1; the heal-order row is left as it is (a stray `projectChanged` at open would fail it); the fixture ports gain the member, a fault port's throwing under the same control as its `project` (`ctl.failProject`). `coordinator-intent-route.test.ts` gains the member in its four ports (the two `broken` ones throw under the same flag) and `delegation-health.test.ts` in its one, nothing else changed in either. - **D-6167** — `the-lane-counts-the-delta-and-measures-the-checkpoint` (the delta projection, part (d)): **ruling 1, verbatim:** **"Delta alone + measuring"**: only the delta projection, plus new timing keys (checkpoint bytes, encode count, delta vs full, rows written). The incremental encode and a split-up hourly repair become named next follow-ups, sized from what run 645's live line shows. This brings the store's projection code into scope (the operator's ruling of 2026-10-21). The operator declined, for this run, an incremental encode and build, a split hourly repair, a full read-back verify per delta, and general deletes and lease changes. **The adapter** `projectChanged: (prior, next) => coord.projectDelegationCheckpointChanges(prior, next)` is the fourth beside the three. **`timedPort`'s fourth member** is timed as `rewrite` (`projects = 1`) exactly as `project` is, so `rewrite` now holds the delta's time. A member passed through untimed, or routed to `project`, is the silent failure (MD12). **The counts.** `projectDelta` and `projectFull` count `projectChanged` calls by the MODE the store returned (a fallback is `projectFull`); `deltaRows` sums the `rows` of the deltas. The answer is read only after the call returns, so a throw counts neither; and a `project()` call (repair, heal, wipe, forced full, the non-witness path) counts in neither: `rewrites` counts both kinds, and `rewrites - (projectDelta + projectFull)` is the bare `project()` calls plus any delta call that threw (the `delegation projection stale:` warn line names it); a dedicated count of the throws would be a new key, left to the follow-up. They are counted on the CURRENT pass's record beside `rewrites`, so they follow `passTiming`. **The five keys and their places.** `projectDelta`, `projectFull` and `deltaRows` sit right after `rewriteEvents` and are present on every line that carries the `rewrite` key, explicit 0 included. `cpBytes` and `encodes` sit in the size group, right after `cpCursors` and before `censusAge`, because the formatter fills in list order and the keys after `censusAge` (`cpActivities` and `cpMapping` among them) drop first under the bound, so beside them the new keys would drop first. `encodes` is present wherever the line carries `cpSessions` and the other checkpoint sizes, that is iff the pass reached its applier (it is `commitMeasures().encodes` now minus the baseline `startTiming` took, the baseline being 0 when there was none) and `cpBytes` iff the applier also wrote a checkpoint in the window (`writes` advanced past the baseline's), so it is absent on a pass that wrote none. **The window rule.** `startTiming` snapshots `commitMeasures()` as the pass's baseline, so `encodes` and `cpBytes` cover the span from `startTiming` to the line, while the three counts follow `passTiming`; under an overlapping cut pass the two can describe different spans (the `LanePassTiming` comment says so). **The bound's new arithmetic** (D-5692's 2,048-byte bound and its `dropped=` rule hold): the input keys are 107 (were 102); the realistic line (7 digits each) is 1,908 bytes (was 1,817) and drops nothing; at `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` the line is 2,039 bytes (was 2,042) with 77 of the 107 input keys kept and `dropped=30` (was 25). **None of the five new keys drops** at the largest values: the dropped keys are the last 30 of the list, and the new keys sit before `censusAge`. Five OLDER keys that were kept at 102 now drop: `vUnresolved`, `vConflicting`, `vUnmeasured`, `eLease` and `eRunChild`; so 15 older keys are at risk, not the ten D-5832 named. The full dropped set is `vUnresolved vConflicting vUnmeasured eLease eRunChild eUnowned eAbsence eRepair eInterval repairPasses repairInserted repairFull repairWiped repairHealed censusAge flushCpu upkeepCpu effectsCpu repairCpu reconcileCpu ownersCpu stallFlush stallMid stallRepair repairRewrite yields cpActivities cpMapping repairSkipped attemptsSkipped`; the bound row pins the five by name and `keys.slice(77)` exactly. **The reading rule** (D-5832's, updated in place there): `rewrite` now includes the delta's time, and `encodes` and `cpBytes` cover the pass's window, not its `passTiming` record. `single-definition.test.ts`'s `watch.ts` literal-pin count is 84 before and after (ceiling 85); no source pin over `watch.ts` was added, so R19 and R22 are behavioural, through the lane. **The rows** (`server/test/delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`; `7d99080d5`, committed red at main; the delta method is reached by a recording wrapper installed on the store instance through a cast):

  | row | what it pins | at main |
  |---|---|---|
  | R1 (d) | re-points D-5836 R1: a steady pass after one ready pass calls the delta method once, from the flush, and `projectDelegationCheckpoint` zero times; the repair's report still says `skipped: true`, with `repairSkipped` and `attemptsSkipped` still asserted; the armed line carries `projectDelta=1`, `projectFull=0`, and `deltaRows` equal to the `rows` the store returned (asserted a number above 0 since `6ebd56d50`, so the check cannot pass vacuously). It keeps its teeth: the three steady passes still pin the full method's silence and the skipped report, now on the returned mode | red (`the store's delta method is called once, from the flush: expected +0 to be 1`) |
  | R19 | through the production lane (nothing stubbed), the delta wrapper advances the virtual clock by 1,500 ms from inside the call: one delta call, `expectTimings(line, { flush: 1500, rewrite: 1500 })`, `projectDelta > 0`, `rewrite === 1500`, `rewrites === 1` | red (`the lane called the delta method once, in the pass: expected [] to have a length of 1`) |
  | R22 | the measures: `encodes` is counted independently by a spy on `canonicalDelegationJson` filtered to whole-checkpoint arguments (`v === 1` plus the six arrays), `cpBytes` against `statSync` of `delegation-checkpoint.json`. (a) an identical interval re-recorded, the applier's no-op compare: spy 2, `encodes=2`, no `cpBytes` key; (b) an appending flush: spy 1, `encodes=1`, `cpBytes` the file size; (c) a flush plus one `applyCheckpointChange` (a new attempt): spy 5, `encodes=5` (1 for the flush's commit and 4 for the change's: the compare's two, the write's one, `verifyDecodable`'s one), `cpBytes` the file size | red (`the line's encodes is the independent count: expected undefined to be 2`, 1 and 5) |

  **Existing rows re-pointed, never loosened.** D-5692 R1: its independent `rewrites` is the delta method's calls plus the full method's calls not nested inside the delta method (a depth counter in call-through `mockImplementation`s), and the expected line gains independent counts for the new keys (`projectDelta`, `projectFull` and `deltaRows` from the spy's RETURNED values, only `mock.results` of type `'return'`; `encodes` from the `canonicalDelegationJson` spy read right after the armed pass, before the test's own decode canonicalises too; `cpBytes` from `statSync`), `projectFull > 0` (its four `createLease` commits fall back), `projectDelta > 0` and `encodes > 0`; it keeps its teeth because no count is read from the line it checks. D-5692 R5 (ii): its mirror `SLOW_KEYS` list gains the five keys in the same places (it asserts the fixed list in order). D-5835 R1 (i): its wrapper wraps both methods through one `queueFromFlush`, so the immediate is queued from whichever the flush calls and the turn after flush is still proved. The rig wraps BOTH `projectDelegationCheckpoint` and `projectDelegationCheckpointChanges`; its `st.projects` hook fires on the first call of either, and a full call made while inside the delta method (its fallback) does not fire it again, so D-5692 R3's flush case, D-5832 R3 and D-5832 R5's flush arm keep their expected values unchanged and pass. The D-5832 bound row is re-measured (above); `delegation-wave3.integration.test.ts`'s lane store-contact line list gains the fourth adapter line, in its place, (and a comment there that counted "a fourth" store contact now counts "a fifth").   **Measured against the expected column.** Every mutant is killed by at least one row (the two survivors by the rows added after), but not always by the row the task text expected. R20 does not red MD3 on sessions, activities or lineage, or MD8 on sessions, attempts or lineage columns: its generator reaches an attempt leaving and an activity's display name through the delta, while a leaving session, activity or lineage row occurs only through the heal's `project`, which never takes the delta (R20 does red MD3 on attempts, MD4c, MD8 on `activities.displayName` and MD10). MD12 in its routed-to-`project` form is reded by R19 and the R1 rows and not by D-5692 R3 or D-5832 R3, which red only the untimed form. The two survivors are real: a lease can leave only with its lease-owned lineage rows (a lease always has an opening row, `lease-opening-lineage`), so with a projectable prior the lineage leaving check already declines it, and a probe in the scratch export confirmed the pure diff gives `applicable: true` under MD3(leases); and `assertWave3Leases` refuses a `disposition`-only change before the diff, so no store-level row can observe the comparator skipping it. They get pure-diff rows (R6 and R16 (d') above), not a declared equivalence: a mutant that is real at the pure level is a guard without a red. The task text and the code disagree once, with no behaviour departing and so no number of its own: R22 (a)'s "no-op flush" cannot be a flush, because the lane's gate never applies an unchanged bucket and `applyIsolating` returns on an empty staged list, so no encode happens at all; the no-op compare (two encodes, an early return, no write) is reached through an identical interval re-recorded through `applyAccepted`, which the row asserts appends nothing to the spool, makes exactly one `applyAccepted` call and leaves the revision where it was.

  **Next follow-ups, named by ruling 1.** The incremental checkpoint encode and build (stretch A of a flush, which the delta makes the longest per-pass stall), and a split or chunked hourly repair (the forced full and the hourly upkeep stay as they are: this run makes them measurable and does not shorten them), each sized from the live line, which now carries `projectDelta`, `projectFull`, `deltaRows`, `encodes` and `cpBytes`. Deletes and lease changes in the delta are wave 4's (ruling 3), with their own tests. The full path's own pre-BEGIN checks and the helper's are two copies of one rule, kept equal by R15's twin parity because `projectDelegationCheckpoint` may not change here; one shared helper is for a later consolidation. Not ruled, and not this run's: when the soak starts, and whether a full read-back verify should ever run outside the forced full. Review 723's two cosmetic minors are fixed: H1 (the two D-5832 R6 titles regain `): with`, and R6 (ii)'s clause is completed) in `6ebd56d50`, and H2 (the MCPUsrc row above) in this block's commit.
- **D-6168** — `the-delta-pre-begin-checks-live-in-the-model` (a departure from the task text, which put the delta's pre-BEGIN checks in the store method): main armed the god-file growth check (#1058) after the text was written, and measured at `16fc9746a` (`GROWTH_CHECK=1`, committed head against the merge-base) `store.ts` grew by 66 net code lines against a limit of 40, where `watch.ts` grew by 10. The pure pre-BEGIN work (the version, the applied position, `assertWave3Leases`, the diff, in that order; a duplicate-session check followed until `4c2993963`) therefore moved into ONE exported pure helper in `delegation-model.ts`, `prepareDelegationProjectionChanges(prior, next)`, which returns the diff; the store keeps the fallback, `PRAGMA foreign_keys` and the SQL, the fallback ahead of the foreign-keys check since `18446f0b6` (the task text's order put the check first, so a declined `next` on a handle without foreign keys was refused `foreign-keys-off` where the full path gives its own code). Otherwise the same order, the same codes and messages (R15's twin parity measures both code and message against the full path on a twin store, so the move cannot drift), no new import, no clock. With the per-table prepare-once change, `store.ts` ends at +38 net code lines (+35 at `8bae10497`, then +3 for the restored import lines and the wrapped compare-and-swap throw that the review asked for; the limit is 40 and the file has little room left in this PR) and `watch.ts` at +10. Re-measure: `cd server && GROWTH_CHECK=1 systemd-run --user --scope -q -p MemoryMax=4G ./node_modules/.bin/vitest run test/growth-check.test.ts --reporter=verbose | grep delta` (the test is unarmed without `GROWTH_CHECK=1`, so the number is the criterion). Commits: `8bae10497` (the helper and prepare-once), `fdd92900c` (the import lines restored byte-identical where a census pins them, the throw wrapped to the file's norm), `4c2993963` (the helper returns a not-applicable diff at once and its duplicate-session loop is removed as unreachable on the applicable path; the diff is a loop that ends at the first declining table; the store inserts before it updates; store.ts stays +38).
