Found in the wave-3 follow-up 7 (run 740, block D-6190..D-6201, taken in order):

- **D-6190** — `an-attempt-is-a-decision-not-its-witnesses` (wave-3 follow-up 7, part 1; PR #1097): **The defect.** `attemptOf` hashed the attempt's evidence list into its ID, and policy and `fileAttempts` deduplicated by that ID, so when a tree's `parent-cwd` or `ambiguous-subagent-path` evidence grew (`delegation-correlation.ts`'s verdicts carry the tree's evidence list), policy filed a NEW row on every pass whose list differed. Each new row goes through a full `applyCheckpointChange`, whose synchronous pre-write path (plan, clone, fold, build, encode) is the stall. **The rulings (operator, 2026-10-21).** Ruling 1, **"Separate follow-up now"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: a small follow-up after run 728, carrying the confirmed defect fixes before wave 4. Attempt re-filing is among them; so are async workflow runs that never end and start-less SubagentStop candidates that never age out, which the next run carries.). Ruling 2, **"Ask the soak watcher"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: measure first, on copies). Ruling 4, **"Split: attempt fix first"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: the attempt key and the collapse ship as their own small run as soon as run 728 merges. The fold rule, the retirement, the workflow ends, the stop ageing, the slow-line keys and the doc corrections follow as a separate run on top of it.). The operator gave ruling 4 after a new finding (below). Ruling 3 decides D-6191 and is quoted there. **The measurements** (on copies, by the soak watcher; inference marked). The first (mail 7561, snapshot 2026-10-21T06:43:48Z): 351 attempt rows, about 30 KB a row, 10.6 MB of a 70.8 MB checkpoint; one key group (parent, incarnation, path, rung, verdict) held 309 of the 351; `cpAttempts` was flat until 2026-10-20 19:00Z and then rose by 20 to 36 an hour; the worst live stall then was 15.4 s, the attempt-filing pass inside `applyCheckpointChange`'s pre-write path (mail 7530). The second (mail 7598, snapshot 2026-10-21T07:35:58Z), by the full eight-field key (parent, incarnation, project, admin record, path, rung, verdict, detail): 379 rows in 43 groups, one group (rung null, `unresolved`, `parent-cwd`) holding 337, so 336 rows beyond the first. The new finding (2026-10-21, the server at v0.0.528): at 12:58:18Z an attempt-filing pass stalled for **19.0 s** (`stallMid` 19,048, `cpAttempts` 509 to 510, `block` 37,325), the soak watcher's 5 s `/health` probe got no answer during it, `cpAttempts` stood at 510 at 12:58 against 351 at 06:44, and it recurs about 26 times an hour. **Inference, not asserted:** after deploy policy files no row for a decision already on file, however its evidence grows, so in steady state the `attempts` phase does not run, `attemptsSkipped` is absent from the line (it reads 1 only when policy re-plans an on-file linked row whose lease was lost), and the 19 s stretch is gone. **The key** (`delegation-model.ts`, one definition for policy, the collapse and the tests, placed with the other two new functions immediately before the `THE OBSERVATION HEALTH` marker, after `prepareDelegationProjectionChanges` and so after the module-private `utf8Compare`; `delegation-health.test.ts` slices from the marker, and the model gains no import, no clock, no randomness): `delegationAttemptKeyOf(a)` (`:1355`) is `canonicalDelegationJson` of `[parentId, parentIncarnation, project, adminRecord, canonicalPath, rung, verdict, detail]`, every column except the clock (`attemptedAt`) and the witnesses (`evidenceJournalIds`); `delegationAttemptsByKey(attempts)` (`:1364`) maps each key to the EARLIEST row on file (the smallest `attemptedAt` through `parseDelegationInstant`; a tie to the smaller `attemptId` under `utf8Compare`). **The v2 ID** (`delegation-policy.ts:291-294`): the hash is the canonical JSON of `['delegation_attempt_v2', parentId, parentIncarnation, project, adminRecord, canonicalPath, rung, verdict, detail]`; the evidence stays on the row as `evidenceJournalIds`. The v1 preimage has ten elements and the v2 nine, so a v1 ID and a v2 ID never coincide, whatever the inputs; the domain tag is a hash input, never a code anything reads. **Policy dedupes by key.** `Ground.attemptIds` became `Ground.attemptsByKey` (`:243`, built at `:259` from `delegationAttemptsByKey(cp.attempts)`), which covers v1 and v2 rows alike, so the first pass after deploy re-files nothing; `finalConflicts` is unchanged. The run-child-owned plan (`:317`), the rungs-disagree plan (`:333`) and the unowned plan (`:388`) test `ground.attemptsByKey.has(delegationAttemptKeyOf(attempt))`; `planWorkspaceOverLease` (`:375-376`) tests both the file and its own `out` by key. **`planLinked` reuses the on-file row** (`:335-337`): `const attempt = ground.attemptsByKey.get(delegationAttemptKeyOf(fresh)) ?? fresh`. The request is still returned, with the current pass's `correlationJournalIds` and `firstObservedAt`. The reason is the lease gate: `watch.ts:12033` creates a lease only when `filed.has(e.attempt.attemptId)`. With a v1 linked row on file and its lease lost, a fresh v2 row would be filed beside the v1 row (a duplicate), and returning `[]` would never request the lease again; the on-file row passes the gate and files nothing (C-W2). **Why `fileAttempts`' ID filter is the key filter, and its code is unchanged.** A v2 ID is a one-to-one function of the key; policy hands `fileAttempts` either an on-file row (reused, so its ID is on file and the filter drops it) or a fresh v2 row whose key is not on file; so for everything policy hands it the ID filter equals the key filter, and a v1 row's key is covered in policy. Only its docstring changed. **Derived (not an operator ruling), from ruling 1 and the measured readers:** a grown evidence list is never written back and nothing is updated in place, because nothing reads an attempt's evidence list and an update in place would keep the full pre-write path (15 to 19 s at the measured size) on every pass where the evidence grows; the on-file row keeps the evidence of its first filing. **Derived (not an operator ruling):** there is no checkpoint validation of key uniqueness, because it would refuse the live checkpoint while v1 duplicates stand, and the collapse (D-6191) removes them anyway. **What did not change:** no new port member, store method, SQL writer, durability edge, checkpoint key, hold reason, journal entry kind, lease refusal word or migration; no new pass phase, marker or slow-line key; the fold, the journal and the checkpoint format are untouched, and the checkpoint stays v1, read by the strict decoder, which checks an attempt ID's uniqueness and shape and never that it hashes the row, so a v2 row is an ordinary attempt row to an older build. After a rollback past this change the older build dedupes by evidence-hashed ID again, so re-filing returns until the roll-forward, whose first `upkeep` collapses whatever was filed meanwhile; nothing refuses and nothing is lost. **The D-5834 re-pin** (`delegation-pass-timing.test.ts`, the constants now at `:1501-1503`: `FILED_ID`, `AT_START`, `AT_END_B`), because the v2 ID changes the hashed checkpoint. R1(b)'s injected attempt was re-pointed FIRST, to another decision key (`{ ...template, attemptId: NEW_ID, detail: 'rungs-disagree' }`, the template being linked, rung 4, detail null; `NEW_ID` unchanged), so `AT_END_B` is computed once, over the final fixture. Then the three constants were re-pinned by the documented method (the sha of the canonical in-memory checkpoint and of the file bytes at the `attempts` and `leases` announcements, repeated runs equal): `FILED_ID` `6e45196d` to `12129f27`; `AT_START` canonical `536b91c4` to `f913857b`, file `cf44ab9a` to `f994f70e` (revision 3 unchanged); `AT_END_B` canonical `2537dd6c` to `480ce0c0`, file `a0d42d89` to `48ff0c5a` (revision 4 unchanged). The values were read from the assertion diff of the first failing runs; two runs gave identical shas, and then the whole file ran twice with the constants set, 85 of 85 both times. The comment now says the values were computed with production at `d9243ee16` plus this change and identical at the commit that re-pinned them. **Flipped-digit check:** `FILED_ID`'s first hex digit changed from `1` to `0`: R1 (a) and R1 (b) both RED (2 failed, 82 skipped), reverted, file diff confirmed, 85 of 85 green. R22's injected attempt (`:1966`, `{ ...template, attemptId: NEW_ID }`) was left as it is: both injections sit in the LAST pass of their fixtures, and the collapse runs in `upkeep`, before `reconcile` files the injection, so no pass ever sees the same-key duplicate (measured at the scratch tip, then at the tip: R22 green); re-pointing R1(b) is defensive, and its constants are what moved. **The nine rows for the key (the collapse's five, C-K1 to C-K5, are in D-6191; fourteen in all)** (each run at base `7da6498a7`, the tip of run 728; red rows give the message; the model's two functions and the lane are reached through a namespace lookup or a cast so each row reds on its own assertion, not on a type error):

  | row | pins | at base |
  |---|---|---|
  | C-P1 | an old-style attempt (ID `hex('legacy-v1')`, key fields as the plan builds them) is on file; a plan of the same decision with later evidence files nothing | RED `expected [ {record-unowned-worktree…} ] to deeply equal []`; MC2 and MC1+2 red it |
  | C-P2 | re-points the old evidence arm of the ID test and retitles it (a different reason is a different attempt, more evidence is the same one): the first two plans have equal IDs, the third (`parent-cwd`) differs, and the second row still carries `[id, more]` | RED `expected '33d772c7…' to be '7991e34a…'`; MC1 reds it |
  | C-P3 | a row of another detail (`ambiguous-subagent-path`) on file for the same parent and tree: a `parent-cwd` plan files exactly one | green by design (the on-file ID is a made-up hex); MC3 reds it |
  | C-P4 | (i) a linked rung-4 row on file under `hex('legacy-linked')`, no lease: one `request-linked-lease-identity`, its attempt `toEqual` the on-file row, its request carrying the NEW evidence; (ii) at rung 3 the attempt's rung is 3 and its ID is not the legacy one | (i) RED (`e.attempt` differs from the on-file row); (ii) green by design; MC2, MC8, MC9 red (i), MC5 reds (ii) |

  **Departures from the task text in these rows, each measured.** (1) C-W1's recipe (append a parent event before passes 2 and 3) cannot work: the birth wait is two passes, so the attempt is first filed on pass 3, and the lane keeps ONE evidence event per (session, incarnation, agent, cwd) tuple, so another parent event at the same cwd adds nothing (the evidence stayed 3 ids). As built, the decision is filed in the first three passes and two further subagents (new agent tuples at the tree) are added before passes 4 and 5. It asserts zero changes across passes 4-5, stronger than the text, so it also reds under MK2. (2) C-W2 seeds the legacy row after `at(0)`, not before the first pass: an attempt row needs its parent's session row (the projection's `needSession`), which exists only after the first spool-reading pass. "More evidence at `at(3)`" is not separately reachable (rung-4 evidence is the listing pair), so the evidence difference is the legacy row's old evidence against the planned one. (3) The mutant table's C-P6 for MC6b is unreachable (below).

  **The mutants (25; each run alone against the tip in a scratch copy, the four delegation files each, with an outer `timeout 900`; none timed out, the longest 133 s).** Measured reds, which replace the text's "reds" column:

  **Added after the whole-branch review (a derived addition, not an operator ruling):** C-P8 (a)-(c) and MC12a-c close the review's finding that three of the four key-dedupe sites were unpinned. Measured in a scratch copy of the tip, each site alone. MC12a reds C-P8 (a). MC12b and MC12c do not red anything, and the reason is in `planRecord`: `finalConflicts` is built from every `conflicting` attempt by tree (project and admin record), and a `linked` or `unresolved` verdict on a tree in that set returns before `planLinked` or `planWorkspaceOverLease` runs. The attempt those two sites plan is always `conflicting`, so a row of its key on file is a conflicting row of its tree, and the guard has already refused. Their key check is a second line, not a first. With the guard also off (`planRecord` without `finalConflict`), MC12b reds C-P8 (b) and MC12c reds C-P8 (c), and neither is red without its own site mutant; the two final-conflict rows ("a prior conflicting attempt for the tree is final" and "the finality is the TREE's") red in that run too, so the guard is pinned elsewhere. C-P8 (b) and (c) therefore pin the outcome, and they would catch a change that removes the guard and the site check together. They do not make the two sites' key check a mutation-tested line. The review's failure scenario (two full rewrites per pass) holds for the run-child-owned site only.

  The collapse's mutants MK1 to MK6 are in D-6191, and MR1 is below. **Three disagreements with the text's table, measured:** MC6b does not red C-P6 (its two parents differ in `parentId`, not in incarnation; C-P5[parentIncarnation] and C-K2[parentIncarnation] carry it); MC10 is not C-P7 alone (the D-5834 pins embed the tag, so R1 (a) and (b) red too); MK2 does not red C-K5 (D-6191). The extra reds listed are harmless, they only strengthen. **Declared equivalents.** *MC10:* a v1 preimage has ten elements and the new one nine, so the two canonical strings never coincide whatever the tag, and nothing reads the tag back; C-P7 pins the tag as a decision, and the D-5834 pins embed it, but no behaviour depends on it. *MC11, for policy alone:* with the latest row kept, policy's 131 of 131 stay green, because the ground row is used only by `has` and as the planned attempt, whose ID meets only membership tests over the on-file IDs (`watch.ts:12033`, and `fileAttempts`' filter at `:12093-12095`), and every row of a group is on file, so any row of the group passes those tests. It is NOT equivalent overall: the collapse shares the function, and C-K1, C-K4 and C-K5 red it there. **Growth check.** `GROWTH_CHECK=1` after `git fetch origin main`, against the merge-base: `server/src/watch.ts` base 5715, head 5726, **delta +11** (limit 40); every other listed file +0. The only `watch.ts` regions edited: the `upkeep` phase (the collapse and its comment), `fileAttempts`' docstring, and one name in the import from `./coord/delegation-model.js`. **Landing runs at the merged head** (re-measured by D-6272 at `bc231bbcb`, the merge of PR #1097; four commits followed `196a9552f` on the branch: `53b3407cc`, `468d83efa`, `ca0da49ba`, `34f0c537c`, and C-P8 among them brought delegation-policy from 128 to 131), each file alone, foreground, from `server/`: delegation-policy 131/131, delegation-model 217/217, delegation-pass-timing 85/85, delegation-watch 250/250, delegation-recovery 384/384, delegation-wave3.integration 32/32, delegation-health 52/52, delegation-delta-applier 26/26, delegation-delta-projection 127/127, delegation-pass-windows 7/7, single-definition 825/825, deviation-refs 31/31, growth-check (`GROWTH_CHECK=1`) 45/45, server `tsc --noEmit -p .` rc 0; typecheck-tests 12/12 in the run-762 worktree on its rows commit (the merged head's code with tests only added; in the scratch clone of the merged head its one red is the check that `pwa`'s compiler resolves under the clone's own `node_modules`, which there is a link). **Rows the change could move, all green and no asserted value moved:** D-5692 R1's rig (`cpAttempts`, `attemptsSkipped`, 728's `projectDelta`, `deltaRows`, `encodes`), the wave3.integration rows (the `:377` lease path, the `:484` ids across a lost database, the `:695` and `:704` rung-1 attempts), 728's R6, R20, R1 (d) and R22; the only values that moved are the D-5834 pins above. **The bench:** bench unrun: disk — at 2026-10-21T20:45Z the worktree volume had 7.6 GiB available, under the 30 GiB rule. **Commits** (`git log --oneline 7da6498a7..HEAD`): `1b82b6123` (the rows and this block's skeleton), `522ed5669` (review 758 F1), `72d1befc2` (review minors in the rows), `d9243ee16` (the model's three functions), `577a0ce09` (the v2 ID, policy's key dedupe, R1(b)'s re-point, the D-5834 re-pin), `196a9552f` (the collapse, D-6191). **Review minors, accepted** (plan-sanctioned): R1(b)'s injected row pairs `linked` and rung 4 with detail `rungs-disagree`, an incoherent combination no policy would plan, kept because it is a distinct key as the text asked; - **D-6191** — `repeated-attempts-collapse-to-the-earliest` (wave-3 follow-up 7, part 2; PR #1097): **Ruling 3 (operator, 2026-10-21), verbatim:** **"Collapse to one (Recommended)"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: the attempts already on file that repeat one decision collapse to the earliest row of each decision key (D-6191).) Rulings 1, 2 and 4 are quoted in D-6190. **Which row stays.** The EARLIEST row of each key: the smallest `attemptedAt`, a tie going to the smaller `attemptId` under `utf8Compare`. It is the same function as policy's ground, `delegationAttemptsByKey` (`delegation-model.ts:1364`), so the row policy reuses for a key (`planLinked`) is the row the collapse keeps. `collapseDelegationAttempts(attempts)` (`:1375`) returns `{ attempts, removed }`: one row per key, relative order kept, and the SAME array with `removed: 0` when no key repeats (as `upkeepDelegationIntervals` does), so a caller can tell there is nothing to do without a change. **Where it runs.** Inside the existing `upkeep` phase (`watch.ts:11124-11142`), after the interval upkeep and inside the same `try { … } finally { endUpkeep(); }`, behind a pre-check that reads the lane's in-memory checkpoint: `if (collapseDelegationAttempts(durable.state().checkpoint.attempts).removed > 0)`. The change is one `applyCheckpointChange` whose closure RECOMPUTES the collapse on `cp` and returns `cp` itself when it removes nothing, then one `console.warn` line naming the count (`ccrc-server: delegation attempts: N row(s) that repeated a decision already on file were removed`); a refusal is caught and warned (`delegation attempt collapse failed`), so a failed collapse leaves the duplicates and the next pass tries again. It needs no marker, no new phase and no new key. It sits in `upkeep`, never in `fileAttempts`: `upkeep` runs on every ready pass, while `fileAttempts` runs only when policy planned an attempt, and after deploy policy plans none for the duplicated keys (MK3 pins this). **One full rewrite, once.** The first application takes run 728's full projection fallback, because attempt rows leave and its diff cannot express a row leaving (see run 728's applier entry, D-6166, which is not edited here; 728's R6 pins that an attempt leaving through `applyCheckpointChange` returns `mode: 'full'`, and C-K4 pins it for this change). That is one full rewrite, once, for the stock (about 510 rows at the last measurement, most in one group). After that no attempt leaves, because policy files no same-key duplicate, so every later pass's pre-check finds `removed === 0` and makes no `applyCheckpointChange` call in `upkeep` (C-K3). The collapse adds no `commitLocked` caller (`applyCheckpointChange` is already among them), no port member, store method, checkpoint key or migration. **The guards.** `applyCheckpointChange`'s guards refuse a moved position, a removed session, a changed natural-key map, a changed activity identity and a changed lease or lineage, and say nothing about attempts, so a change that removes attempt rows passes; measured on the base before the code (no STOP), and by C-K4 and C-K5 on the real applier. The comment on `DelegationCheckpointChange` (`delegation-checkpoint.ts`) now names the second checkpoint-only change, the only edit in that file. **The fault behaviour** (C-K5): a collapse faulted at `checkpoint-rename` (before) leaves all three rows on file, `recover()` keeps them, and the next pass collapses them to the earliest; a collapse that landed survives `recover()` (the attempts equal the collapsed list). **R1(b)'s re-pointed injected attempt** (D-6190): under the base it was a same-key duplicate of the template, which the collapse would remove on a later pass; it now carries another decision key. No pass runs after the injection in R1(b) or R22 (the injection is in the last pass and the collapse runs before `reconcile`), so the re-point is defensive and R22 was left as it is. **The five rows for the collapse** (at base `7da6498a7`):

  | row | pins | at base |
  |---|---|---|
  | C-K1 | (model) five rows of one key with different evidence, `attemptedAt` out of ID order and two sharing the earliest instant, unrelated rows before, between and after (nine in) → removed 4, the kept row the earliest with the tie to the smaller ID, unrelated rows untouched and in order | RED `collapseDelegationAttempts is exported by the model: expected 'undefined' to be 'function'`; MK1 and MC11 red it, and MC3, MC4, MC7a, MC7b |
  | C-K2 | (model) `it.each` over the eight key fields: two rows differing only in that field → the SAME array (`toBe`), removed 0, the keys differ | RED (the function is absent, same message); MK6 and MC3 to MC7c red their case |
  | C-K3 | (pass timing, on the D-5834 `replay` rig) a steady pass with attempts on file, no repeated key, and an interval upkeep with nothing to do (`upkeepDelegationIntervals(…).changed === false` at the armed pass's own instant) → zero `applyCheckpointChange` calls between the `upkeep` and `prune` announcements, the revision unchanged, the attempts' keys distinct | green by design (no collapse exists); MK2 reds it (also MC9) |
  | C-K4 | (watch) three same-key unresolved rows seeded through `applyCheckpointChange`, then a census pass that plans no attempt → one row remains (the earliest), the removing change ran in `upkeep`, one warn line names 2 rows (`/delegation attempts: 2 row\(s\)/`), that application's delta projection answered `mode: 'full'` (spied around the `applyCheckpointChange` wrapper), pass 2 makes zero calls | RED `one row remains: the earliest of the decision`: all three remain; MK1, MK2, MK3, MC11 red it |
  | C-K5 | (watch) the faulted and the clean collapse above; the fault is injected through `DelegationDurability.open`'s `onEdge` at `checkpoint-rename` (before), gated on the last announced phase being `upkeep` | RED `the pass's upkeep reached the rename and was faulted: expected 0 to be 1`; MK1, MK3, MC11 red it, **MK2 does not** |

  C-K5 departs from the text's recipe: it calls the applier's `recover()` directly after the faulted pass, because a lane pass that recovers also runs the upkeep and would collapse in the same pass, hiding the "three rows still on file after recovery" step.

  **The collapse's mutants (D-6190 has the others), measured:**

  | mutant | edit | red (measured) |
  |---|---|---|
  | MK1 | the collapse groups by `attemptId` | C-K1, C-K4, C-K5 |
  | MK2 | the upkeep's `removed > 0` pre-check dropped (`if (true)`) | C-K3, C-K4, C-W1; also D-5834 R1 (a), R1 (b), D-5835 R1 (ii), D-5836 R1 (d), R22 (a), (b), (c); **C-K5 stays green** |
  | MK3 | the collapse is moved out of `upkeep` into the top of `fileAttempts` | C-K4, C-K5 |
  | MK4 | the closure returns a fresh array even when `removed === 0` | nothing red in the policy, model, pass-timing or watch files |
  | MK5 | the closure removes rows by the pre-read snapshot, not recomputed on `cp` | nothing red in the same four files |
  | MK6 | `collapseDelegationAttempts` always returns a fresh array | the eight C-K2 cases |

  **Declared equivalents.** *MK4:* `applyCheckpointChange`'s equal-bytes compare writes nothing when the closure returns an equal checkpoint, and the upkeep's pre-check prevents the call at all when there is nothing to remove; where the closure is reached with nothing to remove, the compare absorbs a fresh array. *MK5:* attempts have ONE writer, this lane's `fileAttempts`, in `reconcile`, after `upkeep`, serialized on the queue, and the intent route's change never touches attempts, so the snapshot and `cp` hold the same attempts; equivalent **until a second attempt writer exists**, and then it is a defect: this entry is the place to re-open it. **Disagreements with the text's table, measured:** MK2 does not red C-K5 (the text says "MK2 and MK3 red it"): C-K5 seeds duplicates, so the pre-check is true whether or not it exists; MK3 reds C-K5, and C-K3 and C-K4 carry MK2; C-W1 also reds MK2 (its zero-change count). "One line of diff" is not exact for MK3 (a block moved) and MK5 (two edits). **The wave-done facts that belong here:** the `upkeep` phase's code delta is the collapse block, +11 net code lines in `watch.ts` (D-6190 has the growth check); `fileAttempts`' docstring and the `upkeep` comment count zero. Commit `196a9552f` (the collapse), after `d9243ee16` (the model's functions it calls).

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 7b (run 762, block D-6267..D-6276, taken in order):

- **D-6267** — `a-bash-call-opens-only-on-a-changed-listing` (wave-3 follow-up 7b, part (a); PR #1115): **The defect.** Before this change a Bash PreToolUse or PostToolUse that carried a worktree `listing` was a qualifying join, so every listed Bash call opened a permanent `bash-worktree` activity at its first listing-carrying record, whether or not the call changed anything. Measured on copies by the soak watcher (mail 7561, snapshot 2026-10-21T06:43:48Z): 84,302 activities, of which bash-worktree active 1,397 and ended 74,800; over the whole journal the listed Bash Pre/Post pairs were 73,841 equal against 921 different (Pre with no Post 1,397, Post with no Pre 38), so 98.8% never changed the listing; the ended bash rows (74,800) match the listed pairs (74,762), read as one row per listed call (an observation, not a measured cause). The checkpoint was 70.8 MB (activities 41.9 MB, natural-key map 15.3 MB). The second measurement (mail 7598, 07:35:58Z): 75,657 equal pairs against 937 different. **The rulings (operator, 2026-10-21), verbatim.** Ruling 1, **"Open only on a change"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: a server fold rule: a Bash call opens an activity only when its worktree listing changed (or a listing is unmeasured); the hook is unchanged; a one-line spec §5.2 amendment). Ruling 4, **"Ask the soak watcher"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: measure first, on copies). Ruling 5, **"1 hour (Recommended)"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: `DELEGATION_BASH_PENDING_MS` = 1 hour (`3_600_000`): a listing-carrying Bash PreToolUse waits that long, in journal acceptance time, for its PostToolUse). Ruling 7, **"Accept and document (Recommended)"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: after a rollback past this change, an older build's delegation recovery refuses `fold-refused` and the lane stays `reconstructing` (re-reading the journal every 5 min) until roll-forward; the journal and checkpoint lose nothing, and a roll-forward within the 24 h read horizon (`DELEGATION_SPOOL_LOOKBACK_HOURS`) ingests every spool line written meanwhile; a longer rollback loses the delegation evidence of the span past that horizon, as any lane outage of that length does). Ruling 8, **"Split: attempt fix first"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: the attempt fix is carved into run 740; the rest of this follow-up follows on top of it as a separate run: this one). Rulings 2, 3 and 6 are quoted in D-6268, D-6269 and D-6270.

  **Rule A, as built** (`delegation-checkpoint.ts`). `namedAgentsOf` names a Bash call with `join: false` always (its `ends` is unchanged: a PostToolUse still ends a mapped call); whether the call opens is decided across its two records by the builder. On an UNMAPPED key, `foldBashCall`: a listing-carrying PreToolUse creates a `'pre'` fact (its `journalId`, `observedAt` and acceptance instant are the identity and the ageing origin) and pushes a due entry; a second listing-carrying PreToolUse keeps the FIRST identity and acceptance instant and takes the LATEST listing (as the window builder keeps the last), `lastObservedAt` the max; a PreToolUse without a listing pends nothing (D-4649); a PostToolUse finding a `'pre'` deletes it and opens nothing when its listing is set-equal (`delegationListingsEqual`), else opens with the PreToolUse's identity, ended at the PostToolUse (also when the PostToolUse carries no listing); a PostToolUse carrying a listing and finding no `'pre'` opens with itself as identity (D-4649; also after the PreToolUse aged out); a PostToolUse with neither opens nothing. Any opening on a covered record refuses `fold-refused`, as the agent join does. **Row parity for a delta call:** the old rule opened at the PreToolUse and touched the row at the PostToolUse; rule A opens at the PostToolUse from the PreToolUse's identity, so `activityId`, `identityJournalId`, `openedAt`, `lastEventAt` and `endedAt` are field for field the old row's (A2). On a MAPPED key, after the unchanged mapping-first branch, `stepMappedBashFact`: a listing-carrying PreToolUse whose `journalId` is the row's `identityJournalId` creates `'pre'`; a set-equal PostToolUse then makes it `'equal'` (`preJournalId`); any other record deletes it (spoiled). It never opens, refuses or touches a row. The start rule is the identity check, so a rule-A row (its fact spoiled by the PostToolUse that opened it, or by a second PreToolUse) never reaches `'equal'`: only an old-rule row can. **The map** (`DelegationBashCallFact`, kinds the single words `'pre'` and `'equal'`, so `mail-routes.test.ts`'s kebab scan of `server/src/coord` meets no new word): ONE builder-private map keyed by `encodeDelegationNaturalKey`, never encoded; recovery rebuilds it from the retained journal like the candidates. `clone()` copies it (and its due heap) as new collections; `adoptRows` and `replaceRows` keep it (a checkpoint-only change cannot add or drop a key). UNMAPPED `'pre'` entries are `identity-candidate` holds (no new hold reason, so an older build decodes the file; spec §5.12: their identity event precedes the opening); a mapped key needs none. Accessors: `bashCallFactOf`, `bashCallFactCount`, `pendingBashCallCount` (unmapped `'pre'` only, read by D-6271's `pendingBash`). **The bound** (`delegation-model.ts`): `DELEGATION_BASH_PENDING_MS = 3_600_000`, written as the literal (derived (not an operator ruling); `single-definition` does not red on it as a copy of `DELEGATION_HOUR_MS`, measured), placed after `DELEGATION_CURSOR_RETENTION_HOURS`; `delegationListingsEqual` (SET semantics, exactly correlation's zero delta, so every window rung 4 can call `mine` is an unequal one) before `THE OBSERVATION HEALTH` marker, the one definition (RB25's census). Mail 7598's gaps under the bound: equal pairs p99 208 s, max 884 s, none at 20 min or more; different pairs p50 70 s, p99 624 s, max 2,868 s, one at 20 min or more, none at 1 h; so no equal pair is kept from retirement by ageing, and no changed pair loses its PreToolUse identity.

  **The due heap** (derived (not an operator ruling)): at the first boot the observe pass builds about 74k mapped `'equal'` facts that stay until the door's first run, so an ageing loop over the whole map on every record would make recovery about records × facts (about 250k × 75k). Ageing pops due entries (a binary heap ordered by due instant, then key) and never scans; the heap is a cache only: every pop re-reads the map entry, and a `'pre'` entry's acceptance instant never changes, so a stale entry (from a deleted and re-created `'pre'`) fails the predicate and is skipped. RB-COST pins it: measured at the tip, 0 entries yielded by the Bash map's iterators over 65,001 records (20,000 equal pairs and 5,000 PreToolUse-only calls covered, then 20,000 fillers), the 20,000 equal facts remaining; a scan per record would yield about 1.3e9 (MA-scan reds it).

  **The fold order, stated once** (`foldEvidence`, both modes): 1. `ageStopCandidates(at)` BEFORE naming (D-6270); 2. `touchSession` (replay only, as before); 3. `indexWorkflowLaunch` and 4. `foldNotification` (D-6269); 5. the naming loop (mapping first; a mapped Bash name steps its fact; an unmapped Bash name goes to `foldBashCall`; agent and workflow names as before, with D-6270's two candidate fields); 6. `ageBashCalls(at)` AFTER naming. `at` is the record's `acceptedAt`, parsed once; nothing reads `observedAt` or a clock for ageing. **Why the two ageing positions differ, and why both are safe:** a stop-only candidate ages BEFORE naming, so a join accepted at exactly the horizon opens from its own record and is active (the stale stop's end is no longer attached); a pending Bash call ages AFTER naming, so its own PostToolUse at exactly the bound still decides the call by its listings (A11c). Both read only `record.acceptedAt`, at a fixed position, in both modes, so the builder's state at every sequence is the same function of the records before it, live and in recovery.

  **Mixed-journal parity.** An old-rule checkpoint's listed Bash keys are all mapped (the old rule opened any call with a listing on either side), so the mapping-first branch takes every one of their records and rule A never meets them; the covered-join refusal in `foldBashCall` is reachable only from a PostToolUse that opens, i.e. a key no checkpoint maps, which an old-rule checkpoint cannot cover with a listing on either side. Rung 4's `mine ⊆ opened`: rung 4 links only through `soleAdded`, which an equal window never is (RB16), and every unequal call opens (A17). **Derived (not an operator ruling):** a call with no listing on either side, `truncated` included, opens nothing: a `truncated` flag does not prove the hook attempted a listing (a line already truncated for another field never attempts one), and no per-line unmeasured marker exists; measured 0 (mail 7561, item 4). D-4649 is kept: a PostToolUse with no pending listing-carrying PreToolUse is its call's identity record, now also after the PreToolUse ages out.

  **The empty-checkpoint replay hazard.** After a LOST checkpoint, a recovery from nothing would re-derive a leased pre-change Bash call's ID from its PostToolUse when that PostToolUse was accepted at least `DELEGATION_BASH_PENDING_MS` after its PreToolUse, and `foldLease` would then refuse. Measured vacuous for today's stock: the one live lease is an agent's (mail 7598), and no changed pair had a gap of 1 h or more. A Bash call first opened after this change takes its identity under the new rule, so a from-nothing replay re-derives the same ID. The residual case is a lease minted after this change on an old-rule ACTIVE row whose changed-listing PostToolUse arrives `DELEGATION_BASH_PENDING_MS` or more after its PreToolUse (1,383 such PreToolUses were 1 h old or more at 07:35Z): unobserved, not impossible; it needs a lost checkpoint to matter, and it stays wave 4's (RE5 keeps its exclusion). **Ruling 7's rollback hazard:** rule A alone makes an older build refuse `fold-refused` once any listing-carrying Bash call that rule A did not open is covered (its fold reads that call's covered listing-carrying PreToolUse, equal, still pending or forgotten, as an unmapped join; the wider wording is the coordinator's ruling on review 767); the lane stays `reconstructing`, re-reading the journal every 5 min, until roll-forward; the journal and checkpoint lose nothing, and a roll-forward within the 24 h read horizon (`DELEGATION_SPOOL_LOOKBACK_HOURS`) ingests every spool line written meanwhile; a longer rollback loses the delegation evidence of the span past that horizon, as any lane outage of that length does. Also in the PR body's Rollback section and the release note line (the PR title). **Wave 4:** compaction must never prune a Bash call's PreToolUse while keeping its PostToolUse (the PostToolUse would then open with itself as identity, and a covered one refuses); `planDelegationPruning`'s contiguous-prefix bound can fall between the two today and nothing acts on it in wave 3 (review of part (b)). `computeHolds` holds nothing for a consumed pair: once a PostToolUse has decided its PreToolUse (a rule-A equal pair never mapped, or a retired one), no hold names either record, so the hold that keeps them together is wave 4's to add (the coordinator's ruling on review 767). The code comments that say nothing such a call named can open again now say so only while both of its records are retained, and spec §5.12's sentence covers every listing-carrying Bash call, not only the zero-delta class. **The spec** (§5.2) gains the paragraph on when a Bash `tool_use_id` opens (amended 2026-10-21, ruling "open only on a change", D-6267; D-4649). It said a pending PreToolUse is forgotten "until a journal record is accepted `DELEGATION_BASH_PENDING_MS` after it"; the code ages on EVIDENCE records only (a `lease-created` record ages nothing), in both modes alike, and after naming, so a PostToolUse at or past the bound still finds its PreToolUse pending. **Resolved:** the spec was corrected to the code's wording (the coordinator's ruling on review 767); evidence-only ageing can only over-retain, never forget early.

  **Declared equivalents.** *MA20* (`namedAgentsOf` keeps `Array.isArray(event.listing)` as the Bash join): `foldBashCall` and `stepMappedBashFact` read the event kind, the listing and the map, never `na.join`, and `namedAgentsOf` has one caller in the fold; fold-equivalent, and A19 pins the documented contract. *MA-replaceRows-mapped* (`replaceRows` deletes every `'pre'` whose key the change mapped): `applyCheckpointChange` refuses any added key and any dropped or re-mapped one, so no key goes from unmapped to mapped across `replaceRows`.

  **The rows** (26, in `delegation-bash-change.test.ts` unless named; each title begins with its id; base = `bc231bbcb`, run on the rows commit `51a80ae60` before any code). Re-pointed existing rows, each keeping its teeth: A19 is `delegation-correlation.test.ts`'s `namedAgentsOf` row, whose two listing-carrying Bash lines now expect `join: false` (a Bash record is never a join; the listing-less case was already `join: false`); the three correlation titles that said a listing alone opens or joins were retitled with their assertions unchanged (the pair under "one that carries a listing opens" is a delta pair, and the unmeasured PostToolUse under "ends the activity its PreToolUse opened" opens with the PreToolUse's identity). Strengthened after the mutation run (a departure from the text's row, measured): A21 accepted its bare agent occurrence one second after the pending PreToolUse, so at the bound the occurrence was younger than the bound and MA-shared-map-sweep stayed green; the occurrence is now accepted at the PreToolUse's own instant, and the mutant reds A21. `delegationListingWindowsOf` is `watch.ts`'s, not correlation's (A17 imports it from there).

  **The mutants** (27). The whole table, 125 mutants, was run once against the tip `ae6d5de45`, each alone in one scratch copy of the tree restored after each, with an outer `timeout 900` per file; none timed out, the longest file run 60 s; 119 red and the 6 green are declared equivalents. **FR1** (the coordinator's ruling on review 767) added RB9b and RB9c, which red MB7 and MB8, and three scalar mutants of the clone census (MCL-s, D-6268), each run alone in a root-disk copy of the FR1 tree against the whole file; the declared equivalents still green are four: MA-replaceRows-mapped, MB27, MW-EQ1 and ME15. Three mutants first stayed green and their rows were strengthened (A21, D-SC7, RE5b, above), then re-run red; MS4 was re-run after D-SC7's change and reds it; the declared equivalents were re-run green after it.

- **D-6268** — `a-zero-delta-bash-call-is-retired` (wave-3 follow-up 7b, part (b); PR #1115): **Ruling 2 (operator, 2026-10-21), verbatim:** **"Retire that class"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: a narrow O4 amendment: ended, unleased, zero-delta bash-worktree rows and their natural-key map entries leave the checkpoint, once and then ongoing; must ship with ruling 1). The amendment itself is the coordinator's O4 amendment in the programme ledger (cited, never edited here). Rulings 1, 4, 5, 7 and 8 are quoted in D-6267.

  **The predicate, as built** (`DelegationCheckpointBuilder.retirableZeroDeltaEntries`, sorted by activity ID): a mapped key whose fact is `'equal'`, whose row exists, is `bash-worktree`, is `ended`, has `identityJournalId === preJournalId`, and is named by no lease row (released or not) and no lineage row (open or closed). `retireZeroDeltaBash` deletes the row, its mapping entry and its fact together, and is the ONE builder code that deletes from the mapping or the activities (RB18's census). It touches nothing else: no session (the change door's session refusal stays meaningful), attempt, lease, lineage, candidate, cursor, interval or applied position. The identity validator needs no relaxation, because row and mapping leave together, and `build()` re-runs it, so a door that dropped only one of the pair throws `natural-key-map`. **The gap condition** is enforced by D-6267's ageing of mapped `'pre'` facts: a call whose PreToolUse aged before its PostToolUse folded never gets an `'equal'` fact, and ageing is the same function of the records live and in recovery, so the door retires only a call recovery will also read as zero-delta. That suffices: an `'equal'` fact needs the PostToolUse folded, so a retired call's two records are both at or below the applied position, and any record after the decision would have spoiled the fact.

  **The door** (`DelegationDurability.retireZeroDeltaBashActivities`, after `pruneBucketCursors`, in its pattern): enqueue, `requireReady`, probe the builder; nothing retirable answers `{ retired: 0, applied: null }` with no clone, build, encode or write (RB26); otherwise ONE `commitLocked({ items: [], mutate, force: false })`, no `verifyDecodable` (the rows come from the builder) and no `project: false` (O5 step 8 runs). It never goes through `applyCheckpointChange`, whose O4 refusals stay byte-identical (RB17). It is one more `commitLocked` caller beside those run 728's applier entry lists (that entry is not edited). With run 728's delta, activity keys leave, so its diff answers `applicable: false`, the store runs the full rewrite and answers `{ mode: 'full' }`, and the next application deltas again (RB23, RE4). **The lease guard:** `'zero-delta-call'` joins `DelegationLeaseRefusalReason` and its total table (`isDelegationCheckpointKebab` admits it), checked in `validateLeaseRequest` after the source-kind check and before `randomUUID` (RB15); never in `foldLease`, where a refusing journal record would brick recovery. The structural guard stands as well: rung 4's `mine` comes only from `soleAdded`, which an equal window never is (RB16). **The lane** (`watch.ts`): `lastRetireHour` beside `lastPruneHour`, `retireZeroDelta` beside `pruneCursors`, called inside the existing `prune` phase right after `pruneCursors`; at most once per UTC hour, the hour spent BEFORE the attempt (N6), a failure warned (`ccrc-server: the delegation zero-delta retirement failed: …`) and retried at most once an hour; the slow line carries `retired` (D-6271). No new phase key, no report field.

  **The first pass after deploy.** The deploy's restart runs recovery, whose observe pass computes every `'equal'` fact; the latch starts at -1, so the first lane pass retires the backlog: about 73,841 rows (the equal pairs of mail 7561, fewer any whose gap reached the bound and any leased one), more by deploy day (mail 7598 counted 75,657 equal pairs, none with a gap of 20 min or more). That application takes run 728's full projection fallback once. When run 740's build has already run on the box, it is the first pass's only full rewrite (run 740's collapse, D-6191, took its own then and finds nothing now); when one rollout carries both builds, the first pass pays two separate full rewrites, the collapse's in `upkeep` and then the door's in `prune`, which cannot share an application (the change door's O4 guard refuses this door's drops). **The ongoing arm:** under rule A a mapped key gets an `'equal'` fact only for an old-rule row whose equal PostToolUse lands within the bound of its PreToolUse, so about one bound past the deploy the door idles for good while the hourly probe keeps running; an idle door is not a fault.

  **B needs A** (the parity argument). After retirement, recovery's observe pass meets every retained record that can name a retired key K, and none is a qualifying join: (a) K's covered PreToolUse becomes an unmapped `'pre'`; (b) K's covered equal PostToolUse deletes it and opens nothing, which the gap condition guarantees (the door retired only a call whose `'pre'` had not aged before the PostToolUse folded, and ageing is the same function of the records in live and in recovery); (c) a listing-less record is no join, and it would have spoiled K; (d) a redelivered line is skipped as a `duplicate-event` (RB19); (e) a genuinely new line naming K is judged by rule A on the unmapped path, live and in replay alike, and at worst opens a fresh ID (derived (not an operator ruling): nothing references a retired ID); (f) no suffix `lease-created` can name a retired activity: `validateLeaseRequest` answers `unmeasured-activity` once the row is gone; (g) agent and workflow records, and the metas, carry other keys. RB3, RE5 and RB-PAR pin it; MB-noA (rule A reverted) reds them.

  **Nothing new of these kinds:** no new port member, store method, SQL writer, durability edge, checkpoint key, hold reason, journal entry kind or SQLite migration; the checkpoint stays v1, read by the existing strict decoder (RE6). The one new kebab word is `zero-delta-call`. **Derived (not an operator ruling):** the stale active old-rule rows (1,397 at 06:43Z; at 07:35Z, 1,451 listed PreToolUses without a PostToolUse, 1,383 of them at 1 h or more) are not retired: ruling 2 names ended rows; their mapped `'pre'` facts age out; wave 4's `unknown` writer owns them. **Derived (not an operator ruling):** a new line naming a retired `tool_use_id` opens a fresh ID. **The `prunableDelegationDropped` re-argument:** its dropped-key prune (`pruneDropped`) reads only `agent` and `workflow` activity rows and the census's agent and run names, never a `bash-worktree` row, so the retired rows' absence changes nothing it decides (RB24 compares its output with and without the bash rows); `parentsNamedBy` is unaffected (rung 3's `parent-cwd` evidence always names the agent identity). **Ruling 7's hazard, widened to the whole stock:** an older build that recovers a checkpoint from which the door retired rows meets their covered listing-carrying PreToolUses as unmapped joins and refuses `fold-refused`, as it does once any listing-carrying Bash call that rule A did not open is covered (D-6267; the wider wording is the coordinator's ruling on review 767); the journal and checkpoint lose nothing, and a roll-forward within the 24 h read horizon (`DELEGATION_SPOOL_LOOKBACK_HOURS`) ingests every spool line written meanwhile; a longer rollback loses the delegation evidence of the span past that horizon, as any lane outage of that length does. **Wave 4:** never retain a listing-carrying Bash call's PostToolUse without its PreToolUse, retired, zero-delta or not (spec §5.12, widened from "such a call" by the coordinator's ruling on review 767); B needs A holds only while both of a retired call's records are retained, and no hold keeps them together today (D-6267); the released-lease proof obligation stands unchanged. **The spec** (§5.12) gains the "Zero-delta Bash calls" bullet (amended 2026-10-21, ruling "retire that class", D-6268).

  **Declared equivalents.** *MB5* (the `ended` conjunct removed, ME11): a comparable fact needs the key's PostToolUse folded, every Bash PostToolUse has `ends: true`, and folding it on a mapped key sets `ended`; nothing writes `unknown`, so it is equivalent on reachable states, pinned anyway by the fabricated RB6 for wave 4's `unknown` writer. *MB7* and *MB8* are no longer declared equivalents (FR1, the coordinator's ruling on review 767): each is equivalent on reachable states (MB7: the start rule is the identity check, so only an old-rule row reaches `'equal'`, and its `preJournalId` is the row's `identityJournalId` whenever the predicate reads it; MB8: facts exist only under keys encoded with `bash-worktree`, and the identity validator ties the row mapped from K to K's natural key), but a fabricated-state row now pins each, as RB6 pins MB5, so a later edit that made the state reachable reds: RB9b sets an `'equal'` fact whose `preJournalId` is not the row's identity, RB9c an `'equal'` fact under the ended agent row's key, and each asserts the door retires nothing and writes nothing. Both red under their mutant and nowhere else in the recovery file (measured, below). *MB27* (the door reports the probe's length, not the mutate's count): with `items: []`, `commitLocked` plans no record, so `next = this.builder.clone()` folds nothing before `mutate`; probe and mutate read identical state in one queue turn. *ME15* (retire before `pruneCursors`): `dropBucketCursors` reads and writes only cursors and intervals; the retirement reads rows, mapping, leases, lineage and facts, and writes rows and mapping; each commit is serialized.

  **The rows** (36: the 34 of the rows commit and FR1's RB9b and RB9c; the rows commit `51a80ae60`'s message counts 99 rows over the whole block, where the tables held 98: the 99th is RB25's two `RB25 census` tests counted as a row of their own, and RB25's 8 cases include them; `delegation-recovery.test.ts` unless named; legacy stock only from the one helper `oldRuleCheckpoint`, `server/test/delegationOldRuleFixture.ts`, which RE0 pins against the base fold by digest). Departures from the text's rows, each measured: RB7 (and RE1, RE4) write the `lease-created` record by building it with the journal module's record builder and appending it to the fixture journal after the helper's applied position, then let `recover()` replay it; RB19 applies K1's original records through `applyAccepted` and asserts both skip as `duplicate-event` with no record, row, mapping, fact or revision change (the lane re-read with the cursor removed is not reproduced); RB20 also compares the faulted applier's own builder with the on-disk checkpoint on the pre-rename faults, without which MB23 stays green; RB-PAR runs six fixed seeds (101, 202, 303, 404, 505, 606) of 16 steps each, the twin re-opened through `recover()` after every step; RB26 spies `canonicalDelegationJson` through the model namespace and also asserts `commitMeasures()` did not move; RB21 does not prime the lane (with the stock on disk its first pass is already its first in the hour, and a prime at `PRIMED_AT` would spend the slot), and reads the revision just before and after the door call. RB18's method tracker covers every class of `delegation-checkpoint.ts` (the integration test's tracker it copies covers `DelegationDurability` only).

  **The mutants** (29 lettered plus the soleAdded mutant, and the 23 of the clone census, RB-CLONE's: MCL(F) aliases each of the 17 builder collections in `clone()`, MCL-r(F) resets each of the six journal-derived fields in `replaceRows`; FR1 adds three, MCL-s). **RB-CLONE's scalars are derived from the instance** since FR1 (the coordinator's ruling on review 767), as its collections are: every own field that is not a Map, an Array or a plain object; the fixture moves each off a new builder's value (its checkpoint is at revision 1), so a scalar `clone()` forgot reads back as the default and reds. No scalar is deliberately left uncopied (the row's `NOT_COPIED` table is empty, and names its reason if one ever is).

- **D-6269** — `workflow-run-ends-on-its-task-notification` (wave-3 follow-up 7b, part (d); PR #1115): **The defect.** `namedAgentsOf` ended a Workflow run only when its launch status was `completed`, and live launches are always async, so every run stayed active for good: at 06:43Z (mail 7561) the checkpoint held 370 workflow rows, none ended; at 07:35Z (mail 7598) 388 launches, 378 matched by a completed task notification and 10 unmatched; the 386 checkpoint workflow rows were all RUNS (no worker row exists yet), and 92 of them had ids that themselves end in `-<digits>`. The hook already journals the run's end, a UserPromptSubmit carrying `notification: { taskId, toolUseId, status }`; nothing read it. The hook and the ingest are unchanged. **Ruling 3 (operator, 2026-10-21), verbatim:** **"Separate follow-up now"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: a small follow-up after run 728, carrying the confirmed defect fixes (async workflow runs never ending, start-less SubagentStop candidates never ageing out, attempt re-filing), before wave 4). Under ruling 8 the attempt re-filing fix is run 740's (D-6190, D-6191).

  **As built** (`delegation-checkpoint.ts`). **The launch index** (`workflowLaunches`, both fold modes, every evidence record): keyed by (session, incarnation, tool-use id), first launch wins (D-WF15), each entry the run's encoded key, run id and task id; builder state only, never persisted and never a row, rebuilt from the retained journal like the candidates, hundreds of entries. One pure guard, `workflowLaunchRunId(e)`, serves both `namedAgentsOf`'s Workflow branch and the index, so an indexed launch is always a qualifying join: it opened its run in replay, or refused in observe. **The notification fold** (`foldNotification`, both modes, before the naming loop): a UserPromptSubmit whose tool-use id and task id are an indexed launch's; a terminal status (`completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`) ends the run and its workers in the same step, `endedAt` the notification's `observedAt` clamped to `openedAt` (D-WF14) and `lastEventAt` the max; `unknown` moves `lastEventAt` only (the model calls `unknown` a measurement state, and a paused workflow is not ended); agents are never touched (their notifications fire on every stop, and the index holds Workflow launches only); there is no pending-notification state, so a notification journaled before its launch is ignored (D-WF8). A run row missing behind an indexed launch refuses `fold-refused`, reachable only for a row that was lost, which the launch's own covered-join refusal already reports; a notification never refuses a checkpoint the launch fold accepted. **The worker predicate is exact:** a worker of the run is a row whose upstream id is exactly the run's id plus `-` plus a canonical decimal (`isWorkerIndex`, now the one definition `openWorkflowWorkerFromMeta` shares, its behaviour unchanged for every input), under the same parent and incarnation; never a `-<n>` suffix test, since live run ids themselves end in digits (D-WF2 (iii): ending `wf_aaaaaaaa-51` never ends `wf_aaaaaaaa-512`, nor the reverse). A run whose own id were another run's id plus `-<decimal>` would read as that run's worker; the observed ids never nest that way. The worker scan is O(activities) per matched terminal notification, tens an hour. A worker opened after its run ended copies the ended run (D-WF3).

  **The observe contract, restated:** `observeRecord` edits no row except through a covered task notification, which ends its run and that run's workers: idempotent on a checkpoint built since the rule existed (ending happens once, `lastEventAt` is a max) and a backfill on one built before it; D-WF13 pins that no other covered event edits a row. **The recovery report** gains `coveredEnds` (the rows the covered-notification fold changed; 0 on every failure return, the save's included), `needsSave` includes `coveredEnds > 0`, and once the save is durable one warn line says `ccrc-server: delegation recovery changed N activity row(s) from covered task notifications` (it counts rows changed, ended or only `lastEventAt` moved; the coordinator's ruling on review 767). The backfill reaches SQLite through recovery's full repair (run 728 keeps recovery full), so at the first boot the open workflow rows whose launch has a matching task notification end, with no one-time pass and no marker (derived (not an operator ruling): yes, through recovery): 378 of 388 launches at 07:35Z; the 10 unmatched stay active until a notification is journaled. Live ends are updates of non-key columns, which run 728's delta takes. **No reader reacts to a run ending** (measured by the review): nothing outside the delegation model, the checkpoint and the schema reads `executionState`, and wave 3 releases no lease, so ending the backlog of runs at the first boot triggers no reader. **Wave 4:** a mapped run needs no pruning hold, so an active run's launch record can be pruned while the run is still running, after which recovery would ignore its notification; hold an active run's launch record, or persist its ids, before pruning. **The spec** (§5.11): workflow-run-ended evidence is the task notification whose tool-use id and task id are the run's launch's, with a terminal status.

  **Declared equivalent.** *MW-EQ1* (workers ended only when the run itself transitions): a run reaches `ended` only by a `completed` launch (which precedes every worker) or by this rule (which ends the workers in the same step); workers open copying the run, and nothing reopens a row. Equivalent until a second run-ending path exists.

  **The rows** (16; `delegation-correlation.test.ts` unless named). Departures, measured: D-WF5's `unknown` case is red at the base too (it asserts the notification's `lastEventAt`, which the base never reads), where the text says only the three terminal words are; D-WF10 (b) compares the run row alone, since from the checkpoint after the session start alone no worker row can exist (workers open only through the meta door).

- **D-6270** — `a-start-less-stop-ages-out` (wave-3 follow-up 7b, part (e); PR #1115): **The defect.** An identity-candidate hold exists per agent key that no join opened, and a SubagentStart is a join, so the held candidates are start-less stops and bare occurrences, not agent starts: at 06:43Z (mail 7561) 7,732 holds, 7,725 of them identity-candidate; at 07:35Z (mail 7598) 7,890 identity-candidate holds, 7,732 stop-only owners (SubagentStop) and 158 bare occurrences (157 on a Bash PreToolUse, 1 on an Agent PreToolUse), the stop-only ones aged under 1 h 220, 1-48 h 5,465, over 48 h 2,047; and of the 7,732 agents first named by a SubagentStop, 0 were ever named later by a SubagentStart (no stop-to-start gap exists). They pin wave 4's prunable prefix for good. **Ruling 3 (operator, 2026-10-21), verbatim:** **"Separate follow-up now"** (quoted in full in D-6269). **Ruling 6, verbatim:** **"48 hours (Recommended)"** (the coordinator's summary of the option chosen: `DELEGATION_STOP_CANDIDATE_HORIZON_MS` = 48 h, derived as `DELEGATION_CURSOR_RETENTION_HOURS * DELEGATION_HOUR_MS`).

  **As built.** `DelegationActivityCandidate` gains `stopOnly` (true while every record that named it is a SubagentStop) and `lastAcceptedAt` (the latest naming record's `acceptedAt`: journal data, never a clock read); a new candidate sets them, an update ANDs `stopOnly` and takes the max, and only a NEW stop-only candidate pushes a due entry onto the shared due heap (D-6267's). `ageStopCandidates(at)` runs at the TOP of `foldEvidence`, before naming, in both modes: it pops every due entry at or before `at`, drops one whose candidate is gone or no longer stop-only, deletes one whose last naming is at least the horizon before `at`, and re-files one whose last naming moved at its new due instant (later than `at`, so the loop ends). `DELEGATION_STOP_CANDIDATE_HORIZON_MS = DELEGATION_CURSOR_RETENTION_HOURS * DELEGATION_HOUR_MS` (the spool's read horizon plus a day, so no line written before the stop can still be accepted after it), in `delegation-model.ts` after `DELEGATION_CURSOR_RETENTION_HOURS`. Bare occurrences never age (the meta door, `openFromMeta`, needs them); the trigger is global (any owner's record ages any owner's stop-only candidate); `clone()` copies the heap as a new array; it holds at most the stop-only candidates created within the horizon, plus stale entries that pop and drop. `openFromCandidate`'s parameter is `Omit<DelegationActivityCandidate, 'sequence' | 'stopOnly' | 'lastAcceptedAt'>`, the shape rule A's opener and the worker literal build. **The fold order and its two ageing positions** are stated once in D-6267: a stop-only candidate ages BEFORE naming, so a join accepted at exactly the horizon opens from its own record and is active (D-SC4), while a pending Bash call ages after naming; both read only `record.acceptedAt`, at a fixed position, in both modes. Like the Bash ageing, it is triggered by evidence records only (a `lease-created` record ages nothing), which over-retains in the safe direction; live and recovery agree. **Resolved:** the spec said "a journal record" and was corrected to the code's wording, "an evidence record" (the coordinator's ruling on review 767).

  **The existing stock needs no pass.** Candidates are never persisted; the first boot's recovery rebuilds them from the whole retained journal, so the stop-only holds older than the horizon vanish, while stops younger than it and every bare occurrence stay: about 2,047 of the 7,732 at 07:35Z, so the stock settles near 5,700 (mail 7598; inference). A hold the new build no longer computes only stops protecting its record from the next checkpoint on; recovery still requires the persisted checkpoint's holds to be retained, and nothing prunes in wave 3. **The empty-checkpoint replay case:** an agent whose start-less stop preceded its first join by the horizon or more would, replayed from NO checkpoint, take a join-derived ID where the live builder had taken the stop's (a lease naming the old ID would then refuse `foldLease`); MEASURED VACUOUS today: of the 7,732 stop-first agents, 0 ever got a SubagentStart (mail 7598), so no ID re-derives. **It amends W3-M17's hold contract** (the recorded entry stays as it was): the `computeHolds` doc's `identity-candidate` bullet now reads "the earliest retained record naming each AGENT no qualifying join has opened (a bare occurrence, or a start-less stop until `DELEGATION_STOP_CANDIDATE_HORIZON_MS` passes, D-6270), and the first listing-carrying PreToolUse of each PENDING Bash call on an unmapped key". **The spec** (§5.2) gains the start-less-stop paragraph (amended 2026-10-21, D-6270).

  **The rows** (10; `delegation-correlation.test.ts` unless named). Departures, measured: D-SC-COST also asserts that the 5,000 stop-only candidates age out (the 5,000 bare ones remain), which is what makes it red at the base; its tip figures are yielded 0 and 55,000 parses (one per record plus one per popped stop entry, under the ruled bound of 220,000). Strengthened after the mutation run: D-SC7's route (iii) replays its SubagentStart, which ages the stop itself before naming, so MS7 (stop ageing only in replay) stayed green; the row now also recovers the r1-r2 checkpoint over r1-r2 alone, where only the covered record can age the stop, and MS7 reds it.

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 8 (run 768, block D-6321..D-6323):

- **D-6321** — `the-spool-cost-pin-reads-a-same-run-ratio` (wave-3 follow-up 8): **Why.** Wave 3's spool-present cost row in `session-hook.test.ts` ('the delegation spool leaves the hook's other writers alone') asserted the p95 of 20 realistic Bash PostToolUse runs, spool directory and generation present, under an absolute 150 ms. On 2026-10-22 it read 164 ms in a merge-queue run on a slow runner, and the queue ejected an unrelated, docs-only pull request; its three 150 ms siblings (the base p95 pin in 'the fleet gate and failure polarity', the `ccd-tool-adj` row of D-5241 and the agent PostToolUse row of D-5232) have failed full runs at 150-233 ms. Under a merge queue every such flake costs whichever pull request is in the line. **The change.** The row now asserts D-1898's shape with D-3704's estimator: two arms timed in the same run, interleaved one present then one absent, 20 pairs after a warm-up of one of each, each timed with `process.hrtime.bigint()`, and `median(present) / median(absent) < 1.5`. The absent arm is the same payload shape with the owner directory moved aside and back outside the timed region, so the gate's `-d` fails and the spool's jq text is never compiled. Its teeth stay: the warm-up and every present run wrote exactly one line (21 lines, every one `PostToolUse:Bash`, the 20 present `toolUseId`s plus the warm-up's), no absent run wrote one, and the absent arm made no owner directory. Only this row changed; the absolute `toBeLessThan(150)` is gone from it alone. **The measurement** (fleet box, isolated runs of a scratch copy of the row that printed the ratio, 1-minute load per set). Shipped: 15 runs 1.193-1.275 (mean 1.230, load 8.8-9.9) and 15 runs 1.160-1.285 (mean 1.236, load 7.4-8.6); absent-arm medians 33.3-39.3 ms. The task text's default mutation, the spool's jq run a second time (one more jq fork per event): 15 runs 1.463-1.628 (mean 1.570, load 8.8-9.9), best case 15% above the shipped worst (1.285), so no R leaves even 10% on both sides (the best, 1.371, leaves ~7% each side): one extra jq fork does not separate, and the row does not see it. The smallest regression it separates, two more forks of the spool's jq: 15 runs 1.749-1.951 (mean 1.858, load 7.4-8.7). **R = 1.5**: +17% over the shipped worst (1.5/1.285), +17% under that mutation's best (1.749/1.5). **Mutation discipline, measured.** The committed row ran green 15 of 15 isolated runs (load 7.5-8.9); the final scratch copy, byte-identical to it but for the hook path, the bound's source and the ratio's print, ran red 15 of 15 against the two-fork mutant (ratios 1.792-1.928, load 7.6-8.9), each on the ratio assertion. **The masking window.** A ratio is blind to anything that inflates both arms, and blind in one direction to a slowdown of the absent arm alone, which is the hook's whole no-spool path: against the bands, a no-spool regression of >=17% (1.749/1.5) pulls the two-fork mutation's best case back under R, and ~24% (1.858/1.5) a mean mutated run. The base p95 pin is what binds that absolute no-spool cost; this ratio binds the spool's increment over it, and if that pin's budget is ever raised the two must be re-argued together. The three sibling 150 ms rows belong to other plans and stay as they are. The block was appended under the coordinator's ruling on ask 77, with no claim of this run on the plan (claim 2182 is run 762's).

Found in the wave-3 follow-up 9 (run 816, block D-6471..D-6474):

- **D-6471** — `an-unreadable-incarnation-defers-its-hour` (wave-3 follow-up 9): **Why.** The seven-day soak's start checks found that a fleet move can cost the stage gate a whole hour of every live session. During the v0.0.570 tap on 2026-10-22, the fleet box's agent restarted at 17:01:04Z, and one census pass (17:01:43Z to 17:01:45Z) read every live session's registry generation as `unreadable` (`settleParentIncarnations`: a dropped agent round trip is `unreadable`). The lane wrote 36 `incarnation-unmeasured: the parent's registry incarnation is unreadable` rows under the stand-in incarnation, all in the 17:00Z hour, although the passes before and after measured the same sessions under their real incarnations; the v0.0.541 tap on 2026-10-21 left 24 in its 20:00Z hour. An unmeasured row is sticky (R90), so that one pass made 36 of the hour's 76 session-hours unmeasured; at about 40 live sessions the gate's 1% allows about 67 in 168 hours, and two such passes fail the soak. The operator ruled "Fix first, then soak": a session whose registry can't be read in one pass waits for a later pass in the same window, and is marked unmeasured only if no pass reads it before the hour leaves the window. **The behaviour** (`server/src/watch.ts`, the delegation lane; lane memory only). Only `unreadable` may defer; `absent` and `changing` keep the immediate gap. In the pass that lists a session `unreadable` in hour H, H is DEFERRED (owed, in `owed` on the `(id, null)` pending owner, hour -> P) only when both hold: (a) the lane holds a prior incarnation P, taken in `ownersOf` before `lastListed` is replaced, from the first source that holds one: `lastListed`, a pending owner of the same id under a real incarnation, or the real incarnation of the session's latest interval row inside the read horizon in the checkpoint at the pass's start (the source after a restart); two candidates in the deciding source (two pending incarnations, or two incarnations sharing the latest row hour) hold none; and (b) the checkpoint held a (session, P, H) row at the pass's start (the `state` `ownersOf` reads, before any read of the pass is flushed). It is decided per hour: an hour already listed for a gap (in `hours`, not owed: a `changing`/`absent` listing, an `unreadable` one with no P, or a loss not yet durable) is never deferred, and a re-listing keeps a deferral only under the same P. While owed, no stand-in gap is staged for H (`observeOwner`'s null branch skips it, the R102 re-pend included), `forgetRead` never drops it, and it stays a correlation lost window through `pendingWindows`. It leaves the memory by one of: **discharge, with no row**, when a later pass's census lists the session live and measured as P and that observation of (session, P) applied, or skipped as unchanged (the no-op skip), a bucket result for H that is not refused by name and not a carve-out word, completed before any cut, and passed R101's gate (the flush did not fail past its first byte and refused none of that owner's buckets) and R107's (the `(id, null)` owner's `listedBy` is not newer than the discharging pass); non-live reads (R102's, R89's, the checkpoint arm's close read) and carve-out reads (`directory-absent`, `capability-absent`) never discharge; **a loss in that pass** when a census measures J != P (the `(id, null)` owner's `listedBy` moves to that pass), when a re-listing in H finds no P or another, or when H is listed `changing` or `absent`; **a loss at expiry** when H leaves the read horizon still owed (`expirePendingOwners`, saying `unreadable`: see The words). A loss gap leaves the memory only once durable: the J, re-listing and `changing`/`absent` gaps are staged by the null owner's observation in that pass and forgotten by R101's gate; an expired owed hour is kept, in `hours` and in `owed`, until a later pass's `noteGap` finds its row on file (`noteGap` now answers whether it did), so a failed or refused flush stages it again, and then leaves both (review 838 F1). **The words.** Every loss is the stand-in incarnation's `incarnation-unmeasured: the parent's registry incarnation is <reason>`. At expiry an owed hour says `unreadable`, the reason it was deferred under (an hour is owed only under an `unreadable` listing), whatever the owner was listed as since (review 838 F3, ruled); a non-owed listed hour's expiry still takes the `(id, null)` owner's last listed reason (base behaviour, unchanged here; on the wave 4 list). A re-listing with no P or another says `unreadable` (that listing's reason), and a `changing` or `absent` listing ending a deferral says `changing` or `absent`. A J != P loss is staged by the null owner's observation and takes the owner's last listed reason: `unreadable`, unless another hour was listed `changing` or `absent` after the deferral and before J, when it says that word (measured in fix round 1: H owed, H+1 listed `changing`, J in H+1, H's row says `changing`); the coordinator ruled it recorded, not fixed, and it goes on the wave 4 list beside F3's base edge. Every one of these is a loss word, so no count changes. **Restart.** The deferral is never what keeps an hour honest; the durable (session, P, H) row is. A `measured` row recorded before H's close is re-read after the close by `ownersOf`'s checkpoint arm, from P's cursor to the end of the shared bucket (one spool file per (session, hour), shared by every incarnation), and any other incarnation's line there is refused (`incarnation-mismatch`), which makes the row unmeasured; an unclosed row past the horizon becomes `hour-never-closed` at upkeep; an unmeasured row already counts. So a restart that drops the owed memory leaves the hour to its P row, and the deferral saved only the stand-in row. A restart also drops a loss gap that was staged and is not yet durable (a J, re-listing, `changing`/`absent` or expiry gap), because "durable before forget" is in-process memory; each falls under the cases here: an expiry is r1's "every listing in H+1 or later", a J in H shows on P's close read (R-F (c)), and a re-listing or a `changing`/`absent` listing in H writes the gap again at once (review 838, answer 2). A pass after the restart that lists the session `unreadable` in H takes P from the checkpoint (the third source), so H is owed again and expires as a loss if never read. **The accepted residue.** Only when every listing after the restart falls in H+1 or later does H rest on its P row alone: it counts measured only if P's bucket was read to its end after H's close with nothing refused, and if the registry is never read again H is not marked by expiry. It is accepted because it is bounded to hours that already hold a P row, P's evidence was read in full, and a respawn inside H shows on P's close read (J's line lands after P's cursor and is refused); a respawn after H leaves H's evidence whole. Two further edges, recorded (review 838): an expired owed hour whose gap bucket meets a STANDING R82 pre-write refusal never finds its row, so it stays in memory past the read horizon and its lost window keeps holding overlapping rung-4 candidates; it is reachable only through an R82 refusal, which nothing known produces, the hold is the safe direction (no candidate leases over an unwritten loss, while forgetting the hour could leave H resting on P's row with no loss written, as r1 does), and bounding it is wave 4's (F4); and on an expiry whose pass's census also measures J != P, the J branch takes the hour out of `owed`, so if that pass's flush fails the next pass's expiry treats it as non-owed and forgets it at staging, and a second consecutive flush failure would lose its gap, base behaviour on a two-failure edge (F11). An hour whose `changing`/`absent` gap is already durable has left `hours`, so a later `unreadable` listing in the same hour can defer it; the sticky stand-in row already makes H unmeasured, so there is no gate effect, and the text's "never deferred" holds until that gap is durable (F10). There is also a FAIL-CLOSED residue: if H's (session, P, H) row is closed by the checkpoint arm before any live census measures P (an outage that spans H's close plus the 120 s margin), no later observation returns H (`hoursOf`'s `closed` filter), so H expires as a loss; accepted. **Verdict finality.** An owed hour counts measured in `delegationHealthWindowOf`, through its P row, until it leaves the memory, and its loss gap can land up to 24 h after H; so a gate verdict over a window is final only once its last hour has left the read horizon, as for R89's listed hours. **Unchanged:** how the window counts (R60, R90), the census (`gitref.ts`), the ingest grammar, the checkpoint (v1), and every key, journal kind, port, store method, migration, hold reason and health field; the existing 'generation disagreement' row stays as it was and green (no prior incarnation: the immediate gap). **Rows** (`server/test/delegation-watch.test.ts`, describe 'D-6471 (wave-3 follow-up 9) …'; base = `origin/main` 7ebdff20c, which carries the text's base c34f82830 with neither file changed since): R-A, the 17:00Z shape, two sessions, plus its across-the-hour variant (10:59:30 / 11:00:30), red at base; R-A (correlation), a rung-4 candidate held while the hour is owed and leased once discharged, with a control world, red at base; R-B, never read again, loss word at expiry, green at base; R-B second case, an expiry gap whose flush fails stays owed and lands after the recovery, red at base; R-C (i, ii), J after one or two unreadable passes, loss in the J pass, red at base; R-C (iii), a J row injected in H so the third source ties, loss in that pass, red at base; R-C (iv), the session listed dead between the unreadable pass and J, red at base (added: in (i) and (ii) R102 re-pends the `(id, null)` owner in the J pass and adds H back, which masks a J-discharges mutant); R-D (i), a pass cut on the session's own listing, red at base; R-D (ii), a failed flush, red at base; R-E (i), `absent` and `changing`, green at base; R-E (ii), `changing` (cut) then `unreadable` in H with P held and its row present, green at base; R-E (iii), `unreadable` then `changing`, the gap in the `changing` pass, red at base (its "not before" assertion; the base wrote the gap in the unreadable pass); R-F (a), red at base; R-F (r1), the accepted residue, red at base; R-F (r2), the boot, red at base; R-F (b), the R102 re-read's own row does not count, green at base; R-F (c), a respawn hidden by a restart, red at base; R-G, a `directory-absent` read of P never discharges, green at base; R-H, no prior incarnation, green at base; R-I, R107, green at base (its later passes are outside H: a later `unreadable` pass in H re-defers H from the checkpoint and would mask the R107 mutant). Added in fix round 1 (review 838), with their colour at 209d424e1 (the first wave-done's tip): R-B (F1), an expired owed hour whose gap is durable leaves `owed` too, the owner kept alive by a second owed hour, red at 209d424e1 (no behaviour reads an expired hour's `owed` entry, whose one reader past the horizon is the J branch's `listedBy` stamp, so the row reads the lane's private pending map by a cast, as the file reads `pendingWindows`; no seam was added); R-B (F3), H owed, H+1 listed `changing`, H expires saying `unreadable`, red at 209d424e1 (green at the base, which wrote `unreadable` in the deferral pass); R-G (capability absent; F5), green; R-D (iii; F6), P's bucket refused before writing (the R82 rows' anchor fault), green; R-E (iv), deferral per hour while the owner was last listed `changing` in another hour, green (and red at the base); and R-B (second case) and R-D (ii) now assert at the first completed pass after the in-process recovery (the first pass after which the applier is ready and not failed; F7), green. Added in fix round 2 (review 862 F2), each green at the tip and red under XOWN1: PROBE-1, H owed, H+1 listed `changing`, and the expiry pass's flush fails at the journal write once (R-B second case's fault, a live demo-b line making the pass journal): the gap that lands in the first completed pass after the recovery says `unreadable`; PROBE-2, the same through two consecutive failed flushes (the second on the first completed pass after the first recovery; recovery itself makes no journal write): once a flush succeeds, H's loss gap is written. Under XOWN1 the retry stages H as a non-owed hour, so PROBE-1's row says `changing` and PROBE-2's second staging forgets H with no loss written, the unsafe direction.
