Found in the wave-3 follow-up 10 (run 968, block D-6967..D-6970):

- **D-6967** — `a-transient-bucket-read-failure-waits` (wave-3 follow-up 10): **Why.** The seven-day soak (T0 2026-10-24T09:00Z, on v0.0.608) had used 46 of the about 68 unmeasured session-hours its gate allows by 2026-10-26 06:32Z, and all 46 came from three short read failures on 2026-10-25 (a link drop or a registry outage of 0.3 to 37 s, at fleet load 77 to 120): 40 + 2 + 2 `directory-unreadable`, 1 `range-unreadable` and 1 `incarnation-unmeasured`. Each row was recorded mid-hour by the pass whose read failed, and the passes after it read the same sessions whole (a failed read does not move the cursor, so nothing was lost), but `mergeDelegationObservationInterval` keeps `unmeasured` over a later `measured` row, so the hour stayed unmeasured. D-6471 (follow-up 9) fixed this shape for the census's incarnation read; the bucket read is the second site. The operator ruled: "Soak continues on today's code. A worker builds follow-up 10 now (a failed folder read waits for a later read in the same hour), with full reviews, but it isn't merged until the soak ends on 10-31. If the count reaches 69 (a certain fail), it merges at once and the 7 days restart on its release. If the soak passes, it merges afterwards with no restart." So the pull request is HELD. **The behaviour** (`server/src/watch.ts`, the delegation lane; lane memory only; a doctrine paragraph beside D-6471's). Only `directory-unreadable` and `range-unreadable` may wait; `capability-unmeasured` (it holds for every owner until the next reconnect), the carve-outs `directory-absent` and `capability-absent`, `bucket-vanished-unread` (a measured loss) and `input-invalid` (refused by name) keep today's behaviour exactly. A transient failure of (session, I, H) waits only when the durable state, read in `observeHour` after the read (`durable.state()`, never a row this pass staged), holds a `measured` (session, I, H) row recorded before `laneCloseAt(H)` (`laneCarries`; an unparseable `recordedAt` is no carrier, the immediate gap); otherwise, and for the first read of an hour, the gap is immediate, as today. Waiting stages no interval and no cursor, answers that the hour was not read, and, only after `observeHour`'s abort check (so a cut pass never records or rewords a wait), records the wait on the (session, I) pending entry: H joins its `hours`, its word (the detail `unreadableDelegationBucketResult` would have recorded) is set in `waits` beside D-6471's `owed`, and `listedBy` becomes `Math.max(listedBy, pass.seq)` (R107); the entry is created with `reason: ''` if missing. A wait exists only while H is in both `hours` and `waits`: every path that removes H from `hours` removes its word in the same statement (`forgetRead`, `expirePendingOwners`, and D-6471's discharge, whose `(id, null)` owner never waits), and so does deleting the entry. A later transient failure keeps the hour waiting under the later word. While it waits, H is a correlation lost window through `pendingWindows`. **Only its own hours** (review 976 F1): R106's arm of `pendingWindows` (every measured, unclosed hour of a real-incarnation pending entry) applies only to an entry holding at least one hour that is NOT waiting; an entry whose hours all wait adds just those hours. Such an entry exists only after a completed, durable pass whose `forgetRead` removed every other hour, so every other measured, unclosed hour of that (session, I) was read whole in that pass, which is exactly when, with no wait, the lane would have forgotten the entry and listed no window: the narrowing restores the base behaviour for every hour but the waiting ones. A cut pass, a failed flush, an R82 refusal or an R107 skip leaves a hour not waiting in `hours`, so R106 applies there as before. Without it, one session's persistently unreadable bucket made that session's open hour, read whole, a lost window on every repository for as long as the wait lasted (the safe direction, a hold, but unstated). **How a wait ends** (`observeHour` answers `'read'`, `'ended'` or `false`; `observeOwner` pushes the hour onto `owner.read` or onto a new `owner.ended`, never both). (1) An applicable result that is not a carve-out ends it as a read: a measured `ok` read, the no-op skip included, any ingest loss (a line refusal, `bucket-vanished-unread`, `window-starved`, a replaced bucket, …) and the reader's own `bucket-vanished-unread`, each landing its own record with no waited word staged before it; the wait leaves once that observation completed and R101's gate holds as `forgetRead` applies it (the flush did not fail past its first byte, no bucket of the owner was refused, R82, and no newer pass listed it, R107). (2) A carve-out result never ends a wait as a read: in that pass the waited word is staged with `noteGap(pass, durable, id, I, H, waits[H])`, an interval-only gap (`cursor: null`), so the cursor row stays the carve-out result's and R90's merge keeps the loss over the carve-out in either staging order; H goes on `owner.ended`, never on `owner.read` (D-6471's discharge input, which never carries a carve-out), and leaves `hours` and `waits` under the same gate; a `true` from that `noteGap` (the loss already on file) counts as durable too, and otherwise the next carve-out pass stages it again. (3) A result refused by name applies nothing; the hour keeps waiting. (4) Expiry: a waiting hour that leaves the read horizon is ended by R89's expiry, unchanged: `hour-never-closed: listed in this hour, and never read before the hour left the read horizon`, and the hour and its word leave as the gap is staged. (5) Read whole after its close: a listed hour `hoursOf` no longer returns (only a cut pass's late flush can close it while it waits, R85: pass N stages the close and is cut, pass N+1's read fails and waits, N's flush lands) goes on `owner.ended`, so the next completed pass whose flush is durable ends the wait with no gap; this is the one exit that is not a read by this pass. A cut pass, an observation that returned on `pass.signal.aborted`, a failed flush and an R82 refusal never end a wait. **Restart.** The wait is never what keeps the hour honest; the carrier row is. `hoursOf` returns that row's hour every pass while it is unclosed and inside the horizon, for as long as the census lists (session, I) live; before H's close an owner the census does not list under I (a respawn, an ended session) writes nothing more under I, and an `unreadable` census of a live I is covered by D-6471's `(id, null)` hour, a lost window over the same hour on every repository; after H's close the checkpoint arm reads (session, I) as non-live; and upkeep turns the row into `hour-never-closed: recorded before the hour ended …` once now reaches H + 25 h + `DELEGATION_HOUR_CLOSE_MARGIN_MS`, in the first pass not cut before upkeep. So a restart loses only lane memory: a read after it closes the hour measured (R-F (a)), no read leaves upkeep's words (R-F (b1)), and a failing pass after it re-creates the wait, which expires with R89's (R-F (b2)). The two edges of expiry are R89's own: a failed flush of the expiry gap, which the next recovery's not-ready window (R88) covers, and an R82 pre-write refusal of that gap's own bucket, which nothing covers for the upkeep margin plus one pass; nothing known produces one. **The accepted residues.** The first read of an hour that fails has no carrier, so it records its gap at once, as today: a link drop in an hour's first minute or two still costs that hour (R-C). A restart during a wait, then a carve-out: the memory is lost and the carrier holds the hour, but if the next result after the restart is `capability-absent` the hour's word is that carve-out, not the transient loss (the gate counts a carve-out unmeasured anyway); only `capability-absent` is certain to mask the loss, since `directory-absent` vetoes on its own when the owner keeps a cursor row or another measured hour (`hadSpool`, computed from the checkpoint after the carve-out replaced the carrier), but not when the carrier was its only measured row and it had no cursor row (an empty hour); the unread stretch before the restart lies before the restart's first ready instant, so R88's not-ready window holds every rung-4 candidate begun at or before readyAt plus the skew (R-F (c)). A persistent failure (a bucket that stays unreadable) shows as a loss only at the horizon, up to about 25 h after H, and until then the hour counts measured, so a gate verdict over a window is final only once its last hour has left the read horizon, as D-6471 already says (R-B). **D-6471's inputs change, and two of its statements change as no-ops for an owed hour** (review 976 F9): its discharge in `forgetRead` also deletes `waits` for the hour (an `(id, null)` entry never has one), and `forgetRead`'s one-line "owed stays" loop becomes the per-hour loop that also keeps a waiting hour (an owed hour is kept exactly as before). Its inputs change in two ways, both the safe direction: `priorOf` reads `pendingOwners`, which a wait now extends, so a waiting old incarnation can become P or make P ambiguous (an ambiguous P gives the immediate gap; a stale P is ended by a census measuring J != P, with the gap); and a waiting read answers not-read, so it no longer discharges an hour D-6471 owes for P on that failed read, which stays owed until the wait ends by a read (R-L; a carve-out ending the wait never discharges it, M12b). **Unchanged:** the merge (R90), how the window counts (R60, `delegationHealthWindowOf`), upkeep, the census, the ingest grammar, `gap()`, `unreadableDelegationBucketResult`, `readDelegationBucket`, `ingestDelegationBucket`, D-6471's behaviour, and every checkpoint key, journal kind, port, store method, migration and health field. **Rows** (`server/test/delegation-watch.test.ts`, describe 'D-6967 (wave-3 follow-up 10) …', each title prefixed `D-6967`; base = `origin/main` 98fd13dad): R-A, the 19:00Z shape, two sessions, for a throwing listing, an `unreadable` listing and a range fault on a bucket with a line, red at base; R-A (correlation), a rung-4 candidate held while the hour waits and leased once it ends, with a control world, red; R-A2, the later transient word lands, red (its wait assertions; the base also lands `directory-unreadable`, by the merge's later row); R-B, never read again, R89's words at expiry and the lost window passing to the durable row, red; R-C, no carrier (a first-ever pass, and H+1's first read after a measured H), green; R-D, a carve-out after a wait, red (the waited loss lands in the carve-out pass; the base lands it at once); R-E, a torn tail, a wait, the bucket deleted: ingestion's `bucket-vanished-unread` lands with no waited word staged, red; R-F (a, b1, b2), restarts, red; R-F (c), the residue, red (its control: the base's loss vetoes it); R-G, a wait survives a cut (i) and a failed flush (ii), each shown by its word at a carve-out and ended by the first completed durable read, red; R-H (a), the close read, red; R-H (b), no carrier after the close under `readEveryListedBucket`, green; R-I, a session that ended, red; R-J, a carve-out pass whose flush fails through the `replaceCheckpoint` spy keeps the hour waiting, red; R-K, an R82 refusal never ends a wait, and the applying pass does, red; R-L, D-6471 with a wait, red; R-M, the reader's `bucket-vanished-unread` never waits, green; R-N, `capability-unmeasured` never waits, green; R-O, no stale word after a read, red. Added beyond the text, each red at base: R-O (second case), hour 11's wait ended by a read while hour 10 still waits keeps the entry, and hour 11 listed again at a carve-out lands its own word (the text's R-O cannot see M14: a read that ends the owner's only waiting hour deletes the whole entry, word included); R-P, the late-flush close read (exit (5) above), which no text row reached (pass N closes hour 10 and is cut, N+1's read fails and waits, N's flush lands, the next pass ends the wait, and the hour is measured at expiry); R-Q, R107 for a wait (a cut pass's late forgetting of a non-live owner's read never ends the wait the next pass recorded). Added in fix round 1 (review 976 F1): R-R, red at the first wave-done's tip e5bf2df51 (the reviewer's PROBE-976): a standing range fault on hour 10's bucket alone, hour 10 measured and then waiting, two passes in hour 11 (an empty hour, read): `pendingWindows` lists (demo-a, INC, 10:00) and not 11:00; its CONTROL, with no fault, lists neither; and a pass cut on demo-a's listing in hour 12 leaves hour 12 in the entry, not waiting, so R106 is kept and lists hour 11 (measured, not closed). The describe holds 23 `it` blocks (the text's 19, the three added rows and R-R), 19 red at base and 4 green (R-C, R-H (b), R-M, R-N), all green at the tip; the whole file is 303/303 (280 + 23). **The two existing rows this changes** (base expectation, why it changes, edit; each keeps its property): 'a measured hour that turns out to have a gap IS applied: unmeasured is never skipped' expected a range fault on a measured hour with no new bytes to be applied as `range-unreadable`; that hour now has a carrier, so the fault waits and nothing is applied; the fault became `capability-absent` (`w.fleet.ccdVerbs = ['caps']`), which does not wait, so the row's assertion (the result is applied, and the hour is unmeasured) holds. Review 976 F7: the row cannot red `intervalMoves`' measured-to-unmeasured clause, and could not at the base either: a gap's cursor carries the new `intervalState` and `detail`, so `cursorSame` is false and the no-op skip is avoided by the cursor alone (a mutant dropping that clause stays green on the row at base and tip). A row that pins that clause is carried to wave 4. 'R90 in the lane: an hour first recorded range-unreadable, then capability-absent …' asserted `range-unreadable` after the range-fault pass; that pass now waits, so the intermediate assertion reads `measured`, and the final assertions (the carve-out result applied to the cursor, the loss kept on the interval, the link vetoed) hold through the carve-out exit ((2) above). Measured: the base test file against the tip's `watch.ts` reds exactly these two rows (278/280), and no other transient-fault row hits an owner with a carrier. **Mutants** (each an in-place source edit of the tip's `watch.ts`, restored from a saved copy and checked by sha-256, run one at a time against the D-6967 rows, the 'a measured hour that turns out …' row and the three 'R90 in the lane' rows (M23b also against the R106 rows); every one RED except the declared equivalents): M1 wait with no carrier: R-C, R-H (b), and 'R90 in the lane: … directory-absent, then directory-unreadable …'; M2 the reader's `bucket-vanished-unread` waits: R-M; M3 `forgetRead` forgets a waiting hour: R-A, R-A (correlation), R-A2, R-B, R-D, R-E, R-F (a, b1, b2), R-F (c), R-G, R-H (a), R-I, R-J, R-K, R-L, R-O (second case), R-Q, and the R90 range-then-capability row; M4 a carve-out ends the wait without the waited word: R-A2, R-D, R-G, R-J, R-K, R-L, R-O (second case), R-Q, and the R90 range-then-capability row; M5 not used; M6 a cut observation ends a wait (the listing-abort return marks its listed hours ended and the owner completed): R-G, by (i)'s carve-out step; M7 a failed flush ends a wait (the wait is dropped before `forgetRead`'s failed-flush return): R-G, by (ii)'s carve-out step, and R-J, R-K, R-P, R-Q (the edit also skips the R82 and R107 checks); M8 a measured row recorded at or after its close is a carrier: R-H (b), under the seam only: without it `hoursOf` never returns a hour whose row was recorded after its close, so M8 differs only when a cut pass's late flush closes the hour DURING a later pass's failing read of it (the row on file before the read was unclosed, the one after it closed), where it waits and then ends with no gap (exit (5) above) instead of today's immediate gap; no row builds that interleaving; M9 `directory-unreadable` does not wait: R-A (throw and `unreadable` variants), R-A (correlation), R-A2, R-B, R-E, R-F (a, b1, b2), R-F (c), R-H (a), R-I, R-L, R-P, R-Q; M10 `range-unreadable` does not wait: R-A (range variant), R-A2, R-D, R-G, R-J, R-K, R-O, R-O (second case), and the R90 range-then-capability row; M11 `capability-unmeasured` waits: R-N; M12 a carve-out's waited hour leaves the memory as its gap is staged: R-J; M12b the carve-out pass records H in `owner.read`: R-L; M13 an R82-refused read ends a wait: R-K, by its carve-out step (for M7 and M13 the row's lane-memory `waitOf` assertion reds first; each carve-out step was measured red alone, with that assertion removed for the measurement and the file restored from a saved copy checked by sha-256); M14 the word is not tied to the hour (`forgetRead` drops the hour and keeps its word): R-O (second case) (it survives the text's R-O, above); M15 (added) a later transient failure keeps the first word: R-A2; M16 (added) a wait does not add its hour to `hours`: R-O (second case), R-P; M17 (added) a wait does not raise `listedBy`: R-Q; M19 (added) an hour read whole after its close never ends its wait: R-P; M22 (fix round 1) R106 for every entry, the narrowing dropped: R-R, its first case; M23 (fix round 1) R106 narrowed for every entry: (a) dropped whenever `waits` is non-empty: R-R, its cut-pass case; (b) dropped always: R-R's cut-pass case and the existing row 'R106 (F6): a pending owner's MEASURED, UNCLOSED hours veto too …'. **Equivalent, declared:** M18, a wait sets `listedBy = pass.seq` (lowering a newer stamp): a pass records a wait only before its abort check fails, and passes are single-flight until a cut, so no newer pass exists then and `pass.seq` is already the highest stamp; M20, the expiry keeps the waited word: an expired hour is past the read horizon, so nothing lists or observes it again; M21, a wait read from `waits` alone: equivalent while every path removes the hour and its word together; M14's row pins `forgetRead`'s path, and M20's argument covers expiry's (M20 itself reds nothing, review 976 F8). **Growth:** `server/src/watch.ts` +27 net code lines against the merge-base (growth-check, limit 40); no waiver. **The census (ARCH-62).** The first CI run reddened `run-session-census.test.ts`: `laneCarries` was a sixth `r.sessionId === id` site in `watch.ts`, whose `NOT_BINDING_SITES` entry allows five. The fix consolidates rather than raising the count: one finder, `laneIntervalOf` (the checkpoint's interval row for (id, incarnation, hour)), serves both `laneCarries` and `noteGap`, whose own `find` it replaces, so the file holds five sites again with no behaviour change (the D-6967 rows and the whole file re-run green, and M8 re-run on the new `laneCarries`). This is the +3 over the first measurement. **Kept as stated:** the text's rows and mutant table, with these differences: three rows added (R-O second case, R-P, R-Q), and R-R in fix round 1; seven mutants added, four measured (M15, M16, M17, M19) and three declared equivalent (M18, M20, M21), and M22 and M23 in fix round 1; R-A2's base colour is red only through its wait assertions; and the wait's word on the pending entry is the detail string `unreadableDelegationBucketResult` builds, so the waited gap reads exactly as the immediate gap would have.
